### The three things S7 was asked to settle before any code

#### 1 — the round trip is not a fixed point on every string, so the property is **convergence**, and 4 is a measurement

§11.3's row is *"Property test: `parse ∘ serialize ∘ parse` is a fixed point"*
and S4 met exactly that over 22 documents and 1302 of 1324 spec examples. A
generated test cannot state it that way, and the reason is not that generators
find harder inputs — it is that the claim is **false as written** and the fixed
corpus does not contain the counter-examples.

**Measured over `round_trip.rs`'s own 1346 at their own option sets:**

| Claim | Result |
|---|---|
| `m1 == m2` — one round trip is enough | **1334 of 1346**. The other twelve are `SECOND_PASS_MOVERS`, a two-directional ratchet, and all twelve are a subset of `FIXED_POINT_EXCEPTIONS`' 22 |
| `m2 == m3` — it settles by the second application | **1346 of 1346**, no exception list at all |

So over the fixed inputs, *settles by 2* is unconditionally true and *is a fixed
point* is not. **Over generated input, 2 is also false**, and the distribution
is the argument for the constant rather than a story about it — 466,909
generated documents:

| Applications to settle | Documents |
|---:|---:|
| 1 | 458,272 |
| 2 | 8,317 |
| 3 | 316 |
| 4 | **4** |
| >4 | 0 |

`SETTLES_BY = 4` is therefore the measured tail and not a safety margin, and the
four documents at the tail are what makes the difference between 2 and 4 a fact
rather than a preference. Three families are excluded **by name**, each pinned by
a ratchet test that asserts the excluded behaviour rather than merely skipping
it: the known upstream panic shape, the backtick-info-string fence whose settling
time is *unbounded* (finding 4 below), and the period-2 oscillator (finding 3).
Each exclusion is asked of **every element of the sequence** and not only of the
source, because a soak run proved the serializer can emit a guarded shape from a
source that is not one.

#### 2 — the two known non-bugs a fuzzer reaches immediately, and only one of them was upstream's

A fuzz target that starts by rediscovering a recorded defect reports nothing on
every run afterwards, so the shapes §10 already owns have to be named before the
target is written. Two were expected. **One of them turned out not to be
upstream's at all**, which is the reason this decision was worth taking before
the code rather than after.

1. **`pulldown-cmark`'s panic (§10, "Owed by S6") — guarded.** It is a
   dependency's defect, reached before any of this crate's code runs, there is
   no upgrade to take and `panic = "abort"` forecloses catching it. A guard is
   the only available answer, and the guard is held honest by
   `the_only_panics_the_generators_reach_are_the_ones_the_guards_name`. S7 also
   found the recorded shape is **too narrow** — see the correction in §10.
2. **`"- a\n\u{2028}"` — repaired, because it is this port's own code.**
   `block.rs`'s `apply_prefix` dedent arm, two lines, and §11.3's clause is the
   one S7 is itself measured on. Guarding it would have been the stage declining
   its own gate.

The rule that separates them is worth stating because it is the one S7 applies
three more times below: **a shape is guarded when the repair belongs to someone
else or needs a harness this stage does not have, and repaired when it is this
crate's and the evidence is already in hand.**

#### 3 — what is universally true of `parse`'s output, and why range **nesting** is not on the list

Five claims are made about every generated document, at both option sets:
totality of `parse` and of its consumers; every source range in bounds and on a
`char` boundary; every live node has a range; the incremental entry point agrees
with a full parse before any edit; and no parse is deeper than the limit.

**Nesting is deliberately absent, and asserting it would have been a bug in the
test rather than a finding about the engine.** §10's second "Owed by S6" item
records that a child's range can escape its parent's on `-\t- [a]: /x[xter\n`,
because a tab inside a list item's marker padding is the one case where a
stripped line's text is not a slice of the source.
`crates/mt-md/tests/source_ranges.rs` asserts nesting **over the corpus**, which
contains no such document — so that invariant is a fact about the corpus, and
`block::tests::the_ranges_are_not_injective_and_do_not_always_nest` is the input
that says so. `fuzz_targets/parse.rs` carries the argument in its module docs,
next to the observation that **`fuzz_targets/tokenize.rs` does assert nesting**,
for `mt-inline` tokens, where it is true. Two targets in the same directory
making opposite assertions is the correct state, and the only thing that makes it
readable is writing down why.

### The stage found two crashes in this port's own code, and that is the justification for the stage

Six stages, 1344 block comparisons, 22 differential documents, 1324 spec
fixtures and 3.2 million generated edit sequences had all been green. Neither of
these was reachable from any of them.

#### The mid-`char` cut in `apply_prefix` — and the answer is chosen by 26,035 of 47,264 rather than argued

`mt_md::parse` panics on `"- a\n\u{2028}"`. The dedent arm read
`let cut = indent.min(text.len()); text[cut..]` — clamped for length but not for
`char` boundary — while its **three sibling arms already use `str::get`**.
`str::trim` counts U+2028 as whitespace, so the dedent branch is taken and the
cut lands mid-character.

**The answer taken is that the cut falls back to the line's end, i.e. an empty
slice**, spelled
`let cut = if text.is_char_boundary(indent) { indent } else { text.len() }` —
one value, because it is used as a slice *and* as a source offset and the two
must agree.

It was chosen by measurement rather than by taste. `marked` 18.0.5
`Tokenizer.ts:348-349` slices with `String.prototype.slice`, which counts
**UTF-16 code units**: a wide-space line is *shorter* than `indent`, and slicing
past the end is `''` — never a throw and never the whole string. Enumerated over
every whitespace-only line of 1–3 characters at `indent` 1..=6, of the **47,264**
rows whose byte cut lands mid-character:

| Candidate | Reproduces `slice(indent)` |
|---|---:|
| empty slice (**taken**) | **26,035 of 47,264** |
| snap the cut up to the next boundary | 20,584 |
| snap down to the previous boundary | 0 |
| return the line verbatim | 0 |

**The residual 21,229 is not this cut's, and that is a finding of its own.**
Driven against muya on 20 probes, the wide line is put **outside** the list
entirely — `- a\n\u{2028}` gives item `"a"` plus a sibling paragraph
`"\u{2028}"` — because `marked`'s `list` rule is an `m`-flagged regex and
JavaScript's regex engine treats U+2028 as a line terminator where `split('\n')`
does not. No choice of cut closes that; it is a structural divergence inside
`pulldown-cmark`. Recorded in the new test, not repaired.

**Three corrections it made to the brief it was given, and two of them are
corrections to this repository's own prose:**

1. The function is named **`apply_prefix`**. `strip_prefix_from_line` appears
   nowhere in the repository — only in prose that cites it.
2. The sibling arms' recorded answer is `.unwrap_or("")` — **empty**, not
   "return the line unchanged". Unchanged scores **0 of 47,264** against muya.
3. **A stale measurement inside the new test file.**
   `the_only_panics_…` claimed the guards skip *"114 of 1,600 — 82 upstream and
   32 stripper"*; re-running both predicates at the committed generator gives
   **119 — 89 upstream and 30 stripper**. It predated the widening of
   `is_the_known_upstream_panic`. Restated as measured, and with the stripper
   shape repaired and back in the checked population the bar tightened from
   one-in-six to **one-in-twelve**, since one guard leaving would otherwise have
   let the survivor triple without firing.

`block.rs` +25/−1, `block/tests.rs` +71
(`a_wide_whitespace_continuation_line_dedents_to_nothing`, carrying the measured
table), `lib.rs` +22/−2 — `parse`'s doc comment now says totality is false for
the `pulldown-cmark` class **alone**, and names it — and
`round_trip_properties.rs` −110 net, because the stripper guard, its helper and
its coverage test were deleted and the `#[should_panic]` became a 144-parse class
test.

#### The unbounded recursion — found at 200 bytes under a sanitizer, reproduced at 4,001 bytes without one

S7's own `round_trip` fuzz target found it on `"> ".repeat(100)` — a **200-byte**
input, under ASan. That is the fuzzer's framing rather than the defect's, so it
was re-taken on a plain `--release` build with no sanitizer and no fuzzer:
`"> " × 2000`, a **4,001-byte file**, aborts with `thread 'main' has overflowed
its stack`. Depth 1000 was fine. Nothing in `docs/` had recorded it, and
`mt-inline`'s `tokenize` does not do it — nested emphasis to depth 1000 is fine —
so it is `mt-md`'s.

**The fix is `MAX_NESTING_DEPTH = 128`, and at the limit the parse stops opening
containers so that the rest of that container's source becomes one paragraph's
text.** `"> " × 2000` is 127 block quotes whose paragraph reads
`"> " × 1873 + "x"` — and **serializing it reproduces the input byte for byte**,
which is what makes the clamp lossless rather than truncating.

**muya has no limit to copy: it dies too, and where it dies is an artifact.**
Measured against `@muyajs/core` at `MARKTEXT_REF` on Node 24.14, one process per
depth, by binary search on `"> " × n + "x"`:

| muya entry point | deepest `n` that returns | first `n` that throws |
|---|---:|---:|
| `MarkdownToState.generate` | 1953 | 1992 |
| `+ ExportMarkdown.generate` | 1757 | 1796 |
| `+ JSON.stringify` | 1953 | 1992 |
| `renderToStaticHTML({sanitize: false})` | 1953 | 1992 |
| `renderToStaticHTML({sanitize: true})` | 1875 | 1914 |

Every one is `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded`, and the number is
**V8's stack rather than a decision**: the same measurement at `--stack-size=500`
gives 938 and at `--stack-size=4000` gives 9550, a straight line through the
interpreter's stack size. There is no muya behaviour to reproduce, so **the limit
is this port's stated choice** — and what the port keeps is the one thing muya's
answer implies: a `RangeError` returns nothing at all, so any output is closer to
muya's intent than a crash.

**Every walk in the crate was audited, not only the crashing one**, which is what
makes this a fix rather than a patch:

| Walk | Answer |
|---|---|
| `parse` ([`block.rs`](../crates/mt-md/src/block.rs)) | clamps at build time; `Builder::would_exceed_depth` argues the choice against the two alternatives |
| `render_to_static_html` ([`html.rs`](../crates/mt-md/src/html.rs)) | clamps. **The deepest-framed of the crate's three walks** — 995 levels on a 1 MiB thread in release, 369 in `dev`, against `parse`'s 1,229 — so it is the walk that *fixes* the limit |
| `dump_state` ([`state.rs`](../crates/mt-md/src/state.rs)) | clamps, **and bounds two recursions rather than one**: its own, and `serde_json`'s over the nested `Value` it returns — `to_string_pretty` and `Value`'s drop glue each descend once per level |
| `serialize` ([`serialize.rs`](../crates/mt-md/src/serialize.rs)) | a `depth` counter on the `ExportMarkdown::convert` frames |
| [`labels.rs`](../crates/mt-md/src/labels.rs) | rewritten **iterative**, with an explicit worklist that reproduces `travel`'s visit order exactly |
| [`reparse.rs`](../crates/mt-md/src/reparse.rs) | `graft`, `subtree_eq`, `record_ranges` and `shift_subtree` documented as bounded by the clamp rather than by guards of their own |

**And the reason the audit is not optional**: `state.rs`, `labels.rs`,
`serialize` and `html.rs` each take a `Document` rather than a parse, so **none
may assume its caller went through `parse`**. `mt_doc::Edit::InsertNode` reaches
any depth without going near this crate, and `tests/edit_inverse.rs` does exactly
that. §11.3 applies to each on its own account.

Diff footprint: `block.rs` +276, `block/tests.rs` +225, `lib.rs` +216,
`serialize.rs` +40, `state.rs` +41, `html.rs` +27, `labels.rs` +24, `reparse.rs`
+7. And the reproducer after the fix, which is a crash traded for neither a hang
nor a truncation:

| depth | `--dump-state` | `--to-html` |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 2,000 | 50 ms | 53 ms |
| 8,000 | 72 ms | 88 ms |
| 50,000 (100 KB) | 301 ms | 394 ms |

### What S7 did not repair, and why — ~~three~~ **five** serializer findings, and a runner four stages have now declined to build

Five findings; two were repaired above and three are pinned with reproducers and
owed forward (§10, "Owed by S7"). The rule is one this document has applied three
times already — *the stage that needs a harness is the stage that should build
it* — and the harness all three need is the same one: **a serializer differential
against `ExportMarkdown`**, which §10 has now declined four times (S1 for
`parse_blocks`, S4 for `ExportMarkdown`, S5 for `renderToStaticHTML`, and here).

1. **The round trip oscillates with period 2 on `"> - [a]: /x\n>   * "`** —
   tight ↔ loose forever, while **muya settles on the first pass**, driven
   directly from the clone `cargo xtask diff` uses. It is a port defect and the
   sharpest form of the harm this gate exists to catch: a user saving that
   document twice gets two different files and saving it a third time gets the
   first back. Pinned by
   `the_round_trip_oscillates_between_a_tight_and_a_loose_rendering`.
2. **A fence with a backtick in its info string has no constant settling time** —
   n fences → n+2 applications, exactly. muya matches byte-for-byte for three
   passes and then **keeps growing where the port stops**. Pinned by
   `the_settling_time_grows_with_the_number_of_backtick_info_strings`.
3. **Serialized output diverges from muya on two generated documents** —
   `"1. a\n--\n0\n\t- a"` and `">a\n \t- a\nd"`.

**Why not fix them here.** Finding 1 turns on looseness, which is decided by
`_insertLineBreak` and by what `loose` is read back from — precisely the
mechanism §10's "Owed by S4" nine turned on, and the thing settled in this
repository by `cargo xtask blocks --require-ts` against the running engine over
1344 inputs. Changing looseness on a guess is how 1344/1344 stops being
1344/1344, and a property-test stage does not run that argument. Findings 2 and 3
are *differences from muya's serializer*, and confirming a repair to a difference
from muya means comparing against muya — which is the runner nobody has built.

**And finding 3 is the first evidence §8's serializer row has ever had.** That
row has read *"a serializer divergence is chosen deliberately and cannot be
registered … found at S4 and **not** mitigated"* since S4, held up by a
measurement that came out 1346 of 1346 identical. Two generated documents where
the two engines disagree is the case rule 3 needs and has no harness to fire on.
Recording it and not repairing it is the honest state; repairing it silently
would have been the failure the row predicts.

**Corrected at round 2 — it is five findings, not three, and the same runner
blocks the two new ones.** The soak's `round_trip` shards produced two further
*serializer* defect families, neither of them one of the three above and neither
of them slow convergence: they are §10's "Owed by S7" items 4 and 5. Nothing
about the rule changed; what changed is that the runner four stages have declined
to build now blocks five findings instead of three.

### The soak went red on four jobs, and every per-commit check that ran before it was green

Soak run [31089007249](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31089007249)
**failed**. All six `mt-inline` shards passed and both `parse` shards passed;
`fuzz round_trip` (both shards), `fuzz reparse` (both shards) and `proptest soak`
on **linux and windows** failed. CI run
[31088979433](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31088979433)
was `success` on all three platforms beforehand.

**That pairing is the finding, not the failure.** Every check that runs on every
commit — `cargo test --workspace`, five `xtask` runners, `fmt`, `clippy`, three
platforms — passed on the same tree that the long run tore apart four jobs of.
The soak is the only thing in this repository whose denominator is time rather
than a corpus, and §6's closing section has said since S4 that the thing a
dispatch buys is **length**. This is the first dispatch that spent it.

Triage (610 lines, `s7-triage.md`) reduced the four failing jobs to **three**
defects, and its own headline is that they are not three independent bugs:

| Defect | What failed | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | `parse` slices mid-`char` at `block.rs:3602` | Five panic sites, **one** root cause — a lone `\r` |
| **B** | `SETTLES_BY = 4` is exceeded by both `round_trip` shards | Two new **serializer** defect families; both inputs settle, at **8** and **6** |
| **C** | `Incremental` disagrees with a full parse | Two mechanisms; **one of them is A's root cause**, the other a missing guard |

The fuzz target was **exonerated structurally** rather than by argument: it
compares against `mt_md::parse(incremental.source())`, so only one string exists
at the point of comparison and no divergence between the target's copy and the
engine's can produce a disagreement. Reproduced a second way, in a harness that
holds no second copy of the source at all.

### One root cause under five panic sites, and the fix is an asymmetry rather than a normalisation

`line_start_of` and `line_end_of` delimited a line with `'\n'` **and nothing
else**, while `pulldown-cmark` — which produces every range those functions are
asked about — also ends a line at a bare `\r`. An offset taken from one grid was
then measured against a string cut by the other, and a computed index landed
mid-character. Eight bytes are enough:

```rust
let _ = mt_md::parse("0é\r1. x", mt_md::Options::SPEC);   // block.rs:3602
```

Sites: [`block.rs`](../crates/mt-md/src/block.rs) 3602, 2927, 1795, 1800 and
[`footnotes.rs:157`](../crates/mt-md/src/footnotes.rs), plus 12 more the triage
could not prove safe — **one root cause, five panic sites**, which is why they
went in one commit. It is also why round 1's answer does not generalise: **the
empty slice is not right here.** That answer was measured for `marked`'s
`nextLineWithoutTabs.slice(indent)`, a continuation-line path where an overshoot
is legitimate. Site 3602 has **no `slice` in `marked` at all** — the item's
first-line content is a *regex capture*, and group 1 always ends immediately
after an ASCII `*`, `+`, `-`, `.` or `)`, so it cannot fall inside a character.
A marker length that is not on a boundary means it was measured against the wrong
string, and the answer is to stop measuring it against the wrong string.

**The fix is the asymmetry.** A lone `\r` is treated as a terminator; the `\r` of
a `\r\n` is **content**. Line *starts* then agree with `pulldown-cmark` exactly —
which is all these offsets are ever used for — while CRLF text is untouched.
`line_start_of`, `line_end_of`, `preceded_by_blank_line` and `strip_lines` all
changed, over the new `is_a_line_terminator` / `next_line_start` /
`previous_line_end` helpers;
`block::tests::a_lone_carriage_return_ends_a_line_and_a_crlfs_does_not` is the
claim, and `a_crlf_document_parses_exactly_as_it_did_before_the_grid_changed`
pins the half that must **not** move — including `lib.rs`'s recorded muya
measurement, `"a\r\nb\r\n"` → one paragraph `"a\r\nb\r"`. The half that no test
can state was measured instead: every CRLF document's parse is **byte-identical
across the change, over 34 documents × 2 option sets**.

Three assumptions became checks in the same commit, because a grid that is right
is not a boundary guarantee:

1. `item_prefix_at`'s ordered arm added 1 for the `.` or `)` **it never looked
   at**. `marked`'s `/ {0,3}(?:[*+-]|\d{1,9}[.)])/` requires that character, so
   checking it is transcription rather than invention —
   `an_ordered_markers_delimiter_is_checked_rather_than_assumed`.
2. `indentCodeCompensation` counted **bytes** where `marked` counts UTF-16 code
   units, so an ideographic space in an indented fence's body split a character —
   `an_indented_backtick_fence_compensates_a_wide_space_by_characters`.
3. `Built::range` ends are snapped to a boundary before they index anything, so
   a future range-provenance mistake degrades instead of aborting; the same
   check answers `footnotes.rs`'s tag scan
   (`a_tag_name_is_never_read_across_a_character_boundary`).

**The evidence is the triage's own sweep re-run, not a new one.** 1.5 M generated
documents from its 34-token alphabet — the one with `\r` in it — × 2 option sets
× round trips = **3,000,000 runs → 0 panics in this crate**, against a baseline
of **284 panics at 5 sites**. The only panic left is upstream `pulldown-cmark
parse.rs:2199`, **4 hits**, already named, guarded and `#[should_panic]`ed.

### The triage's own proposal would have moved every CRLF document, and declining it is why no floor moved

Triage's layer 1 was *"treat `\r`, `\r\n` and `\n` as line terminators, exactly
as `pulldown-cmark` does"* — which moves a CRLF line's **content end** onto the
`\r`, changing the text of every CRLF document this port has ever been measured
against. It priced the consequence honestly and called the re-measure *"the
expensive part"*.

**The narrower reading makes the price zero.** Only line *starts* are read from
the grid, so only line starts have to agree; a CRLF line's content may keep
ending where it always did. The crashes are fixed with **zero CRLF movement**,
the predicted re-measure never happened, and **no floor moved**: `blocks`
1344/1344 with 4,985 leaves and 0/0/0, `diff` 22/22, conformance 463/652 and
476/672, `normalize` 2648/2648.

Triage's suggested `trim_start_matches(' ')` for `block.rs:2927` was declined for
the same kind of reason and it is the sharper one: JavaScript's `\s` **matches**
U+3000, so `marked` really does slice there. Trimming ASCII spaces only would
have removed the panic by making the port answer a different question. Counting
characters is panic-free **and** faithful, and only one of those two was the goal.

### Seventeen slice sites, and five are deliberately left unproven

The triage's crate-wide audit swept every byte-index `&str` slice under
`crates/mt-md/src/` and produced 17 rows. All 17 are accounted for, and the
distribution is the point:

| Rows | State |
|---|---|
| `block.rs` 3602, 2927, 1795, 1800, `footnotes.rs` 157 | **Fixed**, each with the reproducer that panicked before it |
| `footnotes.rs` 159, 163 | Fixed by the same boundary check — they reuse `:157`'s unchecked offset and were unreachable only because `:157` panicked first in the `&&` chain |
| `block.rs` 1466, 1524, 1585 | Fixed. Same `has_blank_line`-of-a-`Built`-range class as 1795/1800, and closing 2 of 4 of a class is how the class comes back |
| `block.rs` 3780, 3787 | Covered by argument, **not by type** — recorded as such rather than as safe |
| `block.rs` 2359, 2277, 2486, 2938, 988 | **Deliberately not fixed, and they remain unproven** |

**Why the five are left.** Every one of them slices a range *and uses the text* —
`trim_trailing_blanks`, `task_checkbox`, `marker_char`, `raw_info`,
`Builder::walk`'s region. Snapping an end to a boundary there does not preserve
an answer; it silently returns a **different** answer, which is the failure mode
`block.rs:3361` demonstrates one line from a crash. A crash that says where it
is beats a wrong checkbox that does not. They are exactly the rows the triage
left open, none of them fired in the 3,000,000-run sweep, and they are owed
forward in §10 rather than described as covered.

### Round 3 — 8 was falsified too, and the third time the answer was to stop asserting it from bytes

Round 2's soak — [31253525223](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31253525223)
— went red on `fuzz round_trip`, both shards, within minutes, while
`fuzz parse`, `fuzz reparse` and the three `proptest soak` jobs ran on past the
points at which round 1's had failed. **So round 2's repairs held and the
settling constant did not**, which is the cleanest possible separation of the
two.

The falsifying input is **305 bytes**: ` ```o ` followed by 300 backslashes,
needing ⌊log₂ 300⌋ + 1 = 9 applications. Reproduced locally in one execution;
60,000 backslashes, still one document, needs 17.

**The property test had already written this down**, one round before the
fuzzer produced it — the margin on `SETTLES_BY` says *"a fence info string with
256 backslashes is where 8 stops being true. That is a **finding, not a
guard**"*. Round 2 recorded the exact input class that would break it and
shipped the constant anyway, because 255 looked far away. It is 300 bytes away.

**Why the constant was not raised a third time.** Three families' settling
times grow with the size of the input:

| Family | Settling time | Excluded by name? |
|---|---|---|
| A backtick inside a code fence's info string | `n + 2` in the fence count | yes |
| A list item's content indented past its own column | linear in the indentation | yes, threshold derived from `SETTLES_BY` |
| A backslash run in a fence info string | ⌊log₂ n⌋ + 1 | **no — "covered" by the constant** |

A coverage-guided fuzzer over `-max_len=65536` can always make the input
bigger. So against bytes **any constant is a statement about `max_len` rather
than about the engine**, and each raise buys one soak: 4 lasted one run, 8
lasted one run. Excluding the third family by name would work exactly as long
as no fourth exists, and the list would then be an enumeration of engine
defects the property had been made blind to — which is not a property.

**What changed.** `fuzz/fuzz_targets/round_trip.rs` no longer asserts settling
at all. It asserts **totality under repetition** — `parse` and `serialize`
driven four times over their own output, each pass fed the previous pass's
result, with the file-layer invariant (`ends_with('\n')`) checked at every pass.
That output is the input class **no fixture in this repository contains**, and
reaching it never needed the settling assertion. The upstream `pulldown-cmark`
guard stays, asked of every element of the sequence rather than only of the
source.

The settling claim now lives in
[`round_trip_properties.rs`](../crates/mt-md/tests/round_trip_properties.rs)
**alone**, at 8, with its four exclusions and its derived threshold, over
generators whose shapes this repository enumerates.

**This inverts a rule the document has applied everywhere else** — that two
harnesses should carry one claim rather than two claims that differ by a
harness's convenience — and the inversion is the finding. A claim that is true
of a generator and false of a fuzzer is **two claims**; writing it once in each
place is the hole rather than the cover. §10's *"Owed by S7"* carries the day
it goes back: when findings 4 and 5 are repaired the bound becomes real and
small, and the byte fuzzer should assert it again.

### `SETTLES_BY` is 8 because a sweep says `t/4 + 2`, and the threshold is written as the inequality

The comfortable hypothesis — *both crash inputs are the known-excluded
backtick-info-string fence, so the predicate is merely too narrow* — is
**refuted on every clause**, which is the only reason the number moved at all.
Shard 2's input contains **0 backticks of 163 bytes**; it is a `~~~` fence. And
**0 of 3** exclusion predicates fire, on either input or on any of the first 20
elements of either sequence, at either option set. Both inputs **settle** — at
**8** and at **6** — so 4 is simply below the true maximum, and the two new
families behind them are serializer defects rather than slow convergence:

1. content indented past a list item's column **sheds 4 columns per pass**, so
   the settling time is linear in the indentation and **no constant bound
   exists**;
2. a backslash run in a fence info string **halves per pass**, ⌊log₂n⌋+1, because
   the parser unescapes `\\` and the serializer does not re-escape it.

**The constant is 8 and the interesting part is the threshold, which is derived
rather than guessed.** Over a 4,590-document sweep — five markers × nine bodies ×
five container wraps × seventeen indents × two option sets — the worst settling
time among documents kept by a threshold of `t` columns is exactly `t / 4 + 2`,
monotone for every `t` from 4 to 32. So `t = 4 * (SETTLES_BY - 2) = 24` is the
widest indentation that 8 covers, and the predicate is **written as that
expression** rather than as `24`, so the two cannot drift apart. It skips **0 of
1,324** spec examples and **0 of 11** round-trip fixtures: the whole cost of the
exclusion is paid by generated and fuzzed documents, which is where the family
lives. `the_settling_time_grows_with_a_list_items_indentation` pins the law at
both sides of the boundary, and both triage numbers reproduce — shard 1 settles
at 8 (32 columns, excluded), shard 2 at 6 (not excluded, two passes of margin).
`fuzz/fuzz_targets/round_trip.rs` carries the same constant and the same
argument, because a bound stated in one of the two places is a bound that moves
in one of them.

**8 is a bound on a defect, not a property of a correct serializer.** §10 says so
too: when the two families are repaired the number should come down, and if it
does not, the residue is a third defect rather than a number to raise.

### S6's gate had a hole cut in its input space, and a large denominator is not a cover

S6's row in §6 reads *"the tree, the leaf ranges and the label map all equal a
full parse's over **3.2 million** generated edit sequences"*. That is true, and
those 3.2 million sequences **could not possibly** have found the class above:

- `crates/mt-md/tests/reparse_properties.rs:210` pipes every fixture through
  `mt_md::normalize_source`, which folds `\r\n` *and a lone `\r`* to `\n`;
- **all 11 of the 11** round-trip fixtures contain a bare `\r`, so not one of
  them reached the property test with it intact;
- `SHAPES` contained **0** `\r` bytes and `INSERTS` contained **0**.

So the generator did not merely fail to reach the class — it **guaranteed** it
could not. §8 has carried the row *"a harness that pre-normalises its inputs
cannot test the layer that normalizes them"* since **S3**, where the same
mistake was in `dump-ts-state.mjs` and turned 11 of 22 corpus files red the first
time `dump_state` returned `Ok`. **This is that row firing a second time, in a
different harness, four stages later** — which makes it a property of how this
project builds harnesses rather than an incident, and it is recorded in §8 as
such.

**The general lesson, stated plainly: a large denominator is not a cover.** 3.2
million of an input space with a hole cut in it is 3.2 million answers to a
question nobody asked.

**What was done about it, and one thing deliberately *not* done.** `SHAPES`
gained **5** CR-bearing entries and `INSERTS` gained **3**, including all three
carriage-return spellings. `fixtures()`' `normalize_source` was **kept**, with
the reason in a comment rather than in a commit message: those files are read
**from disk**, `normalize_source` *is* `mt-cli`'s `read_markdown` and is where
`mt-fs` will live, so a fixture read without it models a caller that does not
exist. The caller who really hands `Incremental` a raw `\r` — a clipboard paste,
a fuzz target — is modelled by the shapes and the inserts, and that is where the
coverage went. `the_generator_can_reach_the_lone_carriage_return_class` asserts a
**lone** `\r` is reachable from both sources and sweeps every offset of every CR
shape × all inserts × both option sets, so the hole cannot be reopened by a
tidy-up.

### The repaired generator found two more of the same class on its first two runs

Which is the only evidence that a repaired generator is repaired.

**Neither is a grid defect**, which is the part worth keeping: unifying the grid
stops the port *crashing* on a disagreement between the two engines behind it; it
does not make `pulldown-cmark` and `marked` agree about what a document
containing a lone `\r` **is**, and both of these assumed they did.

1. **A one-space insert *between* a `\r` and its `\n`** splits one line ending
   into two, so the line grid moves under the whole document. The `\r` whose
   meaning changes is **beside** the edit, not in it, so no `MID_LINE_TRIGGERS`
   check could ever have seen it. `Incremental::leaf_text_edit` now declines an
   edit that starts inside a terminator as well as one at a line start.
2. **A region containing a lone `\r` is not reasonable in isolation.**
   `parse("    indented\n    code\n\n\r#")` drops the trailing heading entirely,
   while the same region parsed on its own keeps it — in isolation there is no
   indented code block in front of it. `span_for` now declines such a region **in
   both sources**, exactly as it already declines one holding a label.

Both are pinned by
`an_edit_that_changes_what_a_carriage_return_means_declines_the_fast_paths`.

Family C's first mechanism was fixed by transcription rather than by discovery:
`leaf_text_edit` lacked the `defines_a_label` guard `span_for` already had, so a
mid-line edit that invalidated a two-line reference definition split a paragraph
the full parse keeps whole
(`a_mid_line_edit_that_dissolves_a_reference_definition_keeps_one_paragraph`).
Its second mechanism was A's root cause — `preceded_by_blank_line` answering a
question about a line that does not exist, so a hard boundary was declared where
the full parse merges two paragraphs — and A's fix closed it exactly as the
triage predicted, one repair for two of the three defects, which is why the grid
was done first
(`a_lone_carriage_return_hides_a_paragraph_merge_from_the_region_reparse`).

### The second soak, job by job — one unambiguous win, and four jobs that are not

Soak run [31253525223](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31253525223),
dispatched on round 2's commit `946f99c`, ran to completion. Job by job against
the first dispatch, so that what moved is visible rather than asserted:

| Job | First soak ([31089007249](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31089007249)) | Second soak ([31253525223](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31253525223)) |
|---|---|---|
| `fuzz tokenize` / `autolink` / `backtracking`, 6 shards | success | success |
| **`fuzz parse`, 2 shards** | success | **success** |
| `fuzz round_trip`, 2 shards | failure | failure |
| `fuzz reparse`, 2 shards | failure | failure |
| `proptest soak` linux / macos | failure | failure |
| `proptest soak` windows | failure | cancelled |
| `fuzz build`, `release invariants`, `full sweep` | success | success |

**Family A is fixed and holding, and that is the one unambiguous win of round
2.** Both `fuzz parse` shards ran **4 hours each** without a crash, and the
panic sweep behind them is **3,000,000 runs with 0 panics in this crate**
against a baseline of **284 panics at 5 sites**. It should not be lost in the
rest of this section, because it is the only clause of the four that changed
state.

**And CI was green on three platforms on the same commit.** Run
[31253517799](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31253517799)
is `success` on linux, windows and macos on `946f99c`, the commit the red soak
was dispatched from. The first dispatch produced that pairing once and this
section is the second time; §6 called it *"the finding, not the failure"* then,
and two occurrences in one stage make it a statement about the **check set** —
everything whose denominator is a corpus agrees, and the only check whose
denominator is time disagrees.

### Round 1's soak died at 424 seconds, so absence from it is not evidence of absence

**Round 1's `proptest soak` failed at 424 s on the char-boundary panic; round
2's ran for ~70 minutes before failing.** The panic was in front of everything
behind it, so the first run never reached the inputs the second one did.

That fact does one job in this section and it is worth stating before the
findings rather than after: **neither finding 6 nor finding 7 can be called a
regression introduced by round 2 without evidence, and no such evidence exists.**
The honest statement is that they are newly *observed*, that round 1's run is
silent about them by construction, and that establishing which came first is
part of what is owed forward — for finding 6 it decides the fix, because the
trigger is a lone `\r` and a lone `\r` is exactly what round 2 changed the
meaning of.

The general form is one §8 already carries in a different spelling: **a run that
stops early has a denominator of everything it reached, not of everything it
was pointed at.**

### Finding 6 — the fourth settling family does not settle slowly, it **grows**

`proptest soak`, **linux and macos**, at
[`round_trip_properties.rs:2311`](../crates/mt-md/tests/round_trip_properties.rs).
Each application of `serialize ∘ parse` **appends one more copy of a line**:

```text
m1: ```Ѩ… \r <line>            2 copies
m9: ```Ѩ… \r <line> × 10      10 copies
```

**This is the distinction that matters, and it is not a rate.** The other three
settling families are *slow* — `n + 2` in a fence count, linear in a list item's
stray indentation, ⌊log₂ n⌋ + 1 in a backslash run — and every one of them
reaches a fixed point eventually. This one **diverges**: the document grows by
one line on every application, forever. No `SETTLES_BY` can cover it at any
value, and widening an exclusion predicate to name it would be excluding *data
growth on every save* from a property whose whole subject is what happens when a
user saves twice.

The trigger is a **lone `\r` inside a fenced code block's body**. Whether that
behaviour is pre-existing or was introduced by round 2's grid fix is
**unestablished**, and for the reason above round 1's soak cannot answer it.
Establishing which comes first is the first thing any follow-up does, because
the two answers point at different code: a pre-existing defect is the
serializer's fence path, and a new one is the terminator asymmetry.

### Finding 7 — a second reparse divergence after the first was fixed, and these look like a **class** rather than a list

`fuzz reparse`, **both shards**, at
[`reparse.rs:194`](../fuzz/fuzz_targets/reparse.rs): `SPEC: ""` with edit
`[4] 11..37 → ""` — *"the trees disagree"*. Round 2's `defines_a_label` guard on
`leaf_text_edit` closed the divergence round 1 found, and this is a different
one, found by the same target on the next run.

**The judgement this stage records is that the reparse divergences look like a
class rather than a list**, and it is a judgement rather than a measurement, so
it is written as one. What supports it: round 1's soak found **two** mechanisms
(the missing `defines_a_label` guard, and `preceded_by_blank_line` answering a
question about a line that does not exist), the repaired generator found **two
more** the same week — all four repaired at round 2 — and the second soak found a
**fifth** in the first hours of a target already pointed at this code. Five
mechanisms across two dispatches, each a *different* reason the incremental path
answers something a full parse does not, is the shape of a class. What would
settle it is not more triage — it is a dedicated reparse campaign, and §10 says
what that means, notes that the target it needs already exists, and states the
consequence for M3/M4.

**Round 1's structural finding is why this matters more than a fourth
reproducer.** S6's gate was 3.2 M generated edit sequences and its input space
had a hole cut in it; the byte fuzzer is the only harness in this repository
whose insertion alphabet is unbounded, and it has now disagreed with a full
parse on every dispatch it has been given.

### Finding 8 — a stack overflow behind the depth clamp, reproducible but with no artifact, and the first guess at why was wrong

Observed in a 420-second local `round_trip` run under ASan, and then
**reproduced in a second run of the same length** — so it is not a one-off.

**The first explanation was wrong and is corrected here rather than quietly
dropped.** The first run's output had been piped through `head`, and the
missing artifact was put down to that pipeline SIGPIPE'ing cargo-fuzz. The
second run took no pipe at all and still wrote nothing, which falsifies it. The
real reason is in the exit:

```text
==40628==ERROR: AddressSanitizer: stack-overflow on address 0x7ffe95fd6142
    <empty stack>
SUMMARY: AddressSanitizer: stack-overflow
error: process didn't exit successfully … (exit code: 0xc0000005, STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION)
```

**libFuzzer never writes the unit because the process dies of an access
violation rather than of a controlled abort**, and ASan cannot even symbolise
the frame — `<empty stack>`. So the input is not recoverable by re-running the
same command, and re-finding it needs `-runs=…` bisection of the corpus or a
larger thread stack; §10 says so. That is a property of how this crash
terminates, not of the invoking shell.

Two things bound how much it should be believed, and both cut towards *artifact*
rather than *defect*:

- **It has been seen only on Windows under ASan.** The Linux soak's
  `fuzz round_trip` shards fail on the settling assertion, not on a stack
  overflow, over 4 hours each.
- **ASan inflates stack frames substantially**, and this was an ASan build.
- **`html.rs`'s walk is the crate's deepest-framed** — measured at **995 levels
  on a 1 MiB thread in release** and **369 in `dev`**, against `parse`'s 1,229 —
  so the headroom a sanitizer has to eat is a measured number rather than a
  guess, and in `dev` it is not large.

Two things bound how much it should be believed, and both cut towards *artifact*
rather than *defect*:

- **ASan inflates stack frames substantially**, and this was an ASan build.
- **`html.rs`'s walk is the crate's deepest-framed** — measured at **995 levels
  on a 1 MiB thread in release** and **369 in `dev`**, against `parse`'s 1,229 —
  so the headroom a sanitizer has to eat is a measured number rather than a
  guess, and in `dev` it is not large.

And one thing bounds what it is **not**: it is **not** the unbounded container
recursion round 1 found and fixed. That one is clamped at `MAX_NESTING_DEPTH =
128`, all six recursive walks were audited rather than the one that crashed, and
`"> " × 2000` — the 4,001-byte file that overflowed a plain `--release` build —
parses in 50 ms today. A second overflow behind the clamp would be a different
mechanism, which is exactly why losing its input costs something.

### S7's cost

**One dependency, and it is outside the workspace this project ships.**
`arbitrary`, in `fuzz/Cargo.toml`, alongside `mt-md` and `mt-doc`. `cargo xtask
deps` still reports 14 crates and four headless, because the `fuzz/` workspace is
not the main one. The package is renamed `mt-inline-fuzz` → **`mt-fuzz`**: it now
holds targets for three crates, and the old name would lie.

**One new integration test** — `crates/mt-md/tests/round_trip_properties.rs`,
2,140 lines, 14 tests plus an `#[ignore]`d `soak()`, with a committed
`.proptest-regressions` beside it holding one seed. No new module in `mt-md`, and
no new `xtask` runner: the fifth was S5's and S7 needed none.

**Four new fuzz targets where §11.3 named one path**, and **48 CPU-hours per soak
dispatch where the file claimed 24** — the same ~4 h of wall clock, six
concurrent runners instead of three.

**One new constant that is public behaviour rather than an implementation
detail.** `MAX_NESTING_DEPTH = 128` changes what deeply-nested documents parse
to, and eight files gained a depth argument, a counter or a worklist to honour
it. It is the first number in this milestone chosen by the port rather than
reproduced from muya, and it is documented as such at every one of the six walks.

**One environment fact that is not a repository fact**, recorded so the next
stage does not rediscover it: `cargo`'s own networking is blocked on this machine
at the application level — `curl` and `git` reach crates.io and `cargo` gets TCP
connect timeouts — and the `fuzz/` workspace needs one crate the main workspace
does not cache. `cargo +nightly fuzz build` was run against a vendored
`CARGO_HOME`. Nothing in the repository depends on that and CI is unaffected.

### Correction to S0's gate row — the 215 was 221 − 6, and §7 excludes 17

M1's rows needed correcting five times out of eight, always in the same
direction, and §6 above says to do the arithmetic against `PENDING` rather than
against the row. Done against the source, S0's row is wrong twice.

**First, the plan disagrees with itself.** §6's S0 row and the exit-gate table
both say **215**. §7's inventory says 221 total and its prose excludes
`flushPendingOps` (4), `setContentClearsOpCache` (2) and `htmlToMarkdown` (11)
— seventeen. 221 − 17 = **204**, not 215. The 215 is 221 − 6: it counts the
op-cache six and forgets §7's own `htmlToMarkdown` row, which §1 excludes in as
many words.

**Second, 204 is still too many**, because seventeen more of the 221 have no
`mt-doc` or `mt-md` surface to assert against at all. S0 was told not to add
entry points, and every one of these would need one:

| Excluded at S0 | Cases | Why it has no M2 surface | Owed to |
|---|---:|---|---|
| `parityExportHtml` | 6 | `MarkdownToHtml.generate()` — inlined CSS, heading slug ids, the TOC stylesheet | M6, `mt-export` |
| `exportHtmlDir` | 3 | `MarkdownToHtml.generate({ dir })` — `<html lang="en" dir="rtl">` | M6, `mt-export` |
| `footnoteExportHtml` | 2 | `MarkdownToHtml.renderHtml()` — the export path's footnote pass | M6, `mt-export` |
| `mermaidExportResilience` | 2 | `MarkdownToHtml.renderHtml()` with the diagram loader mocked | M6, `mt-export` + `mt-diagram` |
| `blockSerialization`'s three live-`CodeBlock` cases | 3 | `codeBlock.lang = 'js'`, a DOM class swap, an OT-op flush | M4 |
| `diagramFlowchartSequence`'s `sequenceTheme` case | 1 | `MUYA_DEFAULT_OPTIONS.sequenceTheme` — a diagram *rendering* default | M3, `mt-diagram` |
| | **17** | | |

The first four are one class, and it is the one worth being explicit about:
**`markdownToHtml.ts` is the standalone-document export and
`renderToStaticHTML.ts` is not.** §2's port table lists the second and not the
first; §4 C3 is about the second; and `renderToStaticHTML.spec.ts` *asserts the
export behaviours are absent* from it — "returns the bare body HTML, no
`<article>` wrapper", and "renders headings with id-less `<hN>`". Transcribing
`parityExportHtml`'s heading-slug cases against `render_to_static_html` would
assert the opposite of what muya asserts about the same function.

**So the number is 221 − 34 = 187**, and every one of the 34 is named above or
in §7 beside the milestone that owes it. Saying so now is what stops them
reading as debt later; `crates/mt-md/tests/state_specs.rs`'s module docs carry
the same table, so it is findable from the code as well as from the plan.

**Three of the 187 pass today, so `PENDING` holds 184.**
`strongCjkFlanking`'s three live-editor-path cases go through
`mt_inline::tokenizer`, which M1 already ported **including** the CJK flanking
widening (`crates/mt-inline/src/emphasis.rs`) — so they pass at S0, and row two
of the ratchet requires them delisted. M1's S1 argument for delisting a case
that passes for a reason the stage did not cause applies unchanged: a listed
case that passes means the list has stopped measuring what is implemented. The
three `static / export path` cases beside them stay listed until S5, so the
file is not delisted as a unit.

**One definitional slip in §7, while it is being corrected.** §7 says *"DOM"
means the file carries `// @vitest-environment happy-dom`*. Ten files do; five
carry **`jsdom`** — `exportHtmlDir`, `htmlToMarkdown`, `mermaidExportResilience`,
`parityExportHtml`, `renderToStaticHTML`. The ● marks themselves are right
(102 cases across 15 files need a DOM, leaving §7's 119 that do not); only the
sentence defining the mark is wrong.

### Correction to S1's gate row — the strict reading, and S2's denominator

**S1's row is loose in one direction and its S2 sibling is stale in another.**
Both are corrected here rather than read charitably, per §6's own instruction.

**1 — the gate.** §6's S1 row says *"every residual disagreement is either
registered or a `meta` field, and no *name* disagreement survives
unregistered"*, which tolerates an unregistered `meta` disagreement
indefinitely. The brief's statement of the same gate does not: *"every residual
disagreement is either a registered block divergence or nothing."* S1 measured
**zero of either**, so the stricter reading costs nothing to adopt and
`cargo xtask blocks` enforces it — an unregistered `meta` disagreement fails
the run. The looser reading would have left a class that can grow without
failing anything, which is the shape M1 spent five stages learning to distrust.

**2 — S2's numbers are not comparable and are re-measured.** The S2 row's
"956 of 1167 … 1,319 of 4,419" is §4 C2's measurement of the **naive** reading
of §4 — a leaf's text as `&src[range]` — over the 1167 cases that agreed
structurally *before* the mapping layer existed. Two things changed underneath
it: the structure now agrees on **1344**, so the denominator is 4,985 leaves
rather than 4,419; and S1's leaves carry the **empty string** by design rather
than a source slice, so the comparison is against nothing rather than against a
wrong guess.

Measured today with `cargo xtask blocks --with-text`:

| | Value |
|---|---:|
| Inputs whose full `TState` JSON already agrees | **13 / 1344** |
| Leaves compared | **4,985** |
| Leaves whose text differs | **4,954** |

The 13 are the documents with no leaf text to get wrong. **This is the number
S2 drives to 1344 / 4,985 / 0**, and it is a starting point rather than a floor
— C2's 1,319-of-4,419 remains the useful figure for *"what a source-slice
implementation scores"*, which is the thing S2 must beat rather than
reproduce.

### Correction to S2's gate row — three things it says that turned out otherwise

Per §6's own instruction, corrected rather than read loosely.

**1 — `--with-text` is gone, and the flag inverted.** The row names the gate as
`cargo xtask blocks --with-text`. That spelling was right while leaf text was a
gap being reported: at S1 every leaf carried the empty string, so a run that
compared text would have printed 4,954 disagreements saying nothing about block
structure, and the flag being opt-in is what let the runner say *"leaf text is
excluded, here is how big the exclusion is"* instead of hiding it. S2 closed the
gap, so **the full `TState` JSON is what a bare run compares and `--no-text` is
what asks for less.** A default that hides the largest class is a default that
stops measuring the moment someone forgets the flag — and `cargo xtask ci` calls
this runner with no arguments but `--require-ts`, so the inversion is also what
puts leaf text in CI.

**2 — "D2's eight rules" is eight of thirteen.** §5 D2's table has eight rows
and they are all correct. Five more leaf kinds have a text rule and D2 does not
list them, because C2's measurement was over the leaves that *differed from a
source slice* and these five either did not appear or happened to agree:

| Kind | Rule | Where D2's table is silent |
|---|---|---|
| the reference-definition paragraph | `token.raw.replace(/\n+$/, '')`, and `Tokenizer.def`'s `cap[0]` **starts at the ` {0,3}` indent** | D3 decides these blocks exist; nothing decided their text |
| `html-block` | `trimTrailingBlankLines` then `.trim()` — two passes, not one | C2's table says "trimmed" |
| `diagram` | the code-block text, **untrimmed**, where `math-block` from the same fence *is* trimmed | D2 lists `math-block` and not `diagram` |
| a task item's first paragraph | `/^\[[ xX]\] +/` off — except in loose ordered lists, where `marked` puts it back and `compatibleTaskList` never takes it off | the brief named the strip; the exception is new |
| `code-block`, either kind | `trimUnnecessaryCodeBlockEmptyLines` (#1265) on top | D2 does not mention the option |

**3 — the stripper is not only a stripper.** D2 calls it *"the one part of D2
that is a scanner rather than a rewrite"*. Two of its levels rewrite:
`blockquoteSetextReplace` **inserts** four spaces in front of a lazy setext
underline, and `expandTabs` inside a list item turns tabs into spaces. Both
change bytes that end up in a leaf's text, and both are `marked`'s. The sentence
is right about the shape and wrong about the strictness, which matters because
it is the reason a line's text is not always a slice of the source — see the
reverse-map finding below.

### Correction to S3's gate row — the delist, and one thing the row asked for that was already true

**1 — the delist is 63, and both predictions were wrong in the same direction.**
§6's S3 row carried S1's correction — S3 delists S1's 39 and S2's 7 alongside
its own 8, which is 54 — and a second reading of §7 said that
`markdownToState`'s 32 belong here too, which would be 86. Run:
**`PENDING` goes from 184 to 121, so S3 delists 63.** Neither number is the
answer and the reason is in §6's own instruction: the arithmetic has to be done
against `PENDING`, and §7's stage column is a prediction about *files* while the
ratchet is a list of *cases*. Twenty-seven of `markdownToState`'s 32 delist and
five do not; eleven of `gitlabMath`'s sixteen do; two of `listSerialization`'s
thirty do. The full arithmetic is below.

**2 — "delete `every_pending_case_fails_because_the_engine_is_unimplemented`"
is right, and the row's other two deletions were half-right.**
`mt_md::tests::entry_points_report_unimplemented_at_m0` does not disappear: its
`dump_state` assertion goes and its `render_to_static_html` one stays, because
D6 stages three entry points and only one of them opened here. `serialize`'s
assertion is *added* in the same edit, so the test shrinks by one clause per
stage and disappears at S5 rather than being deleted early and re-invented.
`mt-cli`'s `dump_state_exits_unimplemented_at_m0` is replaced rather than
deleted, by the assertion its own doc comment predicted — *"when `mt-md` lands
this becomes 0 and the harness starts enforcing"*.

`cargo test --workspace`, the five `xtask` runners, and one measurement with no
runner behind it.

```text
  ci:           run 31000529354 — green on linux, windows and macos, first dispatch

  PENDING:      11 → 2, and both of the two are M6's

                GFM         476 / 672 = 70.8 %   (floor 70.8, unmoved)

  properties:   6, over 3.2 M generated edit sequences at three option sets

| …and one block **of the tree**, not one block of a region | **met** | `one_block_stays_one_block_inside_a_quote_inside_a_list`. §4.1 step 1 is about the block the caret is in, and a paragraph inside a quote inside a list item is still one — which is what made the path re-derive a leaf from `(src, tree, ranges)` rather than re-parse its enclosing top-level block |

Two independent 400,000-case runs of each of the four generated properties — 3.2 M sequences — plus the smaller runs that came before them. What CI runs is the committed config: 192 cases each, on three platforms. Equality is `dump_state`'s JSON **and** the leaf source ranges **and** the label map's size |

It is what caught the region's `hard_boundary` disagreeing with the reparse's own output, which no random position hit in the first hundred cases |

The matching key is **full subtree equality**, so a match is not a heuristic — see the decision below |

| `round_trip.rs`'s two exception lists | **met, unchanged** | 22 fixed-point and 348 identity, both still exact — after a change to how every list item's children are built |
| `mt_md::serialize` still byte-identical to `ExportMarkdown.generate` | **met, 1346 of 1346, re-taken rather than assumed** | S4 took it by hand and S5 re-took it because it touched `parse`. S6 touches `parse` twice over — the re-lex and the region entry point — so it is re-taken again, same script shape, same answer |

| **And green in CI** | **MET, on the first dispatch** | Run [31000529354](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31000529354) — `success` on **linux, windows and macos**. Five stages in a row, which S2 said should be the standard rather than the achievement |

### The three things S6 was asked to settle before any code

#### 1 — the matching key is subtree **equality**, and that is why "a wrong match" is not a risk category

§8's risk row is that a live `NodeId` handed to `mt-layout` addresses a
different block — silently, in a crate that does not exist yet to notice.
Generational ids (D8) do not help: they turn a *stale* handle into `None` and
have nothing to say about a live one.

The key is **full structural equality of the subtree** — name, `meta`, text and
children recursively — matched by a longest common subsequence over each sibling
list, with the common prefix and suffix trimmed first so the quadratic middle is
one or two entries wide on the shape that actually happens.

**Two subtrees that compare equal *are* the same block to every consumer**, so
reusing the id is correct by construction. Between those anchors the leftovers
are paired positionally and reconciled only when they are the same kind — or two
*leaves* of different kinds, which is `Edit::ReplaceBlock` doing precisely what
S0 wrote it for (*"the paragraph-to-heading kind of transformation"*), so that
typing `#` at the start of a line does not cost `mt-layout` its cache entry.
Reconciling then emits the edits that make the node hold exactly the new block.

**So the answer to "what does a wrong match cost" is: not correctness.** There
is no path on which a live id ends up addressing content it was not given. What
a mis-paired leftover costs is *reuse* — a node survives that a human would have
called a different block, and `mt-layout` re-lays it out because its content
changed. That is the whole downside, and it is what makes equality the right key
rather than position or `(name, meta)`.

**Where the entry point lives: `mt-md`, which is what D7 said.** The diff emits
`Edit`s against a live `Document` and D9 forbids a write path that bypasses
`apply` — but `mt-doc` cannot parse, and `block::parse_blocks` has gone through
`apply` since S1 for exactly this reason. `mt-md` is the only crate that can both
run the block parser and see the tree. `block::build_span` is the
region-taking entry point D7 asked for, and `mt_md::reparse::Incremental` is the
ergonomic one over it.

**The two things S5's experience said to check rather than assume are both
real**, and a third turned up beside them:

1. **`seen_labels` is document-global.** A definition whose label was already
   defined anywhere earlier emits no block at all — so a region that does not
   thread it can resurrect a definition the document already consumed, and one
   that *defines* a label can suppress a later duplicate outside itself. The
   guard is a definition-shaped line at or before the region's end, **in either
   source**: asking the new source alone misses the case where the edit *destroys*
   the first of two definitions and un-suppresses the second, which lives outside
   the region and would never be revisited. That case was found by the generator.
2. **Footnote segmentation and region choice disagree**, exactly as the brief
   said. `footnote_segments` scans the whole document and a definition's extent
   runs forward through blank lines, so with `Options::footnote` on the region
   is the whole document — which is still id-preserving, because the fallback is
   a region reparse whose region happens to be everything.
3. **Front matter is a whole-document construct with an *unanchored* closing
   delimiter**, which neither the brief nor D5 names. `---\na\n\nbody\n\nmore---\n`
   is one front-matter block whose text is everything up to the last three
   characters, so typing `---` at the end of the last paragraph of a document
   that merely *begins* with `---` turns the whole document into one block. And
   `Block::Frontmatter`'s range is its **text**, not its extent — the delimiters
   are outside it — so a region that starts at `bounds[0].end` hands the parser a
   `---` that becomes a thematic break the document does not contain. Both are
   guarded and both are tested.

#### 2 — the nine `listSerialization` cases: **a plain re-lex is a no-op, and that is the finding**

§10 has said since S4 that the fix is *"a re-lex of every list item's dedented
content rather than a patch to a sibling list"*, and that S6 is the stage with
the machinery. §6 asks for that to be run rather than read. Run, it is wrong.

**Asked of both engines, the item's dedented content parsed as a whole document
gives the port's current answer *and muya's own*:**

```text
"3. foo\n   20. foo\n       141. foo\n"
  muya      order-list › item › order-list(20) › item › order-list(141)
  the port  order-list › item › paragraph "foo\n20. foo\n141. foo"

"foo\n20. foo\n141. foo\n"          ← the same content, re-lexed at top level
  muya      paragraph "foo\n20. foo\n141. foo"
  the port  paragraph "foo\n20. foo\n141. foo"     ← identical
```

So nothing about *where* the content is lexed is in dispute. What differs is
**which rule set applies**, and `marked` says so in one line of its own source:

```text
Tokenizer.list:   this.lexer.state.top = false; this.lexer.blockTokens(item.text, []);
Lexer.blockTokens: if (this.state.top && (token = this.tokenizer.paragraph(cutSrc)))
rules.ts paragraph: .replace('list', ' {0,3}(?:[*+-]|1[.)])[ \t]+[^ \t\n]')
                    // only non-empty lists starting from 1 can interrupt
```

`state.top = false` removes the `paragraph` rule, and the CommonMark restriction
lives in that regex **and nowhere else**. The two regexes — `list`'s `bull` and
`paragraph`'s substitution — differ in exactly two ways: an ordered marker that
is not `1`, and a marker with no content after it. That is why the fix is bounded
rather than a second block lexer: split the paragraph at the refused line and
re-lex from there, where a list start is a list start in both engines.

**So it is one change after all, but not the change §10 named**, and the
difference matters because the named one would have shipped a no-op and looked
like progress. `block::split_refused_list_starts` carries the argument, the two
regexes and the measurement; `marked_list_line` is the predicate; and the safety
valve is that the split only happens when re-lexing the tail actually produces a
non-paragraph first block, so a line the predicate matches and `pulldown-cmark`
does not open a list on cannot turn one paragraph into two.

**One thing the fix had to learn from the failing case rather than from the
source.** Re-lexing only the *paragraph's* tail leaves `- a\n  * \n  * b` as two
sibling lists, because `pulldown-cmark` has already made `* b` its own list —
`* b` is allowed to interrupt where `* ` is not. `marked`'s loop does not stop at
the list it opens, so the re-lex runs from the refused line to the **end of the
container**, and the recursion inside it is what makes `state.top = false` apply
to the whole item rather than to one paragraph.

#### 3 — the floor **tracks** the ratchet, and D5 says so already

The reading is settled here because the brief is right that D5 can be read two
ways. *"You are choosing the floor, and the floor can never be raised again"*
(`spec/README.md` step 1) is about the **one-way door**: `--update` may be walked
through once, and no later regeneration may re-derive a floor to fit a worse
engine. It is not a claim that the constant is frozen.

D5's third point is the other half and it is unambiguous: *"After re-baselining,
the floor becomes the **measured** rate. That is what makes 'compliance only goes
up' true of the number as well as of the list."* And `spec/README.md`'s own table
records the state that produces: **slack zero, by construction**, "so the two
cannot drift". A frozen constant would let slack accumulate silently, which is
the drift D5 exists to prevent.

**Decided: whenever a delist raises the measured rate, `floor_percent()` is
raised to the new measured rate in the same commit** — exactly as S5 did, and for
the same reason. The one-way door is `--update`; the constant is a backstop that
follows the measurement. Written into D5 rather than only here.

**And the prediction it governs turned out not to be needed: the number did not
move.** 463/652 and 476/672, unchanged, list unchanged, floor unchanged. The
argument was available *before* the run and is worth keeping because it is
sharper than the number: `cargo xtask blocks` is 1344/1344 over an input set that
**contains all 1324 conformance fixtures**, so the port already agrees with muya
on every one of them. A block-layer change either leaves all 1324 parses
untouched — in which case the rendered HTML is untouched and conformance cannot
move — or it changes one, in which case `blocks` drops below 1344 and the build
fails first. There was no third outcome, and the run confirmed the first.

### S6's delist — nine, exactly the nine that were predicted

**`PENDING` goes from 11 to 2, and both survivors are M6's.**

| Spec file | Listed at S5 | Delisted at S6 | Still listed | Why the residue stays |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| `listSerialization` | 9 | 9 | 0 | — |
| `renderToStaticHTML` | 2 | 0 | 2 | KaTeX — `mt-math` is §9's M6 |
| | **11** | **9** | **2** | |

**The prediction, recorded before the run, and it is the first stage to be right
about every number at once:**

| | Predicted | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| `PENDING` | **2** — the two KaTeX names already in `KNOWN` | **2**, exactly those |
| CommonMark | **71.0 %, unmoved** | 71.0 %, unmoved |
| GFM | **70.8 %, unmoved** | 70.8 %, unmoved |
| `expected-failures.json` | **189 / 196, unchanged** | 189 / 196 |

The three ways §6 said the prediction would be wrong, and none of them fired.
*The nine need something S6 does not have* — they needed something §10 did not
have, which is a different failure and is decision 2. *A case starts passing in a
file you did not touch* — none; two stages in a row now. *The arithmetic moves
the other way, and S6 is the first stage that can make a passing case fail* —
none, and the reason is structural rather than lucky: the `blocks` floor makes
"a passing case fails" impossible to reach without `blocks` failing first, over
the same 1324 inputs.

`the_only_listed_cases_that_fail_for_their_own_reason_are_the_two_m6_names` is
the renamed assertion, and it now guards a list with **no case of M2's own left
in it** — the state M1 §10 named as the dangerous one, which is why both
survivors carry their milestone in the constant's own comment.

### The traps the brief named, and which of them fired

| Trap | Fired? |
|---|---|
| The gate says a counter, not a timer | **Not fired, because it was built as a counter.** `Reparse::blocks_reparsed` is on the return value and public. The temptation the row is about is real — a `#[cfg(test)]` counter would have been three lines shorter — and the reason it is refused is that a counter only a test can read is one the shipped code can stop maintaining |
| A wrong `NodeId` match is worse than no match, and generational indices do not protect you | **Not fired, and the key is why.** Subtree *equality* rather than position, so a match means the content is identical and reuse is correct by construction; a mis-pair costs cache reuse and nothing else. Decision 1 |
| `SourceMap` is no longer injective and S5 is why | **Fired, and it is the single most valuable thing the generator found.** The leaf-text path re-derives a block's text from its range, and under a `footnote` — and now under the list-item re-lex — a node's range is the *container's*. It spliced `20.` into a paragraph as though it were the paragraph's own text. The fix is `SourceMap::is_exact`, a new public accessor that says which ranges are their block's own extent and which are a coarse stand-in, and the path declines a coarse one. **The map's non-injectivity is now a fact a caller can ask about rather than a footnote in §10** |
| You are the first stage that can move the conformance number, and getting better fails the build | **Not fired, and it could not have been.** Decision 3's argument: `blocks`' 1344 contains all 1324 conformance fixtures, so a block-layer change that moves conformance fails `blocks` first. Both were re-run; both unmoved |
| S5's floors are not yours to lower, and a list-item re-lex threatens every one at once | **Not fired, and this was the real risk.** `blocks` 1344/1344 with 4,985 leaves, `diff` 22/22, `divergences` 3 over 27 with the 4,237 sweep clean, `normalize` 2648/2648, and `round_trip.rs`'s 22 and 348 byte-exact |
| `serialize` must keep agreeing with `ExportMarkdown` byte for byte, and nothing will tell you if you break it | **Not fired, and re-taken rather than assumed.** 1346 of 1346, for the third stage running |
| `Block`'s variant set is not yours to change; `mt-inline`'s five `debug_assert!`ed branches are not yours either | **Not fired.** Neither touched. `Built` gained a field (`exact`) and `SourceMap` gained a set; `Block` did not |
| Generators are S7's and the property over fixed corpora is S4's — settle it | **Settled, and the two sentences do not conflict.** S4's is about generating **markdown**, which is what a round-trip property needs and what §11.3 schedules for S7. This generates **edits** over a fixed corpus, which is the shape `mt-doc`'s S0 gate has used `proptest` for since the first commit of this milestone. So `proptest` becomes a `[dev-dependencies]` entry of `mt-md`, it adds nothing to the lockfile, and `cargo xtask deps` still counts 14 |

**And the trap nobody named, which is the shape of the whole stage.** Every
guard in the leaf-text path was written from an argument about markdown, and
**five of the seven were wrong in a way only a generator finds** — an offset in a
container's own prefix (`> ` → `># `), a line whose tab expansion makes its
recorded start not its content start, an edit that empties a line and thereby
ends the paragraph, a paragraph whose *cell count* decides whether the next line
is a table delimiter row, and a range that is coarse rather than exact. None is
subtle once seen; not one was visible from the corpus, the 1344, or the 22. The
general form is worth carrying: **a guard derived from a rule is a hypothesis,
and the only thing that tests a hypothesis about "which edits are safe" is
edits.**

### What the generator found that is **not** S6's, and is recorded rather than fixed

Three, and each has a named input and a test. None is reachable from any fixture
in the repository, which is why five stages did not see them.

1. **`pulldown-cmark` 0.13.4 panics on `"> - [a]: /x\n\t"`** — `Option::unwrap()`
   on `None` inside `OffsetIter::next`, before any of this crate's code runs.
   Four ingredients: a block quote, a list item, a link reference definition, and
   a following line that is whitespace-only and ends in a tab.
   `mt_md::parse` therefore is **not** total, and its doc comment says it is.
   0.13.4 is the latest published version, so there is no upgrade to take, and
   catching it is not open either — the release profile is `panic = "abort"`.
   `block::tests::pulldown_cmark_panics_on_a_definition_in_a_quoted_list_item_before_a_tab_line`
   is `#[should_panic]`, which makes it a ratchet in the useful direction: the
   day the fix lands upstream, it fails and says so. `docs/upstream-issues.md`
   carries it and §10 owes the decision forward.
2. **`front_matter` stepped one *byte* where `[\s\S]+?` backtracks one
   character**, so any document whose front-matter delimiter is followed by a
   multi-byte character panicked on a slice. Fixed at S6 rather than recorded,
   because it is a one-character transcription error rather than a decision.
3. **A child's range can escape its parent's**, and `scan_definitions` could map
   a logical offset *past the end of the source*. Both come from the same place —
   a tab inside a list item is the one case where a stripped line's text is not a
   slice of the source, so its recorded start is the line rather than the content.
   `LogicalText::end` clamps and `strip_lines` clamps; the nesting one is a
   characterization (`the_ranges_are_not_injective_and_do_not_always_nest`),
   because repairing `strip_lines`' clip is a change to 30 of S2's 47 first-run
   disagreements and no fixture asks for it.

### S6 registers nothing either, and five stages is the answer rather than the pattern

`spec/divergences.json` holds three entries, all `layer: "inline"`, and the block
layer still has none after six stages.

S6 is the first stage since S1 whose work is a **block-layer change** — the
`state.top = false` re-lex is a change to how every list item's children are
built — so it is the first since S1 that could plausibly have produced a block
entry. It did not, and the reason is the one that makes the whole fix safe:
**the change is a `marked`-versus-CommonMark disagreement resolved in `marked`'s
favour, which is agreement rather than divergence.** `cargo xtask blocks` is
1344/1344 before and after, which is the same statement from the other side.

Rule 3 would have made an entry `Stale` in any case: no input in the 1344 or the
22 reaches the shape at all, which is exactly why the nine cases needed the
transcribed spec suite and a serializer to find them.

### S6's cost

**One dev-dependency, already in the lockfile.** `proptest`, for `mt-md`. It is
`mt-doc`'s and `mt-inline`'s already; `cargo xtask deps` still reports 14 crates
and four headless.

**One new module in `mt-md`** — `reparse` (≈ 700 lines plus 500 of tests) — and
one new integration test, `tests/reparse_properties.rs`, with a
`.proptest-regressions` file beside it. That file is **committed on purpose**:
sixteen hard-won seeds, which proptest re-runs before any new case, and which are
what a future change to the guards will trip over first.

**Two small shape changes to `block.rs`.** `Built` gains `exact`, and `build`
splits into `build` and `build_span` — the region-taking entry point D7 named at
S0. `Block`'s variant set is untouched, `parse_blocks` and
`parse_blocks_with_ranges` are unchanged, and 1344/1344 still holds.

**One addition to a public type.** `SourceMap::is_exact`, which turns "the map is
not injective" from a paragraph in §10 into a question a caller can ask — and
which M4's caret mapping will want for the same reason S6 did.

### S5's verification

`cargo test --workspace`, and the five `xtask` runners — the fifth is new.

```text
  ci:           run 30973785731 — green on linux, windows and macos, first dispatch
  conformance:  CommonMark  463 / 652 = 71.0 %   (floor 71.0, was 87.7)
                GFM         476 / 672 = 70.8 %   (floor 70.8, was 86.3)
  expected-failures.json:    78 → 189 and 90 → 196
                             71 and 83 kept, 7 and 7 removed, 118 and 113 added
  normalize:    2648 / 2648 agree with runner.ts (1324 rendered + 1324 expected)
```

| Clause | State | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `cargo xtask conformance` **enforcing** | **met** | No `Skipped` verdict is produced any more. `neither_suite_is_skipped_from_s5_on` replaces the deleted `every_suite_is_skipped_at_m0` and asserts it, because a suite that goes back to skipping wholesale exits 0 with every verdict green and the ratchet's own four rows cannot see it |
| Pass rate ≥ the re-baselined floor, both suites | **met, with zero slack by construction** | 71.0 / 71.0 and 70.8 / 70.8. The list *is* today's failures and the floor is today's rate truncated to a tenth, so the backstop sits directly under the measurement — which is D5's third point working |
| The list is no longer than 78/90, **or each addition is argued** | **met on the second arm** | 189 and 196 against 78 and 90. The 231 additions are argued below, every number under a named mechanism with a measured reproducer. D5 anticipated this arm and it is the one that fired |
| `spec/conformance.md` rewritten | **met**, and the inherited 80/92-versus-78/90 inconsistency is fixed rather than copied forward | Per-section rates for both suites, the block-versus-inline split, and the diff in both directions. Every number in it is printed by `cargo xtask conformance --sections` |
| `floor_percent()` raised to the measured rate | **met, in the same commit** | 87.7 → 71.0 and 86.3 → 70.8. D5's third point is that the two drift apart if they move in different commits |
| `normalizeHtml` agrees with `runner.ts`'s over every rendered fixture | **met, 2648 of 2648, on the first run** | `cargo xtask normalize` (§10, owed since M0 decision 12). It compares over the port's 1,324 rendered fixtures **and** the 1,324 expected ones, because the conformance comparison normalises both sides and cmark's HTML is the half containing constructs the port never emits |
| `ammonia` on the sanitize path | **met** | `mt_md::sanitize`, configured against `EXPORT_DOMPURIFY_CONFIG` and measured against jsdom + DOMPurify rather than read off the config object. Four rows in that module's table; two are divergences and both drop output rather than admit it |
| `Options::footnote`'s block extension (§10, owed by S3) | **met** | `block::footnote_segments` and friends — muya's own `marked` extension, not GFM's. `markdownToState::converts_block_level_footnote_tokens_into_footnote_states` delists with `footnoteHtml`'s six |
| `mt-cli --to-html` | **met** | And it is the second user of `ExitCode::UNIMPLEMENTED` S3 predicted — half right: the code is inherited as a *contract* rather than as a behaviour, because S5 opened that entry point too |
| `entry_points_report_unimplemented_at_m0` deleted | **met** | Its own doc comment named this stage. It shrank by one clause per stage from S3 and had one left |
| `cargo xtask blocks` still 1344/1344, 0/0/0, 4,985 leaves | **met** | Re-run after the footnote extension, which is a block-layer change and therefore the one thing this stage could have moved |
| `cargo xtask diff --require-ts` still 22/22 | **met** | `22 passed, 0 failed, 0 registered divergence(s), 0 skipped` |
| `cargo xtask divergences` still 3 inline entries `ok` over 27 of 27 | **met** | Plus the 4,237-input sweep clean |
| `round_trip.rs`'s two exception lists | **met, unchanged** | 22 fixed-point and 348 identity, both still exact. The footnote extension changes `parse`, so all four numbers were at risk and none moved |
| `cargo fmt`, `clippy -D warnings`, `xtask deps`, `xtask ci --require-ts`, `cargo test --workspace` | **met locally** | **808 passed, 0 failed, 2 ignored**, against S4's 773. `deps` still reports 14 crates |
| **And green in CI** | **MET, on the first dispatch** | Run [30973785731](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/30973785731) — `success` on **linux, windows and macos**. That is **four stages in a row** where the first dispatch was green, and S2 said that should be the standard rather than the achievement. What it buys here specifically: the re-baselined `expected-failures.json` is now enforced on three platforms, so the floor that can never be raised again was measured by a machine that is not this one — and `cargo xtask normalize` runs there too, which is the first time `spec/runner.ts` has been executed by CI at all |

### The measurement S4 took by hand, re-taken because S5 touched `parse`

**1346 of 1346 inputs still serialize byte-identically to
`ExportMarkdown.generate`.** S4 recorded that number as a one-off because
§10's "a serializer differential, and the register cannot fire without one"
explains why there is no committed runner for it — and a measurement with no
