runner is a measurement that has to be re-taken by whoever moves the code under
it. The footnote extension is a change to `parse`, so this stage re-took it:
same script shape, same 1,346 inputs, same answer.

Worth saying because the alternative was to assume: every harness in the
repository drives `footnote: false`, so *nothing* would have reported a
regression here. That is the same shape §8's risk table calls "a check exists
but has never executed", one layer further out — a check that exists only as a
number in a document.

### The three things S5 was asked to settle before any code

#### 1 — what renders the inline layer: `mt_inline`, and it is what the number costs

§4 C3 decided the HTML goes through `Document` and left half the decision open,
because a `Document`'s leaves carry **unparsed inline source** (§4 C2). So
`Document → HTML` is two renderers and the milestone had neither. The brief's
two candidates were real and neither was free:

- an inline→HTML pass over `mt_inline::tokenizer`'s tokens — 16 handlers,
  marker-preserving, built for a WYSIWYG editor rather than for export;
- a second inline path shaped like `marked`'s `Renderer`.

**Decided in favour of `mt_inline`**, and the argument is C3's own rather than
a new one: *the exporter must agree with the editor.* muya reaches that
agreement by patching `marked`. `cjkEmStrong` exists only so that
`例子**"加粗"**例子` bolds in the export the way it bolds on screen (marktext
#4307); `softBreakExportHtml` (#3676) is the same bug pointing the other way.
Both are "the two inline engines disagreed", and a port that ships a second
inline renderer inherits that class of bug **permanently**, for an engine with
no consumer inside the application. Rendering from the token stream the editor
already holds cannot have it at all — and the proof is free: the three
`strongCjkFlanking` static-path cases pass here with no CJK code in the
renderer, because M1 put the widening in `emphasis.rs`.

**What it costs, stated as a number rather than as a caveat: 71.0 % and
70.8 %, against muya's 87.7 % and 86.3 %.** `mt_inline` is muya's WYSIWYG
tokenizer, not a CommonMark inline parser, and the two disagree most where
CommonMark is hardest: §6.2 *Emphasis and strong emphasis* is 82 of 132,
because a fixed ordered array of regex handlers is not a delimiter-run
algorithm.

Two things are worth being precise about, because the number invites the wrong
reading:

- **The port is not worse than muya at what the user sees.** muya's *editor*
  renders through the same tokenizer, at the same fidelity. What changed is
  which of muya's two inline engines the conformance suite measures, and this
  one is the shipped one. §11.1's percentage was always a claim about an
  engine; it now names the right one.
- **The block half is not where the residue is.** CommonMark, split at the
  seam: blocks 247 of 295 = **83.7 %**, inlines 214 of 355 = **60.3 %**. GFM's
  two block extensions — tables and strikethrough — are at **100 %**. That is
  `pulldown-cmark` plus the mapping layer doing exactly what §5 D1 adopted it
  for.

So `spec/expected-failures.json` is now **a to-do list for `mt-inline`**, which
is the most useful thing a ratchet can be and is not what it was.

#### 2 — what the re-baseline measures, and D5's rule held

D5 was written before anyone knew what the new list looks like, and its rule —
*"no longer than 78 and 90 unless each additional entry is argued individually
in the same commit"* — was set precisely so it could not be chosen to fit the
number. It was not. The list is **189 and 196**, and the second arm fired.

**The diff runs in both directions and both are reported**, because D5 says the
new list is a different *set* and neither direction is visible from a length:

| | CommonMark | GFM |
|---|---:|---:|
| Inherited | 78 | 90 |
| Regenerated | 189 | 196 |
| Kept — both engines fail | 71 | 83 |
| **Removed — muya fails, the port passes** | **7** | **7** |
| Added — muya passes, the port fails | 118 | 113 |

The seven removals in each suite are one class each and they are the direction
a length erases:

| CommonMark | GFM | Mechanism |
|---:|---:|---|
| 155, 174 | 125, 143 | an HTML block ended by a blank line, at top level and inside a block quote |
| 512 | 520 | `[link [foo [bar]]](/uri)` — nested brackets in link text, which `marked` mis-nests |
| 524, 536 | 532, 544 | `[foo <bar attr="](baz)">` — a `]` inside a raw HTML attribute value |
| 526, 538 | 534, 546 | an autolink whose URL contains `](` |

**And there is an eighth class that does not appear in the diff at all,
because it is not a removal**: muya runs Prism, so every ```` ```js ```` fence
in the fixtures comes back as `<span class="token …">` markup no spec example
expects. Those examples are on *both* lists — muya fails them for Prism, the
port fails them for something else — so the ratchet cannot see that the port is
right about them and muya is not. Recorded here rather than in the diff, and
`html::tests::a_known_language_is_not_syntax_highlighted_the_way_prism_would`
pins the behaviour.

##### The 231 additions, argued

D5 asks for each addition to be argued individually. Each number below is
assigned to a named mechanism with a measured reproducer; that is the argument,
and listing them as prose one at a time would be a way of not being read rather
than a review.

| Mechanism | CM | GFM | CommonMark numbers |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| **`mt_inline`'s emphasis is a regex, not a delimiter-run algorithm.** `_foo _bar_ baz_` → `<em>foo _bar</em> baz_`; `_(_foo_)_` does not nest; `**foo*` opens where CommonMark closes | 50 | 43 | 354, 369, 373, 376, 383, 384, 389, 397, 399, 402, 406–409, 413–419, 425, 426, 432, 435, 437, 439, 442–448, 450, 451, 454–459, 463, 466–468, 471, 472, 478, 479 |
| **A link destination is `parseSrcAndTitle`'s split on whitespace**, and the editor renders `token.href` raw (`renderer/link.ts`: `sanitizeHyperlink(token.href + encodeURI(...))`). So `<…>` is not stripped, `\*` is not unescaped, and a destination with a space still matches | 24 | 23 | 486, 488, 489, 491–495, 497–500, 505, 506, 508, 510, 513, 515, 523, 529, 541, 546, 550, 568 |
| **`commonMarkRules.html_tag` covers elements and comments only.** No processing instruction, declaration or CDATA; no bare closing tag; a different attribute grammar | 10 | 10 | 615, 616, 619, 621, 623, 626–629, 632 |
| **`getLabelInfo`'s rule is single-line and not multi-line**, so a definition spanning lines or carrying escapes never enters `Labels` — and the renderer resolves references through the *editor's* label map by the same argument as decision 1 | 8 | 8 | 193–196, 198, 200, 202, 217 |
| **`inline_code` is `` /^(`{1,3})([^`]+\|.{2,})\1/ ``**, not CommonMark's matching-backtick-run rule | 6 | 6 | 330, 331, 340, 342, 347, 349 |
| **A `\` at end of line is a hard break in CommonMark and plain text to `mt_inline`**; and a two-space break inside a raw HTML attribute value is not a break at all | 5 | 5 | 634, 637, 639, 642, 643 |
| **The same, one level down**: `\&ouml;`, `\`+newline, and destination/title unescaping | 4 | 4 | 14, 16, 22, 23 |
| **`Image.src` and `Image.alt` are the tokenizer's spans**, so a nested `![…](…)` inside alt text and a destination followed by extra spaces both differ | 4 | 4 | 574, 575, 579, 580 |
| **`Block::HtmlBlock`'s text is trimmed** (§4 C2's rule), so an html block's leading indent is gone before the renderer sees it. **The purest instance of what C3 costs** | 3 | 3 | 150, 183, 184 |
| **`commonMarkRules.auto_link` is more permissive about what is a URI** — `<foo.bar.baz>` and `<m:abc>` become links | 3 | 3 | 606, 609, 610 |
| **muya's link rule accepts a destination containing an entity and a space** where CommonMark does not | 1 | 1 | 41 |
| **`auto_link_extension`'s trailing-punctuation handling ends the whole match** rather than trimming it, so `www.commonmark.org.` is not a link at all (GFM only) | — | 3 | — |
| | **118** | **113** | |

Every mechanism is `mt_inline`'s or §4 C2's, and not one of them is the block
mapping layer — which is the same thing the 1344/1344 and the 100 % on tables
and strikethrough say from the other side.

##### One class was fixed rather than argued, and it is worth the paragraph

`<p></p>`. `MarkdownToState` gives an empty block quote (`>`), an empty list
item (`*` alone) and `Document::new`'s placeholder a `paragraph` with the empty
string, because the editor needs an editable line the source does not contain.
`marked` has no `paragraph` token for any of them. The first regenerated list
had six more entries than this one for that reason alone; the renderer now
emits nothing for an empty paragraph, and CommonMark went 460 → 463 and GFM
473 → 476 **before** the floor was frozen.

That is the value of running `--update` and then *reading* rather than
committing: three examples per suite that would have been permanent.

#### 3 — `ammonia` lands, and the gate the brief worried was missing is the spec file

The brief's caution is right about the conformance runner: it drives
`sanitize: false` deliberately (CommonMark §6.9 tests that `<bab>` survives),
so a new dependency would arrive with nothing exercising it. **But the gate is
`renderToStaticHTML.spec.ts`, and seventeen of its twenty transcribed cases
pass `sanitize: true`** — including three that assert stripping. S0 transcribed
them for exactly this.

And the alternative is worse than a dependency: `renderToStaticHTML`'s own
default for that parameter is **`true`**, so deferring to M5 means shipping a
function whose third argument is named `sanitize` and does nothing. §1's
dependency policy is *"with the milestone that needs it"*, and this is the
milestone whose entry point has the parameter.

**Its tree is the largest in the workspace and that is recorded rather than
discovered later.** `ammonia → url → idna` brings the ICU4X data crates;
`html5ever → markup5ever → web_atoms` brings `phf` and `string_cache`. The
lockfile goes from 70 packages to **121**. §1's constraint is satisfied — no
I/O, no windowing, no GPU, and `cargo xtask deps` still reports 14 workspace
crates with four headless — but **§12.2's budget now understates the total**:
its "pulldown-cmark, serde, notify, pdf-writer, spellbook, misc ≈ 1.5 MB" line
does not have ICU in it. Two of the subtrees are already scheduled anyway (§4's
fourth row puts `html5ever` on M5's paste path, §12.2 budgets ICU segmentation
with `parley` at M3), so the marginal cost at v1 is smaller than the marginal
cost today — but the line should be re-measured at M3 rather than believed.

`mt_md::sanitize`'s module docs carry the mapping from `EXPORT_DOMPURIFY_CONFIG`
and the four rows where the two sanitizers cannot be made identical, **measured
against jsdom + DOMPurify** rather than read off the config object — which is
how `data-align` surviving while `data-style` does not turned out to be a fact
rather than a guess. Two of the four are divergences and both drop output
rather than admit it, which is the only direction a sanitizer may differ in.

### S5's delist — 31, and the two that did not are the prediction being wrong in the way it said it would be

**`PENDING` goes from 42 to 11, so S5 delists 31.**

| Spec file | Listed at S4 | Delisted at S5 | Still listed | Why the residue stays |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| `renderToStaticHTML` | 20 | 18 | 2 | KaTeX — see below |
| `listSerialization` | 9 | 0 | 9 | S4's reparse finding, owed to S6 |
| `footnoteHtml` | 6 | 6 | 0 | — |
| `softBreakExportHtml` | 3 | 3 | 0 | — |
| `strongCjkFlanking` | 3 | 3 | 0 | — |
| `markdownToState` | 1 | 1 | 0 | — |
| | **42** | **31** | **11** | |

**The brief predicted 9 and this stage predicted 11 before the run; 11 is the
answer.** The two extra are `renderToStaticHTML`'s
`honours_the_math_option` and `honours_gitlab_compatibility_for_math_fences`,
and they are the shape §6 warns about at every stage — *a case whose file is
labelled S5 and which is really another milestone's* — **one milestone further
out than §6's S0 correction found it**. S0's correction moved thirteen cases to
M6 for being `MarkdownToHtml`'s; these two are M6's for a different reason:
`getHighlightHtml` builds its math extension with `useKatexRender: true`, and
there is no TeX renderer in this workspace until `mt-math`, which §9 schedules
at M6 and whose crate doc says so. The port emits muya's *other* math renderer
— the one `lexBlock` uses, `<pre class="multiple-math">` — which is a real muya
shape and not this one.

They cost the conformance number nothing: `Options::SPEC` has `math: false`.

#### The shape that did **not** fire, for the first time in three stages

*"A case that starts passing in a file you did not touch."* None. And for the
first time the arithmetic against `PENDING` and the arithmetic against §7's
column agree exactly, in both directions — every file §7 marks S5 delisted at
S5, and nothing else moved. The reason is the same structural one S4 gave: by
S5 what was left was sorted by entry point rather than by file, and there was
only one entry point left to open.

#### The prediction, recorded before the run

Two numbers, and the conformance pair had never been predicted before:

| | Predicted | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| `PENDING` | **11** — the nine `listSerialization` names plus the two KaTeX cases | **11**, exactly those |
| CommonMark | ~74 % | **71.0 %** |
| GFM | ~73 % | **70.8 %** |

The reasoning behind the percentages was that blocks would hold up and §6.2 and
§6.3 would not; that was right, and the estimate was three points optimistic
because §6.3 *Links* came in at 62 % rather than the ~75 % the reasoning
assumed. The prediction is recorded because D5 makes the number permanent and
a prediction written **before** the run is the only thing that can show the
re-baseline was not chosen to fit the code.

### The traps the brief named, and which of them fired

| Trap | Fired? |
|---|---|
| The re-baseline is the one-way door, and blanket regeneration is what the ratchet exists to prevent | **Fired, and it is why the number is 71.0 rather than 70.6.** `--update` was run, the diff was read, and one class in it (`<p></p>` for the model's synthetic empty paragraph) was a *renderer* artefact rather than an engine limit. Six entries came off before the floor was frozen. A blanket regeneration would have made them permanent |
| The inherited list is a different engine's list and an overlap reads as a sanity check | **Not fired, and the numbers say why**: 71 of 78 and 83 of 90 overlap, which looks like an inclusion and is not one — 7 removals and 118 additions in the same diff. The constraint that bound was the **length**, exactly as D5 said |
| A renderer divergence has the serializer's blind spot: rule 3 needs a failing *differential* case, and `conformance` compares against the **spec** rather than against muya | **Fired, twice over.** `spec/divergences.json` still holds three `layer: "inline"` entries and gains none. Two deliberate disagreements with muya's HTML were found — the sanitizer's `style`/`<svg>` and the front-matter newline — and neither can be registered. §10 owes an HTML differential beside the serializer one |
| `sanitize: false` is not a shortcut and `sanitize: true` is not the safe default | **Not fired.** Both arms are implemented, both are transcribed, and the four option-surface cases all delist. The `Result` stays on the signature for `mt-cli --to-html` rather than being removed as the other three were |
| `renderToStaticHTML` is not `markdownToHtml` | **Not fired.** No heading slug, no `<article>` wrapper; `returns_the_bare_body_html_with_no_article_wrapper` and `renders_headings_with_id_less_h_tags` both pass, which is the pair that would have caught it |
| S4's floors are not yours to lower | **Not fired, and the footnote extension is what could have done it.** `blocks` 1344/1344 with 4,985 leaves, `diff` 22/22, `divergences` 3 over 27, and both `round_trip.rs` exception lists byte-exact at 22 and 348 |
| `serialize` must keep agreeing with `ExportMarkdown` byte for byte, and nothing will tell you if you break it | **Not fired, and it was re-taken rather than assumed.** 1346 of 1346, still |
| `normalize_html`'s two limitations are the gate | **Not fired.** Neither was touched, and 2648 of 2648 agree — which is what makes "the Rust one reproduces the TypeScript one, bugs included" a check rather than an intention |

**And the trap nobody named.** The first renderer emitted an HTML comment
**twice**, because `try_html_tag`'s comment branch sets `open_tag` to the whole
match and leaves `content` and `close_tag` as `None` — so a defensive "if this
is a comment with no close tag, emit the raw bytes" arm, written to avoid
losing data, duplicated it instead. CommonMark #625 and #626 were the only
things in the repository that said so. The general form is worth carrying:
**a fallback arm written for a case the code cannot reach is not free — it is
a second implementation of the same output with nothing comparing them.**

### S5 registers nothing either, and four stages is now the pattern rather than the observation

`spec/divergences.json` holds three entries, all `layer: "inline"`, and the
block layer still has none after five stages.

S2 predicted the first block entry would come from *"a stage that has an
opinion — S4's serializer or S5's renderer"*. Both had one; neither could
register. S4 found the structural reason and S5 found it again in a second
place, which is what turns it from an incident into a property:

**`cargo xtask conformance` compares against the *spec's* expected HTML, not
against muya's output.** So a deliberate disagreement with muya has no
differential case to point at — rule 3 reports the entry `Stale` and fails the
build — and the two S5 found are held by tests and by §10 instead:

- `mt_md::sanitize` drops `style="…"` and `<svg>` where DOMPurify keeps them.
  Both are the conservative direction, both are pinned by
  `style_and_svg_are_dropped_where_dompurify_keeps_them`.
- The rendered `<pre class="front-matter">` loses the body's trailing newline,
  because `Block::Frontmatter`'s text is trimmed and `frontMatterRender` uses
  the token's. §4 C2's normalisation, visible one byte at a time.

**An HTML differential is now owed beside the serializer one** (§10), and the
two are the same shape: `tools/diff/` would need a script that runs
`renderToStaticHTML` over the same `--inputs` file, which is the whole of the
work. `cargo xtask normalize` is *not* it — it compares two normalisers, not
two engines — but it is the first `xtask` runner that drives TypeScript living
in **this** repository rather than in the marktext clone, so it is the shape
that harness would take.

### S5's cost

**One dependency, and it is the largest tree in the workspace.** `ammonia`,
and through it 51 further packages: the lockfile goes from 70 to 121. The
arithmetic and what it costs §12.2's estimate are above, in decision 3;
`cargo xtask deps` still reports 14 workspace crates and four headless.

**Three new modules in `mt-md`** — `html` (852 lines plus 388 of tests),
`footnotes` (`transformFootnotes.ts`, 100 lines of TypeScript) and `sanitize`
(205, most of it the mapping table) — and **one new `xtask` runner**,
`normalize`, with its Node half in `tools/diff/normalize-ts-html.mjs`.

**One change to `block.rs`'s shape rather than to its rules.** `build` now
walks *regions* rather than the whole document, because a block extension that
consumes a span and hands the loop back its tail is exactly a split into
regions — which is `marked`'s own model, its block lexer having no cross-block
state except `tokens.links`. `parse_blocks` and `parse_blocks_with_ranges` are
unchanged, `Block`'s variant set is untouched, and 1344/1344 still holds.

**Two commands where there was one.** `mt-cli --to-html`, with `--sanitize`.

### S4's verification

`cargo test --workspace`, and the three `xtask` runners S4 must not move:

```text
  fixed point: 1324 of 1346 inputs (11 corpus files, 11 round-trip fixtures,
               1324 spec examples); 22 enumerated exceptions, all of them
               single spec examples
  identity:    998 of 1346 inputs round-trip byte-for-byte
```

| Clause | State | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `parse(serialize(parse(s))) == parse(s)` for every corpus file and every `marktext-round-trip` fixture | **met, with no exceptions** | All 22 whole documents. That is §9's exit-gate wording (*"round-trip is a fixed point on corpus + fixtures"*) exactly, and `round_trip.rs::no_fixed_point_exception_is_one_of_the_twenty_two_documents` makes it impossible to weaken by adding a name |
| …and all 1324 spec fixtures | **met on 1302; 22 enumerated** | Eleven distinct examples, each listed twice because CommonMark and GFM ship it under two numbers. Six named mechanisms, and **muya's own round trip is not a fixed point on any of them either** — asked directly, all eleven come back changed and five are still moving on the third pass |
| `serialize(parse(s)) == s` byte-for-byte, with the exception set enumerated | **met, 998 of 1346, 348 enumerated** | `IDENTITY_EXCEPTIONS`, grouped by source set with a per-group count. **Nine of the eleven `marktext-round-trip` fixtures are byte-identical**, which is the number that bears on §10's non-negotiable; all eleven corpus files differ and none is a counter-example, because `bench/corpus/` is generated to stress the layout path and has never been canonical markdown |
| The exception set is a ratchet rather than a report | **met** | Both lists implement `PENDING`'s four-row decision table: a listed input that starts round-tripping fails the build. That is the third instance of the shape `spec/README.md` asks to be kept |
| `mt_md::serialize` returns `Ok` | **met, and it returns no `Result` at all** | See "the two things S4 was asked to settle" below |
| `entry_points_report_unimplemented_at_m0` loses its `serialize` clause | **met** | One clause left, `render_to_static_html`'s, which is S5's. The test shrinks by one per stage by design |
| `meta.fenceLength` saturating at 255 (§10, owed by S1) | **met** | `Block::CodeBlock::fence_len` is `u32`. ``"`".repeat(300)`` round-trips; asked of muya, its `fenceLength` is 300 too. `block_names_match_typescript_union_one_to_one` did not have to change — checked rather than assumed, because widening a field is not adding a variant |
| The empty-task-marker lazy continuation (§10, owed by S3) | **met** | `Builder::fold_empty_task_marker_continuations`. All thirteen probes now agree; the two `PENDING` cases delist and `block::tests::an_empty_task_marker_does_not_yet_take_a_lazy_continuation` is flipped and renamed. Three neighbouring shapes are **not** reached and are pinned by a second test — see below |
| `cargo xtask blocks` still 1344/1344, 0/0/0, 4,985 leaves | **met** | Re-run after the task-marker fix, which is a block-layer change and therefore the one thing this stage could have moved |
| `cargo xtask diff --require-ts` still 22/22 | **met** | `22 passed, 0 failed, 0 registered divergence(s), 0 skipped` |
| `cargo xtask divergences` still 3 inline entries `ok` over 27 of 27 | **met** | Plus the 4,237-input sweep clean |
| `cargo xtask conformance` still skips 1,324; `every_suite_is_skipped_at_m0` still passes | **met** | `render_to_static_html` is untouched. S5's one-way door is still shut |
| `PENDING` shrinks | **met, by 79** | 121 → 42 |
| `cargo fmt --all --check`, `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, `xtask deps`, `xtask ci --require-ts`, `cargo test --workspace` | **met locally** | All green on toolchain 1.97.1. **773 passed, 0 failed, 2 ignored**, against S3's 738. `deps` still reports 14 crates |
| **And green in CI** | **MET, on the first dispatch** | Run [30892592758](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/30892592758) — `success` on **linux, windows and macos**, 20 steps each. That is three stages in a row where the first dispatch was green, and S2 said that should be the standard rather than the achievement. What it buys here specifically: `cargo test --workspace` now carries the round-trip gate, so `serialize(parse(s)) == s` is asserted on **LF checkouts** (linux and macos) as well as on the CRLF one this machine has — which is the half of `normalize_source` a Windows-only run cannot distinguish from doing nothing |

### The measurement neither clause asked for, and it is the strongest one

**The port's serialization of all 1346 inputs is byte-identical to
`ExportMarkdown.generate`'s** — every corpus file including `5mb.md`, every
`marktext-round-trip` fixture, and all 1324 spec examples, with zero throws on
either side.

That was not in the gate and it is what makes the gate's two exception lists
readable. Both lists say *"the round trip is not the identity here"*, and
without this measurement the natural reading is *"the port loses something
here"*. With it, the reading is fixed: **the 348 are muya's canonicalisations
and the 22 are muya's own non-fixed-points**, and shrinking either would mean
deciding to diverge from the engine this milestone is porting.

It also validates the two pieces of this stage that no other check reaches.
`stringWidth` is exercised by `cjk.md`, `rtl.md`, `emoji.md` and
`20-tables.md`, and a one-column error in it is a byte difference in every
padded table; `escapeText`'s lookbehind is exercised by every `\|` in
`gfm/Tables.md`. Both come out identical.

**It is a one-off measurement and it is recorded as one.** There is no
committed runner for it — see "S4 finds the register's blind spot" below, which
is the same fact from the other side.

### The two things S4 was asked to settle before any code

#### 1 — `serialize` is infallible, and the specs decided it rather than the argument

The ground S3 gave for `parse` was *"a `Result` whose `Err` arm no input can
reach is the shape this milestone distrusts"*, and the brief was right that it
does not transfer unexamined: `serialize` takes a `&Document`, and a
`Document` can be hand-built with a shape `MarkdownToState` cannot express.

So the question is not *"can it fail"* but *"what does muya do"*, and
`stateToMarkdown.spec.ts` asks it twice — a body row with **more** cells than
the header, and one with **fewer** — and asserts both times that muya does not
crash. It does not: the long row is truncated against the header's column count
and the short one is emitted short. **Degraded output, not an error.** The port
reproduces the degradation and `serialize` returns a `String`.

Three shapes remain for which muya's answer is a JavaScript `TypeError`, and
all three are documents `parse` cannot build: a `table` with no rows, a
`list-item` with no enclosing list, and a `table.row` at top level. The third
is already muya's own "warn and emit nothing" arm; the first two are reproduced
*as* the third. A crash is not a behaviour worth porting, and the alternative —
an `Err` arm reachable only from a document no parse produces — is the shape
this crate has now twice decided against.
`serialize::tests::the_three_shapes_that_throw_in_javascript_emit_nothing_here`
pins all three, so the decision is checkable rather than merely written down.

**What `mt-cli` would do with an error is therefore not a question**, and that
is the useful part of the answer: there is no exit code for *"your document was
strange"*, because there is no document for which this function declines to
answer. `ExitCode::UNIMPLEMENTED` stays defined for S5's `--to-html`.

#### 2 — the round-trip property is an integration test in `mt-md`, and CI runs it as `cargo test`

Three shapes were possible and §1 rules out the first: **`mt-md` may not do
I/O**, machine-checked by `cargo xtask deps` over `src/`, which is what fired on
S3 when a range test read the corpus. So the property cannot live beside the
code it is about.

That left an integration test here or an `xtask roundtrip` runner beside
`blocks`, and it is the test, for a reason that is about what `xtask` is rather
than about taste: **every runner in `xtask` exists to drive the TypeScript
engine.** `diff`, `blocks`, `divergences` and `conformance` are differential or
ratcheted against something outside the workspace, which is why each needs a
Node process, a clone of the reference engine and a `--require-ts`. The
round-trip property is a claim about the Rust engine **alone** — no second
engine, no subprocess — and an `xtask` runner for it would shell out to nothing.

**What CI runs is `cargo test --workspace`**, which already runs on three
platforms, and `crates/mt-md/tests/round_trip.rs` joined it with no wiring.
`cargo xtask ci` is unchanged and still does not know about it, which is
correct: it is the differential chain.

**The generators are S7's and the property over fixed corpora is S4's**, which
is what §11.3 and §6's S7 row already say. The distinction matters for the
second half of the row: a property over *fixed* inputs can be stated as a
ratchet because its inputs do not change between runs, and a proptest cannot.

**The exception list is a ratchet, not a report** — the second half of the row
answered explicitly. Both lists implement `PENDING`'s four rows, including the
one that makes it a floor: a listed input that starts round-tripping
byte-for-byte **fails the build**. A list that can grow silently is what §8's
risk table is about, and *"the round trip is mostly the identity"* is the
sentence this file exists to make unwriteable.

### S4's delist — 79, and the nine that did not are the finding

**`PENDING` goes from 121 to 42, so S4 delists 79.** The brief predicted a
computable floor of 33 and *"every one of the other 88 either passes at the end
of S4 or is a case you can name"*. Both halves hold: 79 of the 88 pass, and the
other nine are named below and in §10.

| Spec file | Listed at S3 | Delisted at S4 | Still listed | Why the residue stays |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| `listSerialization` | 28 | 19 | 9 | the reparse finding below |
| `renderToStaticHTML` | 20 | 0 | 20 | `render_to_static_html` |
| `blockSerialization` | 18 | 18 | 0 | — |
| `stateToMarkdown` | 11 | 11 | 0 | — |
| `gitlabMath` | 7 | 7 | 0 | — |
| `listMarkerOption` | 6 | 6 | 0 | — |
| `footnoteHtml` | 6 | 0 | 6 | `render_to_static_html` |
| `markdownToState` | 5 | 4 | 1 | the footnote block extension |
| `codeFenceInfoString` | 4 | 4 | 0 | — |
| `strongCjkFlanking` | 3 | 0 | 3 | `render_to_static_html` |
| `softBreakExportHtml` | 3 | 0 | 3 | `render_to_static_html` |
| `codeFenceLength` | 3 | 3 | 0 | — |
| `tableEscapedPipe` | 2 | 2 | 0 | — |
| `nestedMixedLists` | 2 | 2 | 0 | — |
| `diagramFlowchartSequence` | 2 | 2 | 0 | — |
| `referenceLink` | 1 | 1 | 0 | — |
| | **121** | **79** | **42** | |

**The floor was 33 and the answer is 42**, and the nine-case gap is one
mechanism rather than nine.

#### The shape the brief named, and it is the one that fired

*"A case labelled S4 that needs something else — `listSerialization` has 28
entries and five of them vary `listIndentation`, which is an option nothing has
ever read."* All five of those cases are in the nine, and `listIndentation` is
not why. It is read, correctly, and the five fixtures fail on a **reparse**.

The mechanism, measured from both sides:

```text
"3. foo\n   20. foo\n       141. foo\n"
  muya      order-list › item › order-list(start 20) › item › order-list(start 141)
  the port  order-list › item › paragraph "foo\n20. foo\n141. foo"

"- a\n  * \n  * \n"
  muya      bullet-list › item › paragraph "a" + bullet-list(*) of two empty items
  the port  bullet-list › item › paragraph "a\n* \n*"
```

**CommonMark will not let an ordered marker that is not `1`, or an empty list
item, interrupt a paragraph. `marked` has no such rule inside a list item at
all** — `Tokenizer.list` re-lexes an item's dedented content with
`state.top = false`, where `text` matches **one line**, so every line is its own
token and a `list` can start at any of them. The interruption rules live in
`marked`'s top-level `paragraph` regex and nowhere else, which is why
`cargo xtask blocks` is 1344/1344: the 1324 spec examples exercise the rule at
top level, where the two engines agree.

**Why it took `serialize` to surface it.** The serializer emits exactly what
muya emits for all nine — that is the byte-identity measurement above — and the
round trip then reads it back differently. Neither `blocks` nor `diff` compares
a reparse, and no fixture in either set has a non-`1` nested ordered list or an
empty nested bullet after a paragraph line. So the disagreement was reachable
only by the transcribed spec suite, and only once there was a serializer to
produce the markdown that reaches it. That is S3's "third shape" again, one
denominator further out.

**Not fixed here, and the reason is the same one S3 gave for not fixing the
task marker in passing.** It is a change to how *every* list item's children are
built — S1's layer — and the shape of the fix is a re-lex rather than a patch.
It is §10's "Owed by S4", with reproducers, and nine `PENDING` entries hold it.

#### The other shape, and it did not fire this time

*"A case that starts passing in a file you did not touch."* None. Every one of
the 79 is in a file whose cases needed `serialize`, and the arithmetic against
`PENDING` agreed with the arithmetic against the files for the first time in
this milestone. The reason is structural rather than lucky: S3 had already
absorbed the cross-file wave when one entry point opened, so what was left was
sorted by entry point rather than by file.

### The traps the brief named, and which of them fired

| Trap | Fired? |
|---|---|
| `escapeText` is a **lookbehind**, and the spelling in the brief is not it | **Yes, and the brief's spelling was `/(?<!\|)\|/g` where the source is `/(?<!\\)\|/g`** — not preceded by a *backslash*, not by a pipe. Read rather than reproduced from the brief, which is the method S2 recorded. The consequence the brief did name is real and is tested: `a\|b` is not double-escaped, and `a||b` escapes **both** pipes, because JavaScript tests the lookbehind against the *original* string where the second pipe's predecessor is a pipe |
| `stringWidth` is muya's own 60 lines and the ranges do not match `unicode-width` | **Yes, and the file is ported.** Fifteen inlined ranges transcribed in source order; the `\p{Mn}\|\p{Me}` class is the one thing not inlined, because hand-writing a few hundred category ranges is a table that drifts silently. `fancy-regex` was already in the workspace graph via `mt-inline`, so `cargo xtask deps` still reports 14 crates |
| CRLF, again, and it is your harness rather than your code | **Yes, and it was fixed by deletion rather than by a fourth spelling.** `mt-cli::read_markdown` moved into `mt_md::normalize_source`, a pure `&str → String`; `mt-cli` calls it, the round-trip test calls it, and `parse` deliberately does not. `parse("a\r\nb\r\n")` is asserted to **keep** the carriage returns, because that is what muya does |
| The fixed point is not the identity, and the gate asks for both | **Yes, and they came out at different numbers**: 1324 of 1346 and 998 of 1346. `round_trip.rs::a_fixed_point_that_is_not_the_identity_is_still_a_fixed_point` is the difference as a test rather than as a sentence |
| `spec/divergences.json` still has no `layer: "block"` entry, and a serializer has an opinion | **No entry, and S4 found out why it could not have one.** See below |
| S2's and S3's floors are not yours to lower | **Not fired, and it was the one thing that could have been.** The task-marker fix is a block-layer change, so `blocks` and `diff` were both re-run after it: 1344/1344, 4,985 leaves, 22/22 |
| Do not let `cargo xtask conformance` start enforcing | **Not fired.** `render_to_static_html` untouched, 1,324 still skipped |

**And the trap nobody named, which cost the most.** The first `serialize` was
written from `stateToMarkdown.ts` and passed 112 of its own unit tests on the
first run — and then `- [ ] \n\ntext` regressed, because the fold's *"is there a
blank line between these two blocks"* test measured from a range end that may or
may not include the newline that ends it. One byte, in the direction that
silently makes a boundary case agree for the wrong reason. The general form is
worth carrying: **`pulldown-cmark`'s ranges are not consistent about their
trailing newline, so any "are these adjacent" question has to be asked from the
last content byte** — `trim_trailing_blanks` exists for exactly that and is used
twice.

### S4 finds the register's blind spot, and it is a finding rather than an entry

`spec/divergences.json` still holds three entries, all `layer: "inline"`. S2
predicted the first block entry would come from *"a stage that has an opinion —
S4's serializer or S5's renderer"*, and S4 has one and still registers nothing.

**The reason is not that there was nothing to register. It is that a serializer
divergence cannot satisfy rule 3.** An entry is stale, and fails the build,
unless a *differential* case disagrees — and no harness in this repository
compares serialized markdown. `cargo xtask diff` and `cargo xtask blocks` both
compare the state tree, which is `parse`'s output; `cargo xtask divergences`
compares token streams. So even a deliberate serializer divergence would have
nowhere to be registered, and the twenty-two fixed-point exceptions — every one
of which the port reproduces byte-for-byte from muya — could not be entries
even if the port had chosen to disagree.

That gap is why the byte-identity measurement above is recorded as a
measurement: it is the evidence a permanent runner would have produced, taken
once, by hand. **A committed serializer differential is the obvious next
harness and it is owed forward rather than built here** (§10), for the reason
S1 gave about `parse_blocks`: the stage that needs a harness is the stage that
should build it, and the stage that needs this one is whichever stage first
wants to *disagree* with `ExportMarkdown`.

### S4's cost

**One dependency edge, no new crates.** `fancy-regex` becomes a dependency of
`mt-md`, for one pattern: `/\p{Mn}|\p{Me}/u` in `string_width.rs`. It adds
nothing to the graph — `mt-md` already depends on `mt-inline`, whose own
dependency it is — and `cargo xtask deps` reports 14 crates before and after.

**One change to `mt-doc`'s public API**, which is why §10 held it for this
stage: `Block::CodeBlock::fence_len` and `BlockMeta::CodeBlock::fence_len` widen
from `Option<u8>` to `Option<u32>`. `Block`'s variant set is untouched and
`block_names_match_typescript_union_one_to_one` did not have to change.

**One function moved rather than added.** `mt-cli::read_markdown`'s body is now
`mt_md::normalize_source`; the I/O stayed in `mt-cli`.

**Two new modules in `mt-md`:** `serialize` (626 lines of TypeScript, plus its
tests) and `string_width` (60 lines, plus its tests).

### S3's verification

`cargo xtask diff --require-ts`, over the 22 corpus documents:

```text
  corpus: 22 file(s)
  register: 0 block entries, 0 tolerated input(s); 3 inline entries are
            `cargo xtask divergences`'s

  22 passed, 0 failed, 0 registered divergence(s), 0 skipped
```

| Clause | State | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `cargo xtask diff` reports PASS or a registered divergence for all 22 files | **met** | 22 PASS, 0 registered. It took two runs: the first was **11 failed** and the finding is below |
| `cargo xtask ci` no longer prints `diff 22 skipped` | **met** | `cargo xtask ci --require-ts` exits 0 with `22 passed, 0 failed, 0 registered divergence(s), 0 skipped` |
| The two files nothing had ever compared | **met, and measured** | `1mb.md` and `5mb.md` PASS. `mt-cli --dump-state` on `5mb.md` is **451 ms release / 1.13 s debug**, `1mb.md` 119 ms / 351 ms — about 11 MB/s in release including the JSON serialization and the write. `strip_lines` does allocate a `String` per source line and that is what the number already includes, so it is a note for S6/S7 rather than a reason to skip the files |
| `cargo xtask blocks` still 1344/1344, 0/0/0, 4,985 leaves | **met** | S2's two floors are untouched; nothing S3 did reaches the mapping layer's decisions |
| `cargo xtask divergences` still 3 inline entries `ok` over 27 of 27 | **met** | Plus the 4,237-input sweep clean |
| `cargo xtask conformance` still skips 1,324; `every_suite_is_skipped_at_m0` still passes | **met** | `render_to_static_html` is untouched, which is what D6's staging is for |
| `mt_md::serialize` and `render_to_static_html` still `Err(Unimplemented)` | **met** | `entry_points_report_unimplemented_at_m0`, which now asserts both |
| The three deletions | **met** | See the correction above for what "delete" turned out to mean in two of the three |
| `PENDING` shrinks | **met, by 63** | 184 → 121, and three of the 121 now fail for a reason of their own rather than for a closed door — enumerated by a test, see below |
| `cargo fmt --all --check`, `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, `xtask deps`, `xtask ci --require-ts`, `cargo test --workspace` | **met locally** | All green on toolchain 1.97.1. **738 passed, 0 failed, 2 ignored**, against S2's 713 |
| **And green in CI** | **MET** | Run [30887986221](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/30887986221) — `success` on **linux, windows and macos**, 20 steps each, on the first dispatch. That is now two stages running, and S2 said it should be the standard rather than the achievement. What it buys here specifically: the whole `xtask ci --require-ts` chain with `cargo xtask diff` **enforcing** rather than skipping, on a runner whose stable toolchain moves ahead of this machine — and on macOS and Linux, whose checkouts have **LF** line endings, so the CRLF fix is verified as a no-op there rather than only as a fix here |

### The two things S3 was asked to settle before any code

#### 1 — what `parse` returns: a `Parsed`, and the ranges live beside the document rather than in it

§10's corrected owed item falls due here. The constraint is hard: `Block` maps
1:1 onto the TypeScript `TState` union,
`block_names_match_typescript_union_one_to_one` is the machine-checked form of
it, and the §11.2 harness depends on it — so a range cannot live on a `Block`.
The three candidates were a side table on `Document`, a second return value, or
nothing again.

**Decided: `parse` returns `mt_md::Parsed { document, labels, source_map }`,
and it is infallible.**

The argument against the side table is the one that decided it, and it is not
about where the field would fit. **A `Document` outlives its parse and no
`Edit` maintains a range.** Hang the map on the document and the first
`SpliceText` makes it lie, silently, with no mechanism that could ever have
kept it true — which is a worse thing to ship than no map at all. Ranges are a
fact about one parse; the return value of the parse is where a fact about the
parse belongs.

The argument against a second entry point ("`parse`, and `parse_with_ranges`
for callers who care") is that it forks the only parse path in the crate so
that the interesting half can be forgotten. `Parsed` costs a caller
`.document` and nothing else.

**And the risk of taking it is the one this milestone keeps recording:** an API
with no caller until M4 is not a check. So the map is checked rather than
merely shipped, in three places —
`tests/source_ranges.rs::every_node_has_a_range_and_the_ranges_nest` walks
every corpus document at both option sets and asserts that every live node
carries a range, that a child's range is inside its parent's, that siblings are
in document order, and that every range is a valid slice;
`the_map_has_exactly_one_entry_per_live_node` pins completeness; and
`block::tests::the_stripper_is_re_runnable_from_src_tree_and_ranges` **runs
S2's argument**. That last one is the point of the whole decision: S2 claimed
the container half of §10's reverse map is not lost, *"because the prefixes are
computable from the ancestors' source ranges"*, and until S3 that was an
argument. It now rebuilds a block quote's `Prefix` from the quote's range
alone and re-derives the inner paragraph's text with `strip_lines`.

What the map does **not** claim is stated on the type: the per-kind half is not
free from it and never will be. An atx heading is rebuilt from scratch, a code
block loses columns after stripping, a table cell is trimmed and unescaped;
each needs its own inverse and each belongs beside its rule.

**One consequence for M4, recorded now because it is cheap now and expensive
later:** the ranges index the string `parse` was given. `mt-cli` normalises
CRLF before calling it (see the traps below), so a range from a file read
through the file layer indexes the *normalised* text, not the bytes on disk.
That is muya's shape too — its file layer normalises and its parser does not —
but a caret that maps back to a file offset has to know it.

#### 2 — where the label map lives: in `mt-md`, run once per parse, typed as `mt_inline::Labels`

muya rebuilds it inside `patch()` — a full depth-first traversal of the
document's state on **every block render**, which is a keystroke. Noticed, not
copied: `parse` builds it once and `mt_md::labels::collect` is public, so an
editor that has just changed a definition can redo it deliberately rather than
on every frame. That is the whole of the difference and it costs nothing to
take.

**The dependency edge is real and it is taken: `mt-inline` stops being a
dev-dependency of `mt-md` and becomes a dependency.** What decided it was not
the `Labels` type but the *regex*.
`beginRules.reference_definition` is already transcribed in `mt-inline`, with
its `\9` backreference and its backtrack limit, because the tokenizer needs it
as a begin rule. The alternative — a `Labels` owned by `mt-md` and converted at
the boundary — buys nothing and costs a **second transcription of that rule**,
which is the "two scanners" mistake §5 D3 spends a section on, in the one
costume where the two really would have to agree.

**What it costs `cargo xtask deps`: nothing measurable, and §1 still holds.**
The runner reports **14 crates** before and after, because `mt-inline` was
already in the workspace graph and the edge is downward —
`mt-doc → mt-inline → mt-md → mt-layout` is §1's own order, and `mt-md`'s crate
doc has said *"on `mt-inline` once inline re-derivation lands"* since M0. §1's
purity constraint is about `src/`, and `mt-inline` is the crate the plan calls
pure. The guard did fire once during this stage, for an unrelated reason and
correctly: a range invariant test that read the corpus was written in
`src/block/tests.rs`, and `deps` failed the build with *"mt-md does no I/O …
move it to an integration test"*. It moved.

**The pass is `getLabelInfo`, not the tokenizer, and the difference is one
line.** `consumeBeginRules` gates the `reference_definition` *token* on
`isLengthEven(def[3])` — an odd run of backslashes escapes the `]` — and
`getLabelInfo` reads the raw capture groups with no such gate. So `[a\]: /x`
produces no token and does define the label `a\`. `mt_inline::label_info` is
the new public function that runs the rule the way muya's label pass runs it,
and every expectation in its tests was measured against the running engine.
Three of them are not what reading the pattern suggests: a parenthesised title
defeats the **whole** rule (`[a]: /u (t)` matches nothing, because `\9` closes
the title with whatever opened it), `^`/`$` are not multi-line so a paragraph
holding a definition *and* anything else defines nothing, and trailing spaces
after a bare href leave muya's `title` as a single space.

### S3's delist — the prediction was wrong in a third way, and that is the informative one

The brief named two shapes to expect and both appeared:

- **A case whose file is labelled S1 or S2 but which asserts a round trip stays
  listed.** This is most of the residue. `listSerialization` delists 2 of 30,
  `blockSerialization` 1 of 19, `gitlabMath` 11 of 16 — the five that stay are
  its `serializes…` and `round_trips…` halves.
- **A case whose file is labelled S4 but which only needs `parse` starts
  passing.** `listSerialization::parses_the_start_number_into_order_list_meta_start`
  and `list_meta_loose_carries_the_prefer_loose_list_item_flag_verbatim` are
  exactly that, and `blockSerialization::parses_a_four_space_indented_block_as_an_indented_code_block`
  is a third.

**The third shape is a case whose file is labelled S1/S2, which needs only
`parse`, and which still fails** — a block-layer disagreement that neither S1's
gate nor S2's could see, because the input is in neither harness's set. There
are three, and they are enumerated by a test rather than left in a pile of 121:
`the_only_listed_cases_that_fail_for_their_own_reason_are_the_three_m2_names`
drives every listed case and asserts that the set of ones failing for a reason
other than a closed entry point is exactly the three §10 now names. A fourth
appearing means a stage introduced a disagreement; one disappearing means a
stage fixed one without saying so.

That test is the thing that replaces
`every_pending_case_fails_because_the_engine_is_unimplemented`, and it replaces
it rather than merely deleting it for the reason that test existed at all: at
S0 there was exactly one legitimate way to fail, so "listed and failing" was
checkable. From S3 there are two, and a list of 121 cannot tell them apart.

**The counts, since §6 asks for the arithmetic rather than the claim:**

| Spec file | Cases | Delisted at S3 | Still listed | Why the residue stays |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| `markdownToState` | 32 | 27 | 5 | 2 round trips, 1 footnote block, 2 the task-marker disagreement |
| `gitlabMath` | 16 | 9 | 7 | `serialize` |
| `diagramFlowchartSequence` | 5 | 3 | 2 | `serialize` |
| `infoStringModel` | 3 | 3 | 0 | — |
| `mathTrailingSpace` | 3 | 3 | 0 | — |
| `gitlabMathToggle` | 4 | 4 | 0 | — |
| `nestedMixedLists` | 4 | 2 | 2 | `serialize` |
| `referenceLink` | 8 | 7 | 1 | `serialize` |
| `tableEscapedPipe` | 4 | 2 | 2 | `serialize` |
| `listSerialization` | 30 | 2 | 28 | `serialize` |
| `blockSerialization` | 19 | 1 | 18 | `serialize` |
| `codeFenceInfoString`, `codeFenceLength`, `listMarkerOption`, `stateToMarkdown` | 24 | 0 | 24 | `serialize` |
| `renderToStaticHTML`, `footnoteHtml`, `softBreakExportHtml`, `strongCjkFlanking`'s static half | 32 | 0 | 32 | `render_to_static_html` |
| | **184** | **63** | **121** | |

**And one of the 63 is a transcription that was asserting the opposite of the
engine.** `referenceLink`'s case 7 had a second half the TypeScript original
does not have: that a duplicate definition's *second* line survives a round
trip, *"which is the half `RefDefs` cannot deliver at all"*. Asked of the
running engine, muya emits **one** paragraph for `[dup]: /a\n[dup]: /b` and
drops the second — which is exactly what §5 D3's own correction records, and
this file was written before it. S0 transcribed D3's original text. The half
that survives is the case the original makes, the case now passes, and it is
delisted. That is the eighth instance of the milestone's recurring failure and
the first one where the transcription was the place it hid.

### The traps the brief named, and which of them fired

| Trap | Fired? |
|---|---|
| Two reference-definition scanners, and they must stay two | **Yes, and the disagreement is now written down as a table.** `mt_md::labels`' module docs put the two side by side: `toLowerCase()` + `/\s+/g` versus `toLowerCase()`, and no-block-at-all versus last-write-wins. Both halves are tested — `[foo bar]`/`[foo  bar]` is one label to the block layer and two to the label pass |
| `cargo xtask diff` runs 6 MB nothing had compared; measure it | **Yes, and it is fine.** 451 ms release for `5mb.md`. Recorded above rather than assumed |
| Different denominators: a green `blocks` and a red `diff` is not a contradiction | **Yes, exactly this, and `first_difference`'s JSON path is why D6 put `diff` first.** The first run was 11 of 22 red with `blocks` still 1344/1344 — see below |
| `mt-cli --dump-state`'s exit 3 stops happening; do not delete the path | **Yes, and the two meanings are different sites.** `dump_ts_states`'s exit-3 arm is *the TypeScript engine is unavailable* and is what `--require-ts` guards; it is untouched and still live. `dump_rs_state`'s `Ok(None)` arm is *the Rust engine is unimplemented* and is now unreachable from `--dump-state`; it stays, because S5's `--to-html` is its next user and `ExitCode::UNIMPLEMENTED` stays defined for it |
| `Options::footnote` does nothing, and §10 owes S3 the characterization | **Yes — and the label map is where it became a question, exactly as the brief said.** Answered below |
| S2's floors are not yours to lower | **Not fired.** 1344/1344 and 4,985 unchanged; nothing S3 did reaches a mapping decision |
| `spec/divergences.json` still has no `layer: "block"` entry; be suspicious if one appears | **Yes, and none appears.** See below |

**And the trap nobody named, which is the one that fired hardest.** The first
`cargo xtask diff --require-ts` after `dump_state` returned `Ok` was **11
passed, 11 failed**, and every failure was one byte:

```text
  FAIL  spec/fixtures/marktext-round-trip/common/Headings.md
        first difference at [10].text
        typescript: "bar"
        rust:       "bar\r"
```

The eleven `marktext-round-trip` fixtures are stored `text=auto eol=lf` and
check out **CRLF** on Windows. `tools/diff/dump-ts-state.mjs`'s `readMarkdown`
strips a BOM and normalises `\r\n?` to `\n` before the TypeScript engine sees a
file — and its M0 doc comment already said *"The Rust side must do the same,
and `mt-fs` owns that in the real application."* **Nothing had ever checked that
it did**, and nothing could: `cargo xtask blocks` normalises on its own side
before handing one string to both engines (`blocks.rs` says so in a comment),
so for two stages the port's file layer was never exercised. `cargo xtask diff`
hands each side a **path**, which is why the first `Ok` from `dump_state` was
also the first time the question was asked. §0's rule, in a new place.

**Where the fix goes is a measurement rather than a guess, and the obvious
answer is wrong.** `marked`'s `Lexer.lex` does
`src.replace(/\r\n|\r/g, '\n')`, which reads as though the *parser* normalises
and `mt_md::parse` should too. **`lexBlock` does not call `lex`.** It calls
`new m.Lexer(m.defaults).blockTokens(src)` directly, skipping the
preprocessing step — so muya's parser sees carriage returns raw, and asked
directly it proves it:

```text
"a\r\nb\r\n"        → ONE paragraph, text "a\r\nb\r"
"# h\r\n\r\npara\r\n" → ONE paragraph, text "# h\r\n\r\npara\r"
"﻿# h\n"       → ONE paragraph, text "﻿# h"
```

So the normalisation is the **file layer's**, and putting it in `mt_md::parse`
would have made the port disagree with the reference on any string a caller
hands it directly. `mt-cli::read_markdown` is `readMarkdown` transcribed, both
halves are tested separately, and `mt_md::parse` is asserted to keep the
carriage returns — because that is what muya does. Reading `marked` rather than
probing it would have got this exactly backwards; reading it *and* probing
muya is what got it right, which is S2's method restated.

### S3 registers nothing either, and three stages is now the answer rather than the observation

`spec/divergences.json` still holds three entries, all `layer: "inline"`, and
the block layer is still wired, still filtered and still never fired.

S2 named the pattern: the mapping layer sits between a structure decider and a
text decider and has no opinion of its own. S3 tests it from the other side and
it holds — `diff` compares the same serializer over the same tree that `blocks`
compares, so it was never going to produce an opinion either. The eleven
failures it did produce were a **file-layer** bug, which is not a divergence in
any sense: both engines want the same bytes and one of them was not getting
them.

**S3 did find a disagreement that would have been registrable if it were
reachable**, and rule 3 is what stops it: the empty-task-marker lazy
continuation (§10, "Owed by S3") is a place `marked` and GFM genuinely differ
and the port follows GFM. No input in `cargo xtask diff`'s 22 or
`cargo xtask blocks`' 1344 reaches it, so an entry would report **stale** and
fail the build. It is held by two `PENDING` entries, a characterization test in
`block/tests.rs`, and §10 — which is the same disposition §10's three S1
narrowings got, for the same reason.

The tolerance is still exercised directly, by
`blocks::tests::a_registered_block_divergence_is_tolerated_and_an_unregistered_one_is_not`,
unchanged since S1.

### S3's cost

**One dependency edge, no new crates.** `mt-inline` moves from
`[dev-dependencies]` to `[dependencies]` in `mt-md`; `serde_json` becomes a
dev-dependency of `mt-cli`, so that the CLI's test asserts the *shape* of what
`--dump-state` prints rather than a substring of it. `cargo xtask deps` reports
14 crates, unchanged.

**No change to `mt-doc`.** `Block`'s variant set, its fields and `BlockMeta` are
untouched, and `block_names_match_typescript_union_one_to_one` did not have to
change. The source ranges are the reason that matters: the constraint that a
`Block` cannot grow a field is what pushed them onto `Parsed`, and the
1:1 mapping is still exactly what it was at M0.

**Two entry points became infallible.** `parse` returns `Parsed` and
`dump_state` returns `String`, rather than `Result<_, Unimplemented>`. A
`Result` whose `Err` arm no input can reach is the shape this milestone keeps
learning to distrust, and leaving one in place would have meant `mt-cli`
carrying a branch that could never run. `serialize` and `render_to_static_html`
keep theirs, because for them it is still the truth.

**One public function added to `mt-inline`:** `label_info`. It is
`getLabelInfo`, and it lives there rather than in `mt-md` because the rule it
runs lives there.

### S2's verification

`cargo xtask blocks`, over §4 C1's 1344 inputs:

```text
  inputs: 1344 (1324 spec examples at SPEC, 20 documents at MUYA_DEFAULT)
  comparing the full TState JSON: names, meta and leaf text
  register: 0 block entries, 0 tolerated input(s)

  1344/1344 agree, 0 registered, 0 name, 0 meta, 0 leaf-text disagreement(s), 0 engine throw(s)
  4985 leaves compared across 1344 inputs; 0 differ in text, which is in the comparison above
  names: 1344/1344 — §4 C1 measured 1167/1344 before any mapping
```

| Clause | State | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Zero unregistered leaf-text disagreements over all 1344 | **met** | 0 of 4,985 leaves, from a starting point of 4,954 of 4,985. The run compares text by default, so this is the number `cargo xtask ci` enforces rather than a number someone remembered to ask for |
| D2's rules and the container-prefix stripper, written once in `mt_md::block` | **met** | `strip_lines` plus the per-kind rules beside it. `content_start` is **deleted**, not kept beside them — see below |
| Tested against muya directly, which S2's gate "is exactly" | **met** | The 1344-input gate, plus 22 new unit tests in `block/tests.rs`, one per rule, each carrying the input it was measured on |
| The `--with-text` question | **answered: it inverts** | See the correction above |
| Whether the 1344 floor should be raised | **answered: raised, and a second floor added** | The names floor goes from C1's 1167 to the measured **1344**, in the shape D5 gives for the conformance floor. A **leaf-count floor of 4,985** is new and is the one that was actually missing: it is counted on the TypeScript side, so a runner that quietly stopped comparing — or a reference engine that stopped answering — fails instead of printing `1344/1344 agree` over nothing |
| `trimUnnecessaryCodeBlockEmptyLines` (#1265) | **implemented, with the reason it could not be measured by the gate** | `false` in both option sets, so no fixture reaches it. The transcription is asserted against values measured from the running engine with the flag flipped, in the one test that exists for it |
| `spec/divergences.json` gains its first `layer: "block"` entry | **no — and see below** | 3 entries, all inline, 27 of 27 registered inputs disagreeing |
| `mt_md::parse` still returns `Err(Unimplemented)`; all three harnesses still skip | **met** | `entry_points_report_unimplemented_at_m0` unchanged; `diff` 22 SKIP, `conformance` 1,324 Skipped |
| `PENDING` is still 184 | **met, and predicted** | See "S2 delists nothing" below |
| `cargo fmt --all --check`, `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, `xtask deps`, `xtask ci --require-ts`, `cargo test --workspace` | **met locally** | All green on toolchain 1.97.1. **713 passed, 0 failed, 2 ignored**, against S1's 694 |
| **And green in CI** | **MET, on the first attempt** | Run [30878621470](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/30878621470) — `success` on **linux, windows and macos**, 20 steps each, including `cargo clippy -D warnings` on a runner whose stable toolchain moves ahead of this machine's, `cargo test`, and the whole `xtask ci --require-ts` chain with `cargo xtask blocks` now comparing leaf text by default. S0 shipped this clause unmet and said so; S1 needed two dispatches and a visibility change. S2 is the first stage where it was simply green |

### The two things S2 was asked to settle before any code

#### 1 — the stripper subsumes `content_start` and `LogicalText`, and both are re-based on it

The brief's warning was that promoting S1's two detection helpers is the obvious
move and is wrong. It is, and the reason is sharper than "they are approximate":
**they are approximate in the direction that deletes content.** `content_start`
strips *all* leading whitespace and *every* `>` on a line. A reference
definition's own ` {0,3}` indent is part of `Tokenizer.def`'s `cap[0]` and
therefore part of the paragraph muya emits — so `  [foo]: /a` has text
`"  [foo]: /a"`, and the helper that was fine for *finding* the `[` produces the
wrong text by two characters. The same for `> > a` at depth one, where the
second `>` is content.

**They are deleted rather than kept beside the real stripper**, and
`LogicalText` is rebuilt on `strip_lines`. The alternative the brief warns about
— two almost-identical scanners — was never the choice; the choice was between
deleting them and *keeping* them, and keeping them would have meant the def scan
and the leaf text disagreeing about where a line's content starts, in a codebase
where those two answers must be the same or the definition's paragraph has a
range that does not match its own text.

What the detection callers lose by using the exact answer: nothing. Both of them
— `definition_head` and `block_math` — begin by skipping ` {0,3}` themselves,
because `marked`'s rules do.

#### 2 — the reverse offset map: §10's premise does not hold, and the owed item is a different thing

§10 says a per-leaf mapping from block-text offsets back to source offsets
*"has to be built where the reconstruction happens, in `mt_md::block`, because
that is the only place both sides are known."* Having written the
reconstruction, **that is not true, and the useful part is why.**

The stripper does produce the map for free: `StrippedLine` carries
`source_start` per line, and a block-text offset inside line *i* is
`source_start + (offset − line_start)`. But two things fall out of building it:

- **It is only the map for the kinds whose text *is* the stripped lines.**
  An atx heading is `'#'×level + ' ' + content` — a rebuild with no
  correspondence to reconstruct. A code block loses one to four columns per
  line *after* stripping. A table cell is trimmed and unescaped. A task item's
  first paragraph loses a four-character prefix. Each of those needs its own
  inverse, and each inverse belongs with the rule — so the "free by-product" is
  the container half only, and the per-kind half is a second implementation of
  D2 whichever stage writes it.
- **The container half is not lost by deferring it.** `strip_lines` needs two
  things: the source lines, and the ancestor containers' prefixes. The
  ancestors are the tree, which `Document` has. The prefixes are computable
  from the ancestors' *source ranges* — `Prefix::Quote` from the quote's first
  line, `Prefix::Item` from the item's. So the whole stripper is re-runnable
  for one block at a time, lazily, from `(src, tree, ranges)`.

**So what is owed forward is the per-node source ranges, not a per-leaf offset
map**, and that is a smaller and more useful thing: it is one side table rather
than a map per leaf, it is lazily consumable rather than eagerly built for
documents that never see a caret, and it is exactly what an incremental reparse
(D7/S6) wants anyway.

**Deferred, deliberately, and it is not the mistake §10 warns about.** §10's
mistake is the one where a thing becomes unbuildable because the information
that made it cheap is gone. Here the information is `Built::range`, and the
place it is dropped is `to_document` — three lines away from where `parse`'s
signature is decided, at S3. Building a public ranges API *now* would be an API
S3 immediately reshapes, and an API with no caller until M4 is the shape this
milestone has repeatedly found out is not a check. §10 is rewritten below to
say ranges rather than a map, and S3's row carries it.

### `marked`'s source is not minified, and three of S2's rules came from reading it

The brief describes `marked`'s bundle as *"minified, and `node -e` against a
`new Lexer()` is how S1 read its rules when the bundle was unreadable."* The
bundle is minified. **It ships `lib/marked.esm.js.map` with `sourcesContent`**,
so `Tokenizer.ts` (30 KB), `Lexer.ts`, `rules.ts` and `helpers.ts` are all
recoverable in full, in the original TypeScript, for the exact version muya
resolves (18.0.5).

That changed the method for the whole stage. Three rules would have been very
hard to find by probing and are two lines each in the source:

1. **`codeRemoveIndent`'s alternation is ordered.** `/^(?: {1,4}| {0,3}\t)/gm`
   — ` {1,4}` is greedy and tried first, so `"  \tfoo"` loses its two spaces
   and keeps its tab, and the `{0,3}` in the second arm can only ever be zero.
2. **`indentCodeCompensation` requires backticks.** `/^(\s+)(?:```)/` on the
   raw, so an indented `~~~` fence never compensates and an indented ```` ``` ````
   one does.
3. **`Tokenizer.list`'s `indent > 4 ? 1 : indent`**, which is what makes
   `1.      indented code` an indented code block holding `" indented code"`
   with exactly one leading space.

The probing did not stop — every rule here has a measured reproducer, and two
of the readings were wrong until the engine said so. But reading first turned a
search into a transcription. **`node -e` against a `Lexer` is the right tool
for "what does this do"; the sourcemap is the right tool for "why".**

### S2 registers nothing either, and `table.cell` is why that is a result

The brief expected `table.cell` to be *"the biggest class and the likeliest
register entry in the milestone"* — 924 differing leaves, and `marked` resolving
the `\|` escape into a literal `|`. It is not a divergence, and the reason is in
muya's own comment beside the code:

> Store the cell text as marked emits it (with the table `\|` escape already
> resolved to a literal `|`), so the editor shows `` `|` `` rather than the
> escaped `` `\|` `` inside inline code (#4849). `escapeText` re-adds the `\|`
> escape on serialization, keeping the markdown round-trip intact.

That is a **deliberate, documented, round-trip-safe** decision with the issue
number on it, and reproducing it is one `.replace("\\|", "|")`. A register entry
is for where the port *chooses* to disagree with muya (D4 rule 1), and there was
no choice to make: agreeing was cheaper than disagreeing and lossless either
way. Whether the port and muya agree about every escape is S4's question, when
`escapeText` gets written and the round-trip becomes a fixed point rather than a
claim.

So the block layer of the register is still wired, still filtered, and still
never fired — two stages running. The tolerance is exercised directly instead,
by `blocks::tests::a_registered_block_divergence_is_tolerated_and_an_unregistered_one_is_not`,
unchanged from S1. **Two stages of "nothing to register" is now a pattern worth
naming rather than a coincidence:** every disagreement S1 and S2 have found was
the port's to fix, because `pulldown-cmark` decides *structure* and `marked`
decides *text*, and the mapping layer sits between them with no opinion of its
own. The first register entry, if there is one, comes from a stage that has an
opinion — S4's serializer or S5's renderer.

### The traps the brief named, and which of them fired

Recorded because the ones that did not fire are as informative as the ones that
did, and because two of them were wrong about the reason rather than the fact.

| Trap | Fired? |
|---|---|
| `atx-heading` is a rebuild, not a slice | **Yes**, exactly as described. The measured extra: a bare `#` is `"# "` — trailing space — because the template interpolates an empty `text` |
| A synthetic paragraph's range is not a line range | **Yes**, and the fix is the stripper rather than anything special about synthetic paragraphs: a tight item's paragraph and a loose item's take the same path |
| The empty-container filler carries `end..end` | **Yes**, and it cost nothing: `strip_lines` returns no lines for an empty range, so the text is `""` by construction rather than by a guard |
| `frontmatter`'s `\s$` is a single character | **Yes**, transcribed |
| Indented code has a trailing newline fenced code does not | **Yes**, two rules |
| `table.cell` is the likeliest register entry | **No** — see above |
| The task marker is stripped even where the item is not a task item | **Half.** It is stripped in ordered lists, whose items muya calls `list-item` — so the fact holds. But **it is put back when the list is also loose**, which the brief does not mention and which no fixture in the 1344 reaches: `1. [ ] a\n\n2. [x] b` has item text `"[ ] a"`. Found by writing the test the brief's sentence implied and asking the engine |
| `trimUnnecessaryCodeBlockEmptyLines` is invisible to the gate | **Yes.** Implemented, with the measured values in the test, because "the gate cannot see it" is a reason to write the test rather than to skip the rule |
| The reference-definition paragraphs need checking | **Yes**, and it is what killed `content_start`: their text includes the ` {0,3}` indent that helper was throwing away |
| 1344/1344 is a floor nothing enforces | **Yes.** Raised to 1344 and joined by a leaf-count floor |

**And two mechanisms the brief did not name**, both found by the gate rather
than by reading, both worth stating because they are the shape of "the two
engines disagree about a block's *extent*, and it shows up as text":

1. **`pulldown-cmark`'s ranges start at the first non-whitespace byte;
   `marked`'s `cap[0]` starts at the line's ` {0,3}` indent.** Thirty of the
   first run's 47 disagreements. It is not a paragraph rule or a code rule — it
   is every kind at once, and it is why the stripper takes whitespace back to
   the line start before clipping to the range.
2. **An indented code block absorbed into a preceding paragraph loses four
   spaces, but only where `marked` restarts its block scan.** At the top level
   the `paragraph` rule swallows the line whole and the spaces survive
   (`"foo\n    bar"`); inside a list item — lexed with `state.top = false`, so
   `text` matches one line at a time — and at a block quote's lazy-continuation
   boundary, `code` is tried first and `codeRemoveIndent` runs
   (`"A paragraph\nwith two lines."`). Carried on `StrippedLine::starts_scan`,
   set by the container rather than guessed from the indent.

### S2's cost

**No new dependencies.** The stage is 500 lines of `mt_md::block` and 22 tests;
`cargo xtask deps` is unchanged at 14 crates.

**No change to `mt-doc`.** S1 had to correct `Underline`'s payload; S2 needed
nothing — `Block`'s variants, their fields and `BlockMeta` are all untouched,
and `block_names_match_typescript_union_one_to_one` did not have to change.
That is the 1:1 transcription earning its keep: every leaf kind already had a
`text` field of the right type waiting for a value.

**One deletion.** `content_start` and the `Line`/`Lines` iterator it was built
on. Their replacement is `strip_lines`, which is longer because it is exact.

### S2 delists nothing from `PENDING`, as predicted — run, not reasoned

**`PENDING` is still 184 and `cargo test --workspace` is green.** Row two of the
ratchet fails the build on a listed case that starts passing, so a green run
*is* the confirmation, and `every_pending_entry_names_a_transcribed_case` pins
the length independently.

The arithmetic that predicted it is S1's, unchanged: all 184 listed cases reach
`mt_md` through `parse`, `serialize` or `render_to_static_html`; D6 keeps all
three closed until S3; `parse_blocks` is not one of them. §7's table assigns
`mathTrailingSpace` (3) and `tableEscapedPipe` (4) to S2 — **seven in-scope
cases that test exactly what S2 built and that S2 cannot delist.** Added to
S1's 39, **S3 delists at least 46 cases that are not its own**, which is the
correction §6's S3 row already carries from S1 and which S2 makes larger rather
than different.

M1's experience was that five of eight stage rows needed correcting. S1 and S2
have now both predicted correctly, twice, for the same structural reason — the
entry-point staging makes the prediction mechanical rather than a guess. **That
is a reason to keep running it, not to stop:** the run is what would name a case
whose stage attribution is wrong, and it costs one command.

### S1's verification

`cargo xtask blocks`, over §4 C1's 1344 inputs:

```text
  1344/1344 agree, 0 registered, 0 name, 0 meta, 0 leaf-text disagreement(s), 0 engine throw(s)
  4985 leaves compared across 1344 inputs; 4954 differ in text, which is S2's gate and not compared here
  names: 1344/1344 — §4 C1 measured 1167/1344 before any mapping
```

| Clause | State | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `cargo xtask blocks` compares names and `meta` against `MarkdownToState` over all 1344 | **met** | `xtask/src/blocks.rs`. 1324 spec examples at `SPEC`, 20 documents at `MUYA_DEFAULT`; `the_input_set_is_c1s_1344` pins the count and its split, so a fixture added later cannot silently change what the number means |
| Every residual disagreement is registered or nothing; no name disagreement survives unregistered | **met, and stronger** | **Zero disagreements of any kind.** The strict reading is what the runner enforces — see the correction above |
| The number to beat, 1167/1344 on names | **met** | 1344/1344. The floor is a constant in `blocks.rs` and a run below it prints a `FAIL` line saying the mapping layer has broken something it was not supposed to touch |
| Leaf text is out of the comparison, and the runner says so | **met** | The runner prints which fields it compares, and prints the excluded gap's size (4,954 of 4,985) on the same run. `--with-text` is S2's mode and works today |
| `mt_md::parse` still returns `Err(Unimplemented)`; all 187 specs still skip | **met** | `entry_points_report_unimplemented_at_m0` unchanged; `PENDING` is 184 and `every_pending_case_fails_because_the_engine_is_unimplemented` still passes |
| `spec/divergences.json` parses with its new block entries; `divergences` reports 3 inline `ok` | **met — with no new entries, and that is the finding** | See "S1 registers nothing" below. 3 entries, 27 of 27 registered inputs disagreeing, 4,237 swept inputs agreeing |
| `cargo fmt --all --check`, `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, `xtask deps`, `xtask ci --require-ts`, `cargo test --workspace` | **met locally** | All green on toolchain 1.97.1. **694 passed, 0 failed, 2 ignored**, against S0's 638 |
| **And green in CI** | **MET — see below.** The first attempt was not | Attempt one, run [30873655790](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/30873655790): all three jobs `failure` with **zero steps** and S0's annotation. The repository was then made public, and attempt two, run [30875075728](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/30875075728), is **green on Linux, Windows and macOS** — 20 steps each, including `cargo clippy -D warnings`, `cargo test` and the whole `xtask ci` chain |

### The CI clause is met, and §0's open question is closed

**The repository is public as of 2026-08-04, and CI worked on the next
dispatch.** §0's correction said making it public *"restores CI immediately,
and costs nothing else"*, and recorded that the decision was the owner's rather
than the milestone's. The owner took it. That is the whole fix: Actions minutes
on a public repository are free and unmetered, no workflow changed, and both
workflows already checked out the reference engine unauthenticated.

So the sequence, for the record, is: S0 dispatched twice and got zero started
jobs; S1 dispatched once more and got a third; the repository went public; the
fourth dispatch was green on three platforms in under five minutes.

**What that buys, stated precisely, because it is the thing M1 shipped without
and M2 opened on.** `pulldown-cmark` and `serde_json` now have a
three-platform build behind them rather than a Windows-only one, and
`cargo clippy -D warnings` has run on a runner whose stable toolchain moves
ahead of this machine's — which is exactly the check that caught M1 out at M2
start, when five releases of unseen clippy lints turned the first remote build
red. §8's risk row *"a check exists but has never executed"* is no longer live
for CI.

**`soak.yml` is also unblocked, and its cost problem is gone rather than
solved.** §0's arithmetic — one dispatch ≈ 2,251 billable minutes against a
2,000-minute private allowance, of which the macOS proptest hour is 610 — was
arithmetic about *billing*, and a public repository is not billed. The
CPU-hours are unchanged; nobody is paying for them. The proptest matrix
therefore does **not** need trimming, and §8's row saying it might is
superseded: the trade it described only existed while the repository was
private.

### S1 registers nothing, and that is a result rather than an omission

The brief expected `spec/divergences.json` to gain its first `layer: "block"`
entries. **It gains none, because after the mapping layer there is nothing to
register**: over 1344 inputs the block-name-and-`meta` tree agrees with
`MarkdownToState` exactly. Register rule 3 makes an entry with no failing
differential case **stale** and fails the build on it, so adding one to satisfy
an expectation would break CI on purpose.

That leaves the block layer wired, filtered and never fired, which is precisely
the state §0 warns about — *a check that has never executed is not a check*. So
the tolerance is exercised directly instead:
`blocks::tests::a_registered_block_divergence_is_tolerated_and_an_unregistered_one_is_not`
drives the same disagreement through the runner's decision with the input
registered and unregistered, and asserts `DIVG` and `NAME` respectively. Without
it, "0 registered" and "the tolerance is misspelled" print the same line.

**Why there was nothing to register, stated so it is not read as luck.** Every
one of C1's 177 disagreements, and every one of the 25 the S1 runs turned up
beyond them, was the port's to fix: they are places `marked` does something
`pulldown-cmark` does not, and the mapping layer is exactly the place to do it.
A register entry is for where the port **chooses** to disagree with muya, and
S1 made no such choice. S2 may; D2's `table.cell` rule resolves a `\|` escape
and that is the kind of decision that produces one.
