### The fifth mechanism §4 C1's caveat asked about — there were three

D1's caveat: *"the measurement compared **names** … a fifth mechanism hiding
inside a case where the names happen to agree would not have shown up. S1's
gate is the same comparison over the full `TState` JSON, which is what would
surface it."* It surfaced three, all in `meta`, and none of them was
registrable — each is a field the port was simply getting wrong.

**1 — `meta.underline` is the literal run, and `mt_doc::Underline` could not
hold it.** `types.ts:18` declares `underline: string` and comments
`// "==="|"---"`; M0 transcribed the comment. `walkTokens` writes the whole
matched run, so muya's answer for `Hello world\n===========` is
`"==========="`. A two-value enum can only answer `"==="`, which is a
**round-trip data loss** on any underline that is not three characters — and
25 of the 652 CommonMark fixtures and 25 of the 672 GFM ones contain one. §10
marks lossless round-trip non-negotiable, so this is a fix: `Underline` is now
`Equals(u32) | Dashes(u32)`, and the four transcribed spec cases that inherited
the same error are corrected with the reason on them. The TypeScript spec
asserts only `expect(states[0].meta!.underline).toBeTruthy()`, so the
tightening was the transcription's own and so was the error.

**2 — `meta.lang` must be read from the source, not from the event.**
`pulldown-cmark` resolves HTML entities in a fence's info string, so
```` ```f&ouml;&ouml; ```` arrives as `föö`; `marked` keeps the source text and
muya's `meta.lang` is `f&ouml;&ouml;` (`commonmark#34`, `gfm#330`). It does
apply `anyPunctuation` — `/\\([\p{P}\p{S}])/gu → '$1'` — so a backslash escape
*is* resolved (`commonmark#24`, `gfm#320`). Constraint 1 on
`Block::CodeBlock::info` says never to normalise that field and a decoded
entity is a normalisation, so the info string is re-read from the block's first
line and unescaped explicitly.

**3 — looseness has an asymmetry `marked` gets from an ordering accident, and
it is 23 of the 1344.** `Tokenizer.list` sets a flag when an item's raw ends
with a blank line and lets the *next* iteration turn it into `loose`; the last
item has no next iteration, and `marked` then trims that item's raw outright.
So **a trailing blank line after a list's final item does not make the list
loose** — which is the shape of every ordinary document: a list, a blank line,
then a paragraph. `pulldown-cmark` includes that blank line in the last item's
range, so the naive reading says loose and muya says tight, in one direction,
19 times at the top level and 4 more in a nested list.

None of the three is a divergence: all three are the port disagreeing with muya
by accident, and all three are fixed. That is what the caveat was for.

### Three decisions S1 had to take that §5 does not cover

**1 — what `cargo xtask blocks` calls, since it cannot call `parse`.** D6 keeps
`parse` at `Err(Unimplemented)` until S3, so the block mapping needs a surface
that is not `parse`. Three were possible — a second `mt-cli` flag, a temporary
crate entry point, or a direct in-process call — and it is
**`mt_md::block::parse_blocks`, called directly from `xtask`**.

`xtask` already depends on `mt-md` and on `mt-inline`, and `tokens.rs` already
compares in-process for the same reason M1 S7 gives: a subprocess per input is
1344 process spawns to answer a question that needs none. An `mt-cli` flag
would be a *surface to delete at S3*, and D6's whole point is that S3 is the
commit where the entry points change. `parse_blocks` is not scaffolding: **at
S3, `parse` is this function plus leaf text plus the label-map pass**, so S3
folds the harness in by pointing `dump_state` at the same tree and this runner
keeps working unchanged. S2 and S3 both build on that and the reason is
recorded here because they do.

**2 — the state serializer lives in `mt-md`, not in the harness.**
`mt_md::dump_state` returns muya-compatible state JSON by M0's design, so
producing that JSON is the crate's job. Writing it in `xtask` would mean the
thing S1 and S2 measure and the thing S3 ships are two serializers that happen
to agree today. `mt_md::state::to_state` is what `cargo xtask blocks` compares
and what `dump_state` will return.

**3 — the parser builds the document through `Document::apply`.** D9's
confirmation is the constraint *"no edit path may bypass `Document::apply`"*,
and a parser is the one caller that could argue for an exception — it is
building, not editing. The exception is not taken: `mt_md::block` maps the
event stream to a plain tree and inserts it with `Edit::InsertNode`, reading
each minted `NodeId` out of the inverse batch (`InsertNode`'s inverse is
`RemoveNode { node }`). The cost is a revision counter that counts blocks; the
benefit is that `mt-doc` grows no second write path for the invariant to have
an exception to.

### S1's cost

**Two dependencies, both scheduled.** `pulldown-cmark` is named in the
workspace manifest's own "not until the milestone that needs it" list and this
is that milestone; it lands with `default-features = false`, which drops the
HTML writer §4 C3 decided nothing will call, leaving `bitflags`, `memchr` and
`unicase` — all pure, so §1 holds and `cargo xtask deps` stays green.
`serde_json` stops being dev-only and becomes `mt-md`'s, for the serializer
above.

**One correction to `mt-doc`, which S0 closed.** `Underline` gains its run
length. That is a change to a type S0 shipped, and it is made rather than
registered because the alternative is silent round-trip loss; `Block`'s variant
set is untouched and
`block_names_match_typescript_union_one_to_one` did not need to change, which
is the constraint that actually matters.

**One extension to `dump-ts-state.mjs`.** `--inputs` takes a JSON array of
`{src, options}` so that 1324 spec examples can be driven without writing 1324
temporary files, and so that one run can drive both option sets. It is the
shape `dump-ts-tokens.mjs --inputs` already had. The file mode is untouched and
`cargo xtask diff` still uses it.

### S1 delists nothing from `PENDING`, as predicted — and the prediction is confirmed rather than assumed

§6 asks for the arithmetic against `PENDING` rather than against §7's row, and
for the answer to be run rather than reasoned. Run: **`PENDING` is still 184
and `cargo test --workspace` is green**, which is the machine-checked form of
"nothing started passing" — row two of the ratchet fails the build on a listed
case that passes, so a green run *is* the confirmation.

The arithmetic that predicted it: all 184 listed cases reach `mt_md` through
`parse`, `serialize` or `render_to_static_html`; D6 keeps all three closed
until S3 at the earliest; and `parse_blocks` is not one of them. §7's row
assigns seven files to S1 — `gitlabMath` (16),
`diagramFlowchartSequence` (6, less the one `sequenceTheme` case §6's S0
correction moved to M3), `codeFenceInfoString` (4), `codeFenceLength` (3),
`infoStringModel` (3), `gitlabMathToggle` (4) and `nestedMixedLists` (4) —
**39 in-scope cases that test S1's work and that S1 cannot delist**, because
every one of them calls `parse`. That is not a mis-assignment in §7: those
files do test what S1 built, and what S1 built is behind S3's door.

**So the row that needs correcting is S3's, not S1's.** §6's S3 row says only
*"`cargo xtask diff` reports `PASS` or a registered divergence for all 22
files"*; it should also say that S3 delists **S1's 39 and S2's** alongside its
own, because the day `parse` returns `Ok` is the day all of them stop failing
at once. That is D6's staging working as designed — one entry point, one wave
of red — and it should be expected rather than discovered.

M1's S4 made the same prediction and the same confirmation, and the
confirmation was the useful direction: had something started passing, the
ratchet would have named a case whose stage attribution was wrong.

### Three decisions S0 had to take that §5 does not cover

Recorded here rather than in a commit message, because each is a shape later
stages inherit.

**1 — the document root is not a `Block`, and cannot be.** §2 sketches
`Node { parent, block: Block }`. `Block` maps 1:1 onto the TypeScript `TState`
union; `TState` is what an *array* of top-level blocks is made of and has no
root member; and adding a twentieth variant would fail
`block_names_match_typescript_union_one_to_one`, which is the precondition for
the §11.2 harness. So `Node`'s body is `Root { children } | Block(Block)`.

This is not an invention: **muya's own tree is rooted at `ScrollPage`**
(`block/scrollPage.ts`), whose `blockName` is `scrollpage` and which likewise
has no `TState` member. The port models what the reference engine does. M0's
reason for keeping `children` inside `Block`'s container variants survives —
children still live in exactly one place per node kind.

**2 — `Block::meta()` returns `Option<BlockMeta>`.** M0 wrote it as
`-> BlockMeta`, and `BlockMeta` deliberately has no variant for the seven
blocks whose TypeScript interface carries no `meta` object ("there is nothing
to set"). The original signature was unimplementable for seven of nineteen
variants. `Option` also makes `Edit::SetMeta` on a paragraph a clean
`MetaMismatch` rather than a fabricated empty meta.

**3 — `Edit::RemoveNode`'s inverse is `MoveNode`, not `InsertNode`.** Found by
the property test rather than by reading, which is the whole argument for
having written the property test first.

`RemoveNode`'s M0 doc comment says its inverse is `InsertNode` "carrying the
removed block back". That fails in two compounding ways:

- `InsertNode` carries **one** `Block`, and a container `Block` holds
  `Vec<NodeId>`. Restoring a subtree needs those ids still live, so removal
  cannot free the descendants.
- `InsertNode` mints a **new** id. So the batch
  `[SpliceText { node: x }, RemoveNode { node: x }]` inverts to
  `[InsertNode(…), SpliceText { node: x }]`, and by the time the splice is
  undone `x` is stale and the undo fails. That is not a contrived batch: any
  edit that rewrites a block and then merges it away has that shape.

So **`RemoveNode` detaches**: the node and its subtree stay in the arena with
the node's `parent` cleared, and the inverse is `MoveNode` back to the old
`(parent, index)`. The node keeps its id, which makes an apply-then-undo pair
restore the `NodeId`s and not merely the content — a **stronger** guarantee
than §2.2 asks for, and the one `mt-layout`'s cache will want at M3. §2.2's
enum shape is untouched, so D9's bet is intact.

The price is that a detached subtree is reachable only from the undo stack.
`Document::prune_detached()` reclaims it, and its doc comment names the only
legitimate caller — M4's history trimming, the one place that knows an inverse
has been dropped. The property test calls it after every undo and asserts the
arena returns to its pre-batch size, so the cost is bounded by the undo stack
rather than unbounded.

### S0's verification

| Clause | State | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| No `todo!()` left in `mt-doc`, `#![allow(dead_code)]` gone | **met** | All 25 filled. `grep -rn 'todo!\|allow(dead_code)' crates/mt-doc/` returns two prose mentions in doc comments and no code |
| Property: a batch's inverse restores the document byte-for-byte, ≥ 10⁴ batches | **met, and stronger than the clause** | `crates/mt-doc/tests/edit_inverse.rs` — 2,000 proptest cases × 5–10 batches. The batch count is **asserted**, not assumed: the test fails if the run did not reach 10,000, so the gate's number is measured rather than inferred from `ProptestConfig`. It also asserts the `NodeId`s are restored and that `prune_detached` returns the arena to its pre-batch size |
| 187 `#[test]`s exist and fail for the right reason | **met** | `crates/mt-md/tests/state_specs.rs`, 19 modules. `every_pending_case_fails_because_the_engine_is_unimplemented` runs all 184 listed cases and asserts each panic names `Unimplemented` — so "for the right reason" is machine-checked rather than claimed. **Delete that test at S3**, when `parse` first returns `Ok` |
| `PENDING` holds exactly them, and the ratchet can fail | **met, at 184** | `every_pending_entry_names_a_transcribed_case` and `the_transcribed_counts_match_the_typescript_suite` pin both directions. Row two is exercised by M1 S5's **injected-list variant**, plus `spec_case_implements_that_decision_table`, which drives the real `spec_case` over a real `PENDING` entry and asserts it panics with "Delete it from the list" |
| Register parses with `layer`; `cargo xtask divergences` still reports 3 `ok` | **met** | 3 entries, 27 of 27 registered inputs disagreeing, unchanged. `layer` is **required** — a missing or misspelled one is a parse error, because a defaulted layer would file an entry under a runner that never runs it |
| `cargo xtask deps` green | **met** | `ropey` (`mt-doc`), `proptest` (`mt-doc` dev), `mt-inline` (`mt-md` dev). §1's four headless crates stay headless |
| All three harnesses still skip, and say why | **met** | `diff` 22 SKIP, `conformance` 1,324 Skipped, `blocks` does not exist yet (S1's) |
| `cargo fmt --all --check`, `clippy -D warnings`, `xtask deps`, `xtask ci --require-ts`, `cargo test --workspace` | **met locally** | All green on toolchain 1.97.1. **638 passed, 0 failed**, against M1's 371 |
| **And green in CI** | **NOT MET — the jobs did not start** | Two dispatches, [30868427952](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/30868427952) and [30868472316](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/30868472316); all six jobs annotated *"the job was not started because recent account payments have failed or your spending limit needs to be increased"*. §0's correction has the arithmetic — one `soak.yml` dispatch is ≈ 2,251 billable minutes against a 2,000-minute private-repo allowance. **Blocked on infrastructure, not on code**, and the fix is the repository owner's |

**The last clause is not met, and it is recorded as not met.** §0 of this
document opens on M1 having shipped a CI configuration that had never
executed, and on the lesson that *a check that has never run is not a check*.
That applies here without an exception: this commit's local verification is
green on one platform and one toolchain, and **the three-platform claim has not
been made by anything.** In particular the two clippy releases and the two
libc/`ropey` combinations that only CI sees are unverified, which is exactly
what caught M1 out at M2 start.

Unlike M1's soak clause, this one does not hold the stage open: S0's gate is
about `mt-doc`, the transcription and the register, and all of those are met
and measured. But the clause is listed here, unmet, with its run ids — so that
S1 inherits an open item rather than a green tick nobody earned.

**What a green S0 does *not* claim.** `mt_md` still returns
`Err(Unimplemented)` from every entry point, so there is nothing to diff
against and all three harnesses skip. **A green run at S0 is not agreement with
muya about anything.** It is three things and no more: `mt-doc` implemented and
property-tested, 187 transcribed cases compiling against the real API and
failing for the stated reason, and a register that parses with `layer` and
filters on it. Saying which is what stops the next stage inheriting a claim
nobody made.

**The property test earned its place on the first run.** It found the
`RemoveNode` inverse bug in under a second, on a two-edit batch, before any of
`mt-md` existed — the S0 analogue of M1's finding that a harness catches what a
reading does not. The counterexample it produced is now a named unit test in
`edit.rs`.

**The ratchet was falsified in both directions, by hand, on the real list** —
M1 S7's method, which is what turns "the mechanism exists" into "the mechanism
fires":

- **Row two.** Adding
  `strong_cjk_flanking::editor_path_tokenizes_strong_or_em_in_cjk_context` to
  `PENDING` — a case that passes — fails the build with *"is listed in PENDING
  but PASSES. Delete it from the list"*.
- **Row four.** Removing
  `markdown_to_state::parses_a_bare_dashes_line_as_a_single_thematic_break` —
  a case that still fails — fails the build with *"is NOT listed in PENDING and
  FAILED — this is a regression"*, carrying the original panic. It also trips
  `every_pending_entry_names_a_transcribed_case`'s count, so the list's length
  is guarded independently of any one case.

Both were reverted; `PENDING` is 184 and `cargo test --workspace` is **638
passed, 0 failed** (M1 closed at 371).

**Three negative controls, because a check that cannot fail is not a check.**
In M1 S6's shape: the property's comparison is driven over one edit of every
kind and must see each (`the_comparison_the_property_uses_can_tell_two_documents_apart`
— which caught that a `MoveNode` to the index it already occupies renders
identically, so the control moved to index 1); `spec_case` is driven over
synthetic passing and failing cases against a real `PENDING` entry; and the
register's layer filtering has a test showing that an entry run by the *wrong*
comparator reports a false `Stale` and fails CI, which is why the filtering is
not cosmetic.

### S0's cost

**Two dependencies, both scheduled rather than opportunistic.** `ropey` is
named by §2.1 and falls due at D8. `proptest` was already in the workspace for
`mt-inline` and becomes a dev-dependency of `mt-doc` too. `mt-inline` becomes a
**dev**-dependency of `mt-md`, because `referenceLink` and `strongCjkFlanking`
drive the tokenizer alongside the parser — a downward edge `mt-md`'s own crate
doc already anticipated.

**One addition to `mt_md::Options`: `list_indentation`.** §2's table and M0's
doc comment both derive `Options` from `MarkdownToState`'s option interface and
`renderToStaticHTML`'s. There is a **third** source and neither mentions it:
`ExportMarkdown`'s constructor takes `{ listIndentation }`
(`state/stateToMarkdown.ts:64`), it is the only option the serializer reads,
and five of `listSerialization.spec.ts`'s cases vary it. Carrying it on
`Options` rather than widening `serialize`'s signature keeps one options type
for the crate, which is what lets the differential harness drive both engines
from one struct.

**D11's filings are drafted and not filed, and rule 4 stays open.** The
decision to fork was taken (§5 D11) and the three issue bodies are written —
`docs/upstream-issues.md` — but posting three public issues on someone else's
repository is a step S0 was asked to confirm rather than assume, and the answer
was to draft for review. So all three register entries still read
`upstream: null` and `cargo xtask divergences` still prints `NOT FILED` beside
each of them. **That is the correct state, not an oversight**: the runner's own
output is what will keep asking, and `docs/upstream-issues.md` says exactly
what closes it. Case 3 is not drafted and its entry records why — it is a
divergence from a *test-only* DOM at a site where the shipped Electron build
behaves differently again, so there is no upstream change that makes it go
away.

### The M2 exit gate, clause by clause

Stated now, in the shape M1.md's closing section had to invent at the end:

| Clause | How it is checked |
|---|---|
| ~~All 215~~ **All 187** transcribed state specs pass; `PENDING` is empty | `cargo test --workspace`, with the ratchet still armed — the injected-list variant is in from S0 rather than added at the end, so emptying the list is never the edit that switches the ratchet off. **The 215 is corrected to 187 above**; `PENDING` started at 184 because three already pass. **At S6 it holds 2, and both are M6's**, so the clause as written is unreachable inside M2 — see the correction below the table |
| ~~≥ 87.7 % CommonMark / ≥ 86.3 % GFM~~, **or the re-baselined floor if higher** | `cargo xtask conformance`, enforcing. **Re-baselined at S5 to 71.0 % and 70.8 %**, which is the second arm of this clause and the one §5 D5 exists to govern. The inherited numbers were `getHighlightHtml`'s and §4 C3 guaranteed the port's would differ; §6's "S5's verification" has the diff in both directions and the argument for all 231 additions |
| Round-trip is a fixed point on corpus + fixtures | `crates/mt-md/tests/round_trip.rs`, in `cargo test --workspace`. **Met at S4** over all 22 documents with no exceptions; the same file states it over the 1324 spec examples too, where 22 of them are enumerated. **And restated at S7 as a property over generated markdown**, where *fixed point* is false and the true claim is that the round trip ~~**settles by the fourth application** — 458,272 of 466,909 generated documents settle on the first, four need the fourth, none needs a fifth~~ **settles by the eighth — corrected at S7's round 2**, where the soak found documents settling at 8 and at 6 that no exclusion covered, and the bound stopped being an observed maximum: its threshold is derived from a 4,590-document sweep whose law is `t / 4 + 2`, and `4 * (SETTLES_BY - 2) = 24` is written as that expression so the two cannot drift. It skips 0 of 1,324 spec examples and 0 of 11 fixtures. `crates/mt-md/tests/round_trip_properties.rs`. **And corrected once more at round 3, in the other direction:** a **305-byte** input falsified 8 as well, so `fuzz/fuzz_targets/round_trip.rs` stopped asserting settling from bytes and asserts totality under repetition instead; the settling claim is stated at 8 in the property test alone. **The clause as originally written — a fixed point — remains met over the corpus and the fixtures** (22 documents, 1302 of 1324 spec examples with 22 enumerated); what is not true of *generated* input is any constant at all, and **finding 6 is a family that never settles** |
| **Malformed input must never panic (§11.3)** — added at S7, because this is the clause the stage exists to establish and the table did not carry it | `cargo-fuzz` over `parse`, `round_trip` and `reparse`, plus the six generated properties, plus `the_only_panics_the_generators_reach_are_the_ones_the_guards_name`. ~~**Two crashes were found in this port's own code and both are repaired**~~ — **corrected at S7's round 2, and this is the clause the soak falsified.** Round 1 repaired two crashes (`apply_prefix`'s mid-`char` cut on `"- a\n\u{2028}"`, and unbounded container recursion — a **4,001-byte** file overflowed the stack, answered by `MAX_NESTING_DEPTH = 128` with all six recursive walks audited rather than only the one that crashed) and then wrote a **class** claim on the strength of them. The soak found **four more sites** of a single class — a lone `\r` making the port's line grid disagree with `pulldown-cmark`'s — plus one failing silently. What this clause can now say is the sweep: **3,000,000 runs, 0 panics in this crate**, against a baseline of **284 panics at 5 sites**, with **five** audit rows named as unproven (§10) ~~One panic remains and it is not this port's~~ — **one panic remains, `pulldown-cmark`'s, 4 hits in that sweep**, named, guarded, `#[should_panic]`ed and owed forward. **Round 3 leaves two marks on this row and neither is a panic in `parse`:** a **stack overflow reproduced twice** under ASan on Windows for which no artifact exists, because the process dies of an access violation before libFuzzer can write one — finding 8, seen on no Linux run, and *not* the recursion the depth clamp fixed — and finding 6, which is this clause's *"Data safety"* half rather than its panic half: a document that grows by one line on every save |
| `cargo-fuzz` and the property tests run at soak length **and the run is green** | `.github/workflows/soak.yml` — **6 targets × 2 shards × 4 h = 48 CPU-hours**, plus `round_trip_properties` in `proptest-soak` on all three platforms. ~~**Wired at S7 and not yet dispatched**~~ — **dispatched, and it came back red on four jobs** ([31089007249](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31089007249)). ~~Round 2 answers all three defects it found; the re-dispatch is the clause below~~ — **re-dispatched ([31253525223](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31253525223)) and red on the same four jobs, with findings 6, 7 and 8. This is the one exit-gate clause M2 does not meet.** The half that is green is `fuzz parse`, **both shards over 4 h each**, which is round 2's panic repairs holding. **NOT MET, itemised in ten rows and closed on deliberately** — see *"M2 closes on an enumerated red soak"* below |
| Differential vs. the TS engine agrees on the corpus **or the disagreement is registered** (D4) | `cargo xtask diff --require-ts`, 22 files |
| The block tree matches muya over §4 C1's 1344 inputs | `cargo xtask blocks`, added at S1 and in `cargo xtask ci` from then on |
| Every registered block divergence still disagrees, and nothing else does | `cargo xtask divergences`, both layers |
| M1's inline claims are undisturbed | `cargo xtask divergences` — 3 entries `ok`, 4,264-input sweep clean |
| `normalizeHtml` agrees with `runner.ts`'s | `cargo xtask normalize --require-ts`, added at S5. 2,648 of 2,648 — M0 decision 12's owed item, discharged |
| `fmt`, `clippy -D warnings`, `xtask deps`, `cargo test --workspace` | CI, on three platforms, **on the remote**. ~~**860 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored at S7**~~ **873 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored after S7's round 2**, against S6's 837. **Met on the remote**: run [31088979433](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31088979433), green on three platforms — and green on the tree the soak then tore apart, which is the point of the clause below |

**Correction to clause 1, at S5.** *"`PENDING` is empty"* cannot be met inside
M2 and saying so now is what stops it reading as slippage later. Eleven cases
remain: nine are §10's "Owed by S4" — one mechanism, held for S6 because the
fix is a re-lex of every list item's content and S6 is the stage that builds
that machinery — and **two are M6's**, because they assert KaTeX markup and
`mt-math` is §9's M6. The right reading of the clause is therefore *"`PENDING`
holds only cases owed to a named later stage, and each one is named"*, which is
what `the_only_listed_cases_that_fail_for_their_own_reason_are_the_eleven_m2_names`
asserts as a machine-checked list rather than as a sentence. S6 is expected to
take it to two.

**Resolved at S6, and the clause is met on the corrected reading.** `PENDING`
holds **two**, both `renderToStaticHTML`'s KaTeX cases, both M6's — so **not one
case M2 owes is left in the list**, which is the strongest form the clause can
take inside this milestone. `the_only_listed_cases_that_fail_for_their_own_reason_are_the_two_m6_names`
is the renamed assertion. S5's expectation of two was exactly right, and the
nine came off with their mechanism corrected rather than merely implemented —
§6's "S6's verification", decision 2.

What S7 inherited from this clause was therefore **nothing to delist**, and one
thing to keep true: a list with no M2 case in it is precisely the state M1 §10
named as the one in which an owed check becomes a forgotten one, which is why
both survivors carry their milestone in the constant's own comment rather than
only here.

**And S7 delisted nothing, as inherited.** `PENDING` still holds the two
`renderToStaticHTML` KaTeX cases and the assertion still names them. S7's own
findings produced no candidate for the list either, which is the right outcome:
the three it deferred are pinned by named tests with reproducers (§10, "Owed by
S7"), and `PENDING` is a ratchet over *transcribed muya spec cases*, not a
general to-do list — putting a port defect of S7's own into it would have made
the list stop meaning what six stages have made it mean.

### ~~The soak is dispatched last, and M2 stays open until it is green~~ — it was dispatched twice, both were red, and M2 closes on the second

**Kept as written, because the stance it records is the one this milestone
departed from and the departure is the decision.** M1 closed when its soak went
green and this section inherited that rule verbatim. M2 does not meet it. What
follows is the record of both dispatches, and then the close.

Every clause above is met except one, and the one is the same one M1 closed on.
`gh workflow run soak.yml --ref main -f fuzz_minutes=240` dispatches D6's matrix
as S7 rebuilt it — **6 targets × 2 shards × 4 h = 48 CPU-hours** of libFuzzer on
Linux, plus the platform-independent proptest soak (now including `mt-md`'s
`round_trip_properties`) for an hour on all three platforms, plus the unbounded
tiling run and the full divergence sweep.

~~**M2 is open on that clause and on the remote CI dispatch beside it, with a
named reason and no run id yet.**~~ **The CI dispatch is green and the soak
dispatch was red.** It closes when the soak reports green — *not* when the run
exists. That distinction was the whole of M1.md's last section, and §0
records why inheriting it as "done" would have been exactly the failure mode S4's
false negative demonstrated: the run was green, it did not have to be, and that
it did not have to be is the reason to keep doing it this way. Closing M2 is a
later commit, and it writes the job-by-job breakdown here.

The thing this dispatch buys that nothing above can is **length**. The stack
overflow was found by a fuzz run of sixty seconds, and the 60-second runs of all
three new targets after the fix came back clean — 7,138, 2,130 and 17,883 execs.
Six targets at 8 CPU-hours each is the first time the parse, round-trip and
reparse paths are driven long enough for that "clean" to mean anything.

**And the first dispatch spent that length. Run
[31089007249](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31089007249)
is red**, and M2 did not close on it. All six `mt-inline` shards passed and both
`parse` shards passed; `fuzz round_trip` (both shards), `fuzz reparse` (both
shards) and `proptest soak` on **linux and windows** failed. Three defects, one
of which is four panic sites sharing a root cause and a claim in §6 that was
false when it was written — §6's "S7's verification" carries all of it, and the
per-commit CI run that was green on three platforms
([31088979433](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31088979433))
beside it, because *every check that ran on every commit passed and only the long
run found these* is the sentence this section exists to be able to write.

~~**All four saved crash artifacts now replay clean, and a second soak has not
been dispatched.** That is the open clause and it is the only one: M2 stays open
until a soak run is green, and a green *local* replay of a red run's artifacts is
exactly the evidence M1 spent five stages learning not to accept in place of
one.~~

**The second soak was dispatched, and it is red too.** Run
[31253525223](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31253525223)
on commit `946f99c`: the same four jobs failed, with three findings round 1's run
could not have produced, while CI run
[31253517799](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31253517799)
was green on three platforms on the same commit. The sentence above is still the
right rule and M2 does not meet it. What changed is not the rule — it is the
decision about what to do when the rule is not met for the third round running,
and that decision is below rather than folded into a verdict.

### M2 closes on an enumerated red soak

Three soak rounds have each found **new** defects in the two newest subsystems —
`mt-md`'s serializer and S6's incremental reparse — and there is no evidence that
a fourth would be the last. So M2 closes here: on the clauses that are met, with
the soak clause recorded as **failing and itemised**. Stated in the order that
keeps it honest — what landed, what did not, why closing anyway, and what this
close must not be read as saying.

#### 1. What M2 delivered, clause by clause

Seven of the exit gate's clauses are met and measured. Every number carries its
denominator and every one was re-measured after round 2's repairs:

| Clause | Measured |
|---|---|
| The block tree matches muya over §4 C1's 1344 inputs | `cargo xtask blocks --require-ts` — **1344 / 1344**, **4,985** leaves, **0** name / **0** meta / **0** leaf-text |
| Differential vs. the TS engine on the corpus, or the disagreement is registered (D4) | `cargo xtask diff --require-ts` — **22 passed, 0 failed, 0 registered divergence(s), 0 skipped** |
| Conformance at or above the re-baselined floor (D5) | CommonMark **463 / 652 = 71.0 %** (floor 71.0), GFM **476 / 672 = 70.8 %** (floor 70.8), `expected-failures.json` unmoved at **189** and **196**. Both **at** the floor, not above it, which is what a one-way door looks like when nothing has regressed |
| `normalizeHtml` agrees with `runner.ts`'s | `cargo xtask normalize --require-ts` — **2,648 / 2,648**. M0 decision 12's owed item, discharged |
| Round trip on corpus + fixtures | `crates/mt-md/tests/round_trip.rs`, **unedited by S7 in three rounds**: **22** fixed-point exceptions and **348** identity exceptions, both still exact, both ratchets in both directions |
| Every registered divergence still disagrees and nothing else does | `cargo xtask divergences` — **0** block entries, **3** inline entries `ok`, **4,237** swept inputs agreeing and **0** disagreeing. The block layer registered nothing across seven stages |
| `fmt`, `clippy -D warnings`, `xtask deps`, `cargo test --workspace`, on three platforms | **873 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored** (S6: 837), `deps` ok at **14 crates**, `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings` clean, and CI run [31253517799](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/31253517799) `success` on **linux, windows and macos** |

Two further clauses are met on a **corrected** reading and are recorded here as
such rather than counted clean:

- **`PENDING` is empty** — corrected at S5 and resolved at S6 to *"`PENDING`
  holds only cases owed to a named later stage"*. It holds **2**, both
  `renderToStaticHTML` KaTeX cases, both **M6's**. Not one case M2 owes is in
  the list.
- **§11.3 — malformed input must never panic.** What is supportable is a sweep
  and its denominator: **3,000,000 runs, 0 panics in this crate**, against a
  baseline of **284 panics at 5 sites**, and both `fuzz parse` shards green over
  **4 hours each** in the second soak. What is *not* covered by it is named
  below and in §10: **five** audit rows that slice-and-use text, the upstream
  `pulldown-cmark` panic (**4 hits** in that sweep), and finding 8. Round 2's
  lesson was that a repair to a site is not a claim about a class; this reading
  is that lesson applied to its own row.

#### 2. The soak clause, failing and itemised

**`cargo-fuzz` and the property tests run at soak length — the run must be
green. It is not, and this is the clause M2 closes red on.** Two dispatches,
both red on the same four jobs. Every open finding at close, with the reproducer
that produces it or the §10 entry that carries it:

| Open finding | Where it came from | Reproducer, or §10 |
|---|---|---|
| **6 — the growth family.** `serialize ∘ parse` appends one more copy of a line on **every** application, forever | `proptest soak`, linux and macos, second soak | A lone `\r` inside a fenced code block's body; `round_trip_properties.rs:2311`, `m1` 2 copies → `m9` 10 copies. §10, "Owed by S7" item 6 |
| **7, and the reparse divergences as a class.** `Incremental` disagrees with a full parse of its own source | `fuzz reparse`, both shards, **both** soaks | `SPEC: ""` with edit `[4] 11..37 → ""` at `fuzz/fuzz_targets/reparse.rs:194`. §10, "the reparse divergences are a class rather than a list" |
| **8 — a stack overflow behind the depth clamp** | two 420-second local `round_trip` runs under ASan, Windows only | **Reproducible, but no artifact exists**: the process dies of `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` with `<empty stack>` before libFuzzer writes the unit. Not seen on the Linux soak. §10 says what would re-find it |
| **4 — a list item's stray indent sheds 4 columns per pass** | first soak, `round_trip` shard 1 | ``format!("- a\n{}```o\n", " ".repeat(4 * n))`` settles after exactly `n`, **6 of 6** measured. §10 item 4 |
| **5 — a backslash run in a fence info string halves per pass** | first soak, `round_trip` shard 2 | `format!("~~~ a{}\nx\n", "\\".repeat(n))` settles after ⌊log₂ n⌋ + 1, **8 of 8** measured. §10 item 5 |
| **1 — the round trip oscillates with period 2, and muya does not** | round 1, generated | `"> - [a]: /x\n>   * "`. §10 item 1 |
| **2 — a backtick in a fence info string has no constant settling time** | round 1, generated | n fences → exactly `n + 2` applications. §10 item 2 |
| **3 — serialized output diverges from muya on two generated documents** | round 1, generated | `"1. a\n--\n0\n\t- a"` and `">a\n \t- a\nd"`. §10 item 3 |
| **Five slice sites that are unproven rather than safe** | the crate-wide audit, round 2 | `block.rs` **2359**, **2277**, **2486**, **2938**, **988** — each slices a range *and uses the text*. None fired in the 3,000,000-run sweep, and *"did not fire"* is the whole of what is known. §10 |
| **The upstream `pulldown-cmark` panic** | S6's generator, widened at S7 | `mt_md::parse("- [a]:x\n\u{b}", …)` → `pulldown-cmark-0.13.4/src/parse.rs:2199`. **4 hits** in the 3,000,000-run sweep. `docs/upstream-issues.md`; §10, "Owed by S6" |

One more item is owed and is not a finding: **the byte fuzzer's settling
assertion**, removed at round 3 and owed *back* the day findings 4 and 5 are
repaired. §10 carries it as one item with the constant that should come down
with it.

#### 3. The argument for closing anyway — a decision, with an owner

**This does not follow from the evidence; it is a choice made against a named
alternative, and the alternative is a fourth round.** Taken at S7 round 3, on
2026-08-08, by this repository's author (`fjamesprice`), who is also the only
committer and therefore the only person the decision can be attributed to.

What it weighs:

- **M2's actual deliverable landed in full** — `mt-doc`, `mt-md` in both
  directions, the incremental reparse, 1344/1344, 22/22, the conformance
  re-baseline, `normalize` 2648/2648. The seven clauses above are not a partial
  scope; they are the scope.
- **Each round found new defects in the two newest subsystems**, which is S7
  working exactly as designed — this is the first time either path has been
  driven from bytes rather than from a corpus. It is also why a fourth round has
  no argument for being the last: three rounds is a rate, not a tail.
- **The reparse divergences look like a class**, and a class is not closed by
  fixing its next instance. Grinding one more mechanism inside a stage scoped as
  *"invariants and soak"* buys one reproducer and spends the evidence that
  produced it.
- **What S7 proved is worth more written down than ground down.** The finding of
  this stage is not any single defect; it is that **the incremental path is not
  yet trustworthy**, with reproducers attached. That statement is more useful to
  whoever picks this up than a fourth round of repairs would have been.

**The cost of the decision, stated rather than discounted:** M1's rule — a
milestone closes when its soak reports green — is broken here, deliberately and
once. M1 earned that rule by spending five stages learning that a hand-run can
return a false negative, and nothing about this close weakens it. The
alternative it protects against is closing on a soak nobody ran or nobody read;
this soak was run twice, read in full, and its failures are enumerated above
with reproducers.

#### 4. What a reader must not conclude

Two readings this section exists to foreclose:

1. **Not** that `mt-md`'s incremental path is trustworthy. It is not. `mt_md::reparse`
   has disagreed with a full parse on **every** dispatch it has been given, on
   **five** distinct mechanisms — four repaired, one open and uncharacterised —
   and the class is open. §10 states the consequence
   for M3/M4 explicitly: **do not build on `mt_md::reparse` without
   re-establishing it first.**
2. **Not** that *"M2 closed"* means the soak passed. It did not pass. It ran
   twice, it was red twice, and the second run's findings are listed above
   rather than summarised precisely so that no later document can compress this
   into a green tick. Any sentence of the form *"M2 is complete"* is false
   without the clause that follows it: **complete on its deliverables, red and
   itemised on its soak.**

---

## **M2 is closed with its soak clause red and enumerated.** 2026-08-08

**Seven exit-gate clauses met and measured**, two more met on a **corrected**
reading that is written down where the correction was made, and **one — the
soak — failing**, with ten open findings against it. Of those ten rows: seven
carry a concrete reproducer, one (finding 6) carries its trigger but not yet a
minimal input, one (finding 8) is **reproducible but leaves no
artifact**, dying of an access violation before libFuzzer can write one, and one
is the five slice sites, which are **unproven rather
than broken**. Nothing was weakened to close it, and no clause was closed with an
exception: the failing clause is recorded as failing.

The handover is §10, which is now the working document rather than a list of
courtesies: findings 6, 7 and 8 are added there, the reparse divergences are
recorded as a **class** with what would establish its extent, and the M3/M4
constraint is stated in as many words. §8 carries what round 3 established about
this project's own habits — a check that gets raised rather than reasoned about,
a property that predicted its own falsification and shipped anyway, and a
first explanation that fit every fact available and was still wrong.
