## 8. Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| `pulldown-cmark` diverges from `marked` somewhere the mapping layer cannot reach, and it is found at S5 rather than S1 | C1 measured the four mechanisms up front; S1's gate is the same comparison over `meta` too, which is where a fifth would surface. A residual becomes a register entry (D4) with named inputs |
| The `RefDefs` shortcut is taken later by someone who does not know it is lossy | D3 decides against it **with the reproducer** (`[foo]: /a` + `[foo]: /b` → one span), and the reproducer becomes a test in `mt_md::block` |
| Leaf text is implemented as a source slice because §4 says so | C2 measures 1,319 differing leaves in seven named classes; S2's gate is zero unregistered leaf-text disagreements — **met at S2**, 0 of 4,985, and the gate compares text by default rather than behind `--with-text` |
| Three ratchets wake on the same commit and the failures are uninterpretable | D6 stages them by entry point, finest-grained message first; S3, S4 and S5 are that order and must not be reordered |
| The conformance re-baseline quietly lists whatever fails | D5: the new list may be no longer than 78/90 unless each addition is argued in the same commit; `floor_percent()` is raised to the measured rate in that commit. **Fired at S5 and the rule worked**: the list is 189 and 196, the second arm was taken, and *reading* the first regenerated diff turned up a class (`<p></p>` for the model's synthetic empty paragraph) that was a renderer artefact rather than an engine limit — six entries came off before the floor was frozen. A blanket regeneration would have made them permanent |
| The re-baseline measures a pipeline nobody decided on | C3 forces the `Document`-vs-direct choice **before** S5, because the number depends on it |
| "Differential agrees on 100 %" is read as a claim about the whole port | C4: a block-state comparison cannot see a token. The claim about inlines is `cargo xtask divergences`, and §6's gate table names which harness carries which claim |
| §4.1 is treated as an optimisation and `Edit`/`DirtySet` are written without it | D7 settles it before `edit.rs`; `DirtySet` carries `Text`/`Structure` from S0 |
| A stale `NodeId` addresses a recycled slot after an incremental reparse | D8: generational indices, so it is `None` rather than the wrong block. **And the other half, closed at S6**: a *live* id matched to the wrong block is what generational indices cannot see, so the subtree diff matches on **full structural equality** — two subtrees that compare equal are the same block to every consumer, and reuse is correct by construction rather than by heuristic. A mis-paired leftover costs `mt-layout` a cache entry and nothing else. §6's "the three things S6 was asked to settle", decision 1 |
| `Block`'s 1:1 mapping is "improved" while filling in the `todo!()`s | `block_names_match_typescript_union_one_to_one` fails on any change; `lib.rs` says **do not improve the shape** in as many words |
| A check exists but has never executed | §0: M1 shipped with a CI configuration that had never run, and the first run was red on all three platforms. `cargo xtask ci --require-ts` in CI, and the soak's first green run is what closes M1. ~~**Live again at S0 for a new reason** — the Actions allowance is exhausted and the jobs will not start~~ — **closed at S1**: the repository is public, run [30875075728](https://github.com/fjamesprice/marktext-rs/actions/runs/30875075728) is green on three platforms, and every commit from here is remotely verified again. **Fired again at S3, in a place nobody was watching**: `dump-ts-state.mjs`'s `readMarkdown` had said since M0 that the Rust side must normalise CRLF and strip a BOM, and nothing had checked that it did — `cargo xtask blocks` normalises on its own side before handing one string to both engines, so the port's file layer was unexercised for two stages. The first `Ok` from `dump_state` turned 11 of 22 corpus files red on a `\r`. The general form is worth carrying: **a harness that pre-normalises its inputs cannot test the layer that normalises them**. **Fired a third time at S7's round 2, in a `#[test]` rather than in a runner**: `crates/mt-md/tests/reparse_properties.rs:210` passes every fixture through `mt_md::normalize_source`, **all 11 of the 11** round-trip fixtures contain a bare `\r`, and `SHAPES` and `INSERTS` contained none — so S6's 3.2 M generated edit sequences could not reach the lone-`\r` class the soak found. Twice in one milestone, in two harnesses written by different stages, is what promotes this from an incident to the row below it |
| The soak is too expensive to run at the cadence it was designed for | ~~§0's correction: one dispatch is ≈ 2,251 billable minutes against a 2,000-minute private-repo allowance … either the repository goes public (free, unmetered) or the proptest matrix is trimmed~~ — **moot at S1.** The repository went public, so the minutes are unmetered and the arithmetic was about billing rather than about CPU. The matrix stays as D6 built it |
| M2's own numbers are measured once and never again | S1 and S2's gates are `cargo xtask blocks`, a committed runner, not a scratch script. M1 spent five stages learning that a hand-run can return a false negative and be recorded as a result |
| `SourceMap`'s ranges are read after an edit and silently address the wrong bytes | Added at S3, and the shape of the API is the mitigation: the map is returned by `parse` and is **not reachable from a `Document`**, so there is no way to ask a document for a range it cannot keep true. Its own docs say the ranges are a fact about one parse; S6's region reparse produces new ones for the region rather than patching these. **Met at S6, and the commitment held**: the region's ranges come from the parse that just ran, and the two halves outside it are an exact shift — checked against a full parse's, leaf by leaf, over 3.2 M generated edit sequences |
| A range that is a **coarse stand-in** is sliced with as though it were exact | Added at S6, and it is what the risk above could not have said, because the map only stopped being injective at S5. Every node under a `footnote` carries the definition's own range and every node the `state.top = false` re-lex produces carries a line-mapped one; both are true and neither is tight. `SourceMap::is_exact` makes the difference askable, and S6's leaf-text path declines a coarse range rather than re-deriving text from it — **which is not hypothetical**: without it, `20.` was spliced into a paragraph as though it were the paragraph's own text |
| A guard about "which edits are safe" is derived from a rule and never tested against edits | Added at S6, where five of the leaf-text path's seven guards were wrong on first writing and **none** of the five was reachable from the corpus, the 1344 or the 22. The mitigation is the generator: `crates/mt-md/tests/reparse_properties.rs`, `proptest` over edit sequences at three option sets, with a committed `.proptest-regressions` file so that sixteen hard-won seeds re-run before any new case. It also found a **panic in `pulldown-cmark`** (§10, "Owed by S6"), which no fixture in the repository reaches |
| "The round trip is mostly the identity", written once and never checked | Added at S4: `crates/mt-md/tests/round_trip.rs` enumerates both exception sets — 22 fixed-point and 348 identity — as **ratchets** rather than reports, so a listed input that starts round-tripping fails the build and an unlisted one that stops fails it too. `no_fixed_point_exception_is_one_of_the_twenty_two_documents` additionally forbids §9's own exit-gate set from ever appearing in a list |
| A **renderer** divergence is chosen deliberately and cannot be registered | Added at S5, and it is the serializer risk below in a second place, which is what makes it a property of the harness set: `cargo xtask conformance` compares against the **spec's** expected HTML rather than against muya's, so rule 3 has no differential case to fire on. Two were found (the sanitizer's `style`/`<svg>`, the front-matter newline), both are pinned by tests, and §10 owes the runner to whichever stage first wants to disagree on purpose |
| A serializer divergence is chosen deliberately and cannot be registered | Found at S4 and **not mitigated**, deliberately: rule 3 needs a failing differential case and no harness compares serialized markdown. The evidence a runner would have produced was taken by hand instead — 1346 of 1346 inputs byte-identical to `ExportMarkdown.generate` — and §10 owes the runner to the stage that first wants to disagree. Recorded rather than closed, which is what the register's own rules require |
| A check exists, has executed on every commit for six stages, and has never been pointed at input nobody wrote | Added at S7, and it is §0's *"a check that has never executed is not a check"* one denominator further in. `round_trip.rs` asserts the fixed point over a fixed 1346 and `source_ranges.rs` asserts nesting over the corpus; both are green, both have always been green, and both are claims about the inputs this repository happens to own. Pointed at generated markdown, the first is **false** — 1334 of 1346 settle in one application at the fixture set, but over 466,909 generated documents four need a **fourth** — and the second is false too, which is why `fuzz_targets/parse.rs` declines to assert it. The mitigation is the generator and the fuzzer as a pair: `crates/mt-md/tests/round_trip_properties.rs` for the properties a `#[test]` can state, `fuzz/fuzz_targets/{parse,round_trip,reparse}.rs` for the ones only a crash can, and 48 CPU-hours of soak so they run at length rather than for 192 cases |
| Unbounded recursion, reachable from a document a user can paste | Added at S7 and **it had already shipped**: `"> " × 2000` — a **4,001-byte** file — overflowed the stack on a plain `--release` build with no sanitizer and no fuzzer. Found by S7's `round_trip` fuzz target at 200 bytes under ASan; depth 1000 was fine, so nothing shorter would have shown it. `panic = "abort"` makes a stack overflow a crash, and §11.3's clause is that malformed input must never produce one. The mitigation is `MAX_NESTING_DEPTH = 128` **plus the audit**: all six of the crate's recursive walks are accounted for rather than the one that crashed, because four of them take a `Document` and may not assume their caller went through `parse` — `mt_doc::Edit::InsertNode` reaches any depth without going near `mt-md`. muya offers no number to copy: it dies too, and its depth tracks V8's `--stack-size` in a straight line, so the limit is this port's stated choice |
| A fuzz target asserts an invariant that is not one, and the failure is the test's | Added at S7, and it is the risk a fuzzing stage is *most* likely to walk into, because a target is written by asking "what is always true" of a function whose edge cases are unknown. Concretely: asserting that source ranges **nest** in `fuzz_targets/parse.rs` would have crashed on `-\t- [a]: /x[xter\n` and reported a defect in `mt-md` that is a defect in the assertion — §10's "Owed by S6" item 2 records that a child's range can escape its parent's, and `source_ranges.rs`'s nesting test is true of the corpus rather than of every string. The mitigation is that every claim a target makes is written down **with what it deliberately does not claim**, in that target's module docs, next to the observation that `fuzz_targets/tokenize.rs` *does* assert nesting for `mt-inline` tokens, where it holds. Two targets in one directory asserting opposite things is correct, and only the prose makes it readable |
| The runner keeps agreeing because it has quietly stopped comparing | Added at S2: the **leaf count** is a floor (4,985) and is counted on the TypeScript side, so a projection bug or a reference engine that stops answering fails instead of printing `1344/1344 agree` over nothing. The names floor is raised from C1's 1167 to the measured 1344 in the same commit, in the shape D5 gives for the conformance floor |
| A repair fixes a **site** and the documentation claims a **class** | Added at S7's round 2, and **it had already happened in this file**: §6's S7 gate row said §11.3's *"malformed input must never panic"* was *met on this port's own code* on the strength of one two-line repair to `apply_prefix`, and the soak found **four more sites of the same class** within hours of that commit — plus `footnotes.rs:157`, reachable from a document containing an accented letter. The mitigation is not a test, it is a form of words: a class claim needs a **sweep with a denominator**, so the row now reads **3,000,000 runs, 0 panics in this crate** against a baseline of **284 panics at 5 sites**, and the **five** audit rows that slice-and-use text are named as unproven rather than covered. *"No input panics"* is unavailable from any fixed corpus and is not asserted anywhere |
| A generator's **input space has a hole** and the gate's denominator hides it | Added at S7's round 2. S6's gate is *"equal to a full reparse over **3.2 million** generated edit sequences"*, and those 3.2 M could not possibly have contained a lone `\r`: `reparse_properties.rs:210` normalises every fixture, all 11 of 11 fixtures carry a bare `\r`, `SHAPES` and `INSERTS` carried none. **A large denominator is not a cover.** Mitigated by `the_generator_can_reach_the_lone_carriage_return_class` — a `#[test]` that asserts the *generator* can produce the class, from both of its sources, sweeping every offset of every CR shape × all inserts × both option sets — which is the only kind of check that fails when someone tidies a table. The normalisation in `fixtures()` was deliberately **kept**, because those files are read from disk and `normalize_source` *is* `mt-cli`'s `read_markdown`; the raw-`\r` caller is modelled by the shapes and the inserts instead |
| A constant that guards a check is **raised** rather than reasoned about | Added at S7's round 3, and the history is the whole argument: `SETTLES_BY` was **4** — an observed maximum over a generator that reached neither of the two families behind it — and it lasted **one soak**; it was then **8**, derived from a 4,590-document sweep whose law is `t / 4 + 2`, and it lasted **one soak**, falsified by a **305-byte** input (` ```o ` and 300 backslashes, ⌊log₂ 300⌋ + 1 = 9; 60,000 backslashes needs 17). Each raise bought exactly one run, which is the tell: three families' settling times **grow with the input**, so against a coverage-guided fuzzer over `-max_len=65536` **any constant is a statement about `max_len` rather than about the engine**. The mitigation is not a bigger number. `fuzz/fuzz_targets/round_trip.rs` **stopped asserting settling** and asserts totality under repetition (`APPLICATIONS = 4`, the file-layer `ends_with('\n')` invariant checked at every pass); the settling claim lives in `round_trip_properties.rs` alone, at 8, over generators whose shapes this repository enumerates; and §10 owes the assertion **back** — with the constant coming down — the day findings 4 and 5 are repaired, so that "removed" cannot quietly become "gone" |
| A property **predicts its own falsification in its margin** and ships anyway | Added at S7's round 3, and it had already happened here. Round 2's `SETTLES_BY = 8` carried the note *"a fence info string with 256 backslashes is where 8 stops being true. That is a **finding, not a guard**"* — and the very next soak produced 300 backslashes in a 305-byte input. The constant shipped because 255 looked far away; it was **300 bytes** away, and a coverage-guided fuzzer walks that distance in minutes. The general form, and the only mitigation that would have worked: **a comment naming the input that breaks a constant is a failing test that has not been written yet.** Either write that input as a case — where it becomes a finding the day it is true — or do not ship the constant. Nothing in this repository enforces it, which is why this is a risk row rather than a check, and it is the second time round 3 found the evidence written down one round before it mattered |
| A **first explanation fits the evidence and is still wrong** | Added at S7's round 3, from finding 8. A stack overflow left no crashing input in `fuzz/artifacts/`, and the run's stdout had been piped through `head`; SIGPIPE killing cargo-fuzz before it wrote the artifact fit every fact available and was written into this document as the cause. **A second run with no pipe at all reproduced the crash and still wrote nothing** — the real cause is `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` with `<empty stack>`, which kills libFuzzer before the write. The mitigation is the one this row is: **re-run before recording a cause**, because an explanation that accounts for the evidence is not the same as the one that produced it. The habit it was mistaken for is still worth keeping — do not pipe a fuzz run's stdout through a truncating filter — but it was not what happened |
| **"M2 closed" is read downstream as "the soak passed"** | Added at S7's round 3, because the close is the first in this project to leave a gate clause **failing**. M2 met seven exit-gate clauses and closed with the soak red on two dispatches and **ten open findings**. The mitigation is entirely in the form of the record: the status line, the closing heading and §6's closing section each carry the qualification **in the same sentence as the close**; the open findings are a table with a reproducer or a §10 pointer on every row rather than a paragraph; and §10 states the consequence for the next milestone in as many words — **M3/M4 must not build on `mt_md::reparse` without re-establishing it**. Any restatement of this milestone's status without that clause is wrong, and the enumerated table is what makes the wrongness checkable rather than arguable |
| A defect fails **silently** one line away from one that fails loudly | Added at S7's round 2, from the audit rather than from a crash. `block.rs:3602` panicked on a mid-`char` index and `block.rs:3361` took `.get(marker_len..).unwrap_or("")` on **the same wrong index**, returning `""` as a list item's first-line content and deleting its text. Only the crash was reported by anything. The mitigation is that the audit swept the *class* and not the reproducer's site — 17 rows: **10** fixed with tests, **2** covered by argument rather than by type, **5** named as unproven — and that the five left open are left open **because** they slice-and-use text, where a boundary snap converts a loud failure into a quiet wrong answer. A guard that turns a panic into a wrong answer is a regression written as a fix |

---

## 9. Open decisions carried forward

Three of §14's four are settled above — the CRDT deferral (D9), the upstream
parity tag (D10), fork-and-rename (D11). What remains:

- **GPU vs CPU default (§12.2)** — decide at M3, unchanged. §12.2 says to treat
  it as a real decision informed by measurement; `mt-layout` and `mt-render`
  are where the measurement becomes possible.
- **`Arena` demotion policy** — D8 implements promotion to `ropey` and not
  demotion. If a profile ever shows a document holding thousands of ropes that
  are no longer large, revisit; the decision is recorded on `Text` rather than
  left implicit.

---

## 10. Owed forward

### Owed to M3/M4 — block text has no document offset

C2's consequence, recorded here because the layer that needs it does not exist.
A leaf's `text` is a reconstruction, so a token's `range` — a byte offset into
that text — does not map to a document offset by addition. Anything that needs
the reverse direction (caret placement from a click, a search hit's position in
the file, an error message with a line number) needs a per-leaf mapping from
block-text offsets back to source offsets, ~~and it has to be built where the
reconstruction happens, in `mt_md::block`, because that is the only place both
sides are known~~ — **see the correction below.** Recorded on `Block::text`'s
docs as well as here.

#### Corrected at S2 — the thing that is owed is the per-node source ranges

Written before the reconstruction existed; having written it, the premise is
wrong in a way that makes the owed item **smaller**. The argument is in §6's
"the two things S2 was asked to settle"; the short form:

- The map is only a free by-product for the leaf kinds whose text *is* the
  container-stripped lines. An atx heading is rebuilt from scratch, a code
  block loses columns *after* stripping, a table cell is trimmed and
  unescaped — each needs its own inverse, and each inverse belongs beside its
  rule wherever it is written. There is no stage at which that half is free.
- The expensive-looking half — the container prefixes, which need the whole
  ancestor chain — is **not** lost. `strip_lines` needs the source lines and
  the ancestors' prefixes; the ancestors are the tree, and their prefixes are
  computable from their **source ranges**. So `(src, tree, ranges)` is enough
  to re-run the stripper for one block, lazily, at any later stage.

So: **keep the per-node source ranges.** They exist as `Built::range` while
`parse_blocks` runs and are dropped by `to_document`, because `mt_doc::Block`
maps 1:1 onto `TState` and has nowhere to put them. **S3 is where that is
decided**, because S3 is where `parse`'s signature is written; a public
ranges-returning API added at S2 would be an API S3 reshapes and M4 is the
first caller. `block::parse_blocks`'s doc comment carries this so it is
findable from the code.

#### Taken at S3 — the ranges survive, on `Parsed` rather than on `Document`

**`mt_md::parse` returns `Parsed { document, labels, source_map }`**, and
`SourceMap` is a `NodeId → Range<usize>` side table handed back *with* the
document rather than stored *in* it. §6's "the two things S3 was asked to
settle" has the argument; the short form is that a `Document` outlives its
parse and no `Edit` maintains a range, so a side table on `Document` would be a
field whose invariant nothing could ever keep. `block::parse_blocks_with_ranges`
is the function; `block::parse_blocks` still exists and drops them, which is
what `cargo xtask blocks` calls.

**The half that is owed is smaller again, and it is now the *only* half.** The
container half is discharged: `block::tests::the_stripper_is_re_runnable_from_src_tree_and_ranges`
rebuilds a block quote's `Prefix` from the quote's range alone and re-derives
the inner paragraph's text with `strip_lines`, so S2's argument is a passing
test rather than an argument. What remains owed to M4 is the **per-kind**
inverse — an atx heading's rebuild, a code block's post-strip columns, a table
cell's trim and unescape, a task item's four-character prefix — and §10 has
said since S2 that there is no stage at which that half is free.

**One thing M4 has to know that S2 could not have said**: the ranges index the
string `parse` was given, and `mt-cli` normalises CRLF before calling it. A
range from a file read through the file layer is an offset into the normalised
text. muya has the same split — file layer normalises, parser does not — but a
caret mapping back to a file offset has to account for it. See §6's "the trap
nobody named".

#### Narrowed at S6 — the region reparse needs none of the per-kind half, and the map now says which ranges are exact

Two things S6 settled about this item, both by needing it rather than by
reasoning about it.

**The per-kind inverse is still owed and S6 is not its caller.** §10 has said
since S2 that there is no stage at which that half falls out of anything, and §6
has twice recorded "S6 will have the machinery" without checking. Checked: the
region reparse needs no inverse at all — it re-parses and produces new ranges —
and the leaf-text fast path is deliberately restricted to `Paragraph`, **the one
leaf kind whose text is exactly the container-stripped lines**
(`block::paragraph_text`). An atx heading, a code block, a table cell and a task
item all take the region path instead, which is correct rather than fast. So M4
is still the first caller, and the reason is now a design decision with a test
beside it rather than an omission.

**The container half is used for the first time outside a test.**
`block::prefix_chain` rebuilds the `Prefix` chain from the ancestors' ranges,
which is S2's argument and S3's
`the_stripper_is_re_runnable_from_src_tree_and_ranges` — and S6's fast path is
its first real caller.

**And the map now says which ranges may be sliced with.**
[`SourceMap::is_exact`] is new at S6: `false` for every node under a `footnote`
(S5's coarse retarget) and for every node the `state.top = false` re-lex produces,
both of which lex a **de-indented copy** and therefore carry a range that is true
but not tight. The leaf-text path consults it before re-deriving text, and that
check is what stopped `20.` from being spliced into a paragraph as though it were
the paragraph's own text. **M4 wants the same accessor for the same reason**: a
caret cannot be placed from a range that is a stand-in.

### Owed by S1 — three narrowings, each with its reproducer

Named here rather than left in a comment, and **not registered**: register
rule 3 makes an entry with no failing differential case stale, and none of the
three is reachable from any of S1's 1344 inputs. Each says what would close it.

1. **`Options::footnote` does nothing in the block pass.** muya's footnotes are
   its own `marked` extension — `[^id]:` with a 4-space de-indent and a
   recursive lex (`utils/marked/extensions/footnote.ts`, 114 lines) — not GFM's,
   so `pulldown-cmark`'s `ENABLE_FOOTNOTES` is not a substitute and is left off.
   Both option sets in S1's input set have `footnote: false`, under which muya
   emits a plain `paragraph` for `[^a]: note` and so does the port. ~~**Owed to
   S3**~~ — **answered at S3, and the narrowing survives unchanged.** The label
   map is where `[^a]` first becomes a question, exactly as the brief predicted,
   and the answer is that it is not a question about `Options::footnote` at all:
   `beginRules.reference_definition` has no opinion about `^`, so
   `[^a]: note` is a definition-shaped paragraph and **the label pass registers
   the label `^a`** — in muya and in the port. A later `[^a]` in prose is then a
   shortcut *reference link* to `note` rather than plain text, with footnotes
   off, in both engines.
   `mt_md::labels::tests::a_footnote_definition_registers_a_reference_label_with_footnotes_off`
   is the characterization. What `mt_md::Options::footnote` will gate is
   `lexBlock`'s footnote extension at S5 and, through it,
   `mt_inline::SyntaxOptions::footnote`; it has never gated this pass and does
   not need to. `markdownToState::converts_block_level_footnote_tokens_into_footnote_states`
   is the case that still fails on the missing extension, and it is one of the
   three §10's "Owed by S3" enumerates.
2. ~~**`meta.fenceLength` saturates at 255.**~~ — **paid at S4.**
   `Block::CodeBlock::fence_len` and `BlockMeta::CodeBlock::fence_len` are
   `Option<u32>`; ``"`".repeat(300)`` round-trips, and asked of the running
   engine muya's `fenceLength` for the same input is 300 as well.
   `serialize::tests::a_fence_longer_than_255_characters_survives_the_widened_field`
   is the reproducer this item asked for. `Block`'s variant set is untouched, so
   `block_names_match_typescript_union_one_to_one` did not have to change —
   checked rather than assumed, because widening a field is not adding a variant.
3. **A code fence's info string is unescaped over ASCII punctuation only.**
   `marked`'s `anyPunctuation` is `/\\([\p{P}\p{S}])/gu`; CommonMark §2.4 makes
   only ASCII punctuation escapable, and that is what the port does. The two
   differ on ```` ```\— ````, where `marked` emits `—` and the port emits `\—`.
   Reachable, not reached — **if a fixture ever hits it, it is a register entry
   rather than a fix**, because it is the port following the spec where `marked`
   does not, which is the direction D3 and M1's D3 both chose.

### ~~Owed by S3 — three cases that fail for a reason of their own~~ — all three paid

The delist found what neither S1's gate nor S2's could: **`cargo xtask blocks`'
1344 inputs and `cargo xtask diff`'s 22 documents are not the only denominator,
and the transcribed spec suite reaches inputs neither of them contains.** Three
listed cases now fail because the port disagrees with muya rather than because
an entry point is closed, and
`state_specs.rs::the_only_listed_cases_that_fail_for_their_own_reason_are_the_ten_m2_names`
asserts that these are all of them — a further one appearing fails the build.
**Renamed at S4**, when the set went from three to ten: two came off (the task
marker, fixed) and nine went on (the reparse finding below).

**1–2. An empty task marker takes a lazy continuation in `marked` and not in
GFM.** `markdownToState`'s
`parses_an_empty_task_marker_with_lazy_continuation_text_as_a_task_item` and
`keeps_lazy_continuation_text_on_the_final_empty_task_marker`.

```text
"- [ ] \ntext\n"
  muya      task-list › task-list-item › paragraph "text"
  the port  task-list › task-list-item › paragraph ""  +  paragraph "text"
```

The mechanism is one line of `marked` and it is upstream of everything the
mapping layer does: to `marked` the item's content is the string `"[ ] "`,
which is **not blank**, so the item's paragraph stays open and `text` is an
ordinary lazy continuation of it — the `[ ] ` prefix comes off afterwards, in
`strip_task_markers`. To GFM the `[ ]` is a *task-list marker* rather than
paragraph content, so `pulldown-cmark` leaves the item empty and `text` starts
a new block at column 0.

Measured, so the next stage inherits the rule and not the symptom — seven of
thirteen probes differ and the six that agree are the boundary:

| Differ | Agree |
|---|---|
| `- [ ] \ntext` | `- [ ] \n\ntext` — a blank line closes the paragraph |
| `- [x] \ntext` | `- [ ] \n# head`, `- [ ] \n> quote` — a block start is not lazy |
| `1. [ ] \ntext` | `- [ ] \n  text` — indented, already inside the item |
| `- [ ] \ntext\nmore` | `- \ntext` — no task marker, nothing to disagree about |
| `> - [ ] \n> text` | `- a\ntext` — an ordinary lazy continuation, already right |
| `- [ ] \ntext\n- [ ] b` | |

~~**Not registered** (rule 3: no failing differential case) and **not fixed at
S3**~~ — **fixed at S4.** `Builder::fold_empty_task_marker_continuations`
reproduces `marked`'s loop for this one shape: when a list's last item is a
single line that is nothing but a marker and a checkbox, the following
paragraph is folded into it, dedented by the marker's column where the line
reaches it and appended verbatim where it does not, and the list the paragraph
had interrupted is merged back. All thirteen probes agree; the seven that
differed now match and the six that agreed are unmoved.
`block::tests::an_empty_task_marker_takes_a_lazy_continuation` is the flipped
test and it carries all thirteen.

Two rules the fix needed that S3's measurement did not have, both from
`compatibleTaskList` rather than from `marked`:

- **The two regexes differ by one quantifier and it decides a real input.**
  `EMPTY_TASK_REG` ends `[ \t]*$` and `TASK_MARKER_PREFIX_REG` ends `[ \t]+`, so
  `- [ ]` alone is an empty task item and `- [ ]\ntext` is **not a task item at
  all** — muya emits a `list-item` whose text is `"[ ]\ntext"`. Only the prefix
  form can fold, so only the prefix form is what the port tests for.
- **An ordered list keeps the marker in the item's text**, because
  `compatibleTaskList` calls `stripTaskTextPrefix` in its bullet branch only.
  `1. [ ] \ntext` is an `order-list` holding `"[ ] \ntext"`.

~~**3. `Options::footnote` has no block extension.**~~ — **paid at S5.**
`markdownToState::converts_block_level_footnote_tokens_into_footnote_states`
asked for a `footnote` state with `footnote: true`, and muya builds one from its
own `marked` extension (`utils/marked/extensions/footnote.ts`, 114 lines) which
is not GFM's. It is ported now: `block::footnote_segments` splits the document
at `[^id]:`, `clean_footnote_body` is the extension's five chained `replace`s,
and the body is re-lexed by a recursive `build`. The case delists with
`footnoteHtml`'s six.

`block::tests::the_footnote_option_gates_muyas_own_block_extension` is S1's
test flipped rather than deleted — the `false` arm still asserts exactly what
S1 asserted, because both option sets every harness drives have
`footnote: false` and that behaviour may not move.

**Two narrowings the fix carries, neither registered** (rule 3: no input in the
1344 or the 22 reaches either, so an entry would report `Stale`):

- **A definition is recognised at column 0 only.** `marked` runs its extensions
  inside the nested `blockTokens` call a list item's dedented content gets, so
  `- [^a]: x` holds a footnote in muya and a paragraph here. The scanner works
  on the document's own lines.
- **Every node under a `footnote` carries the definition's own source range.**
  The body `marked` lexes is a de-indented *copy*, so an offset into it does not
  map to a document offset by addition — §4 C2's fact, one level up. Coarse and
  true, rather than an offset into a string the document does not contain. The
  finer map is owed to whichever stage first wants a caret inside a footnote.

### ~~Owed by S4 — one mechanism, in three shapes, with nine cases holding it~~ — paid at S6, and the mechanism as stated was wrong

**The prediction below is right about the disagreement and wrong about the fix**,
and S6 measured the difference rather than inheriting it. Kept in full, because
§6 asks for a prediction to be corrected against a run rather than quietly
replaced, and because the correction is the useful part.

**What was wrong: "a re-lex of every list item's content" is a no-op.** Asked of
both engines, the item's dedented content parsed as a whole document gives the
port's own answer *and muya's*:

```text
"foo\n20. foo\n141. foo\n"     muya      paragraph "foo\n20. foo\n141. foo"
                              the port  paragraph "foo\n20. foo\n141. foo"
```

Nothing about **where** the content is lexed is in dispute. What differs is which
rule set applies: `state.top = false` removes `blockTokens`' `paragraph` branch,
and the CommonMark restriction — `rules.ts`'s
`.replace('list', ' {0,3}(?:[*+-]|1[.)])[ \t]+[^ \t\n]')`, whose own comment says
*"only non-empty lists starting from 1 can interrupt"* — lives in that regex and
nowhere else. So the two regexes differ in exactly two ways, and the fix is
bounded: split the paragraph at the refused line and re-lex from there, where a
list start is a list start in both engines.

`block::split_refused_list_starts` is the fix and carries the argument; the
recursion runs from the refused line to the **end of the container** rather than
to the end of the paragraph, because `marked`'s loop does not stop at the list it
opens. All nine `listSerialization` cases delist, `cargo xtask blocks` is
1344/1344 before and after, and `spec/divergences.json` gains nothing — the change
resolves a `marked`-versus-CommonMark disagreement in `marked`'s favour, which is
agreement rather than divergence.

The three shapes below all pass now;
`block::tests::the_three_shapes_the_task_marker_fix_does_not_reach` keeps the
neighbouring ones pinned.

---

*The S4 statement, kept for the record:*

**`marked` re-lexes a list item's dedented content line by line and CommonMark
does not.** `Tokenizer.list` calls `blockTokens(item.text)` with
`state.top = false`, where the `text` rule matches **one line**, so every line
of an item is its own token and a `list` can start at any of them. CommonMark's
rules about which list starts may interrupt a paragraph live in `marked`'s
top-level `paragraph` regex and nowhere else.

`cargo xtask blocks` is 1344/1344 because the 1324 spec examples exercise those
rules at the top level, where the two engines agree. The disagreement is
*inside a list item*, and the transcribed spec suite is the only input set that
reaches it — and only once S4's serializer existed to produce the markdown that
does.

```text
"3. foo\n   20. foo\n       141. foo\n"
  muya      order-list › item › order-list(start 20) › item › order-list(start 141)
  the port  order-list › item › paragraph "foo\n20. foo\n141. foo"
            — an ordered list may interrupt a paragraph only if it starts at 1

"- a\n  * \n  * \n"
  muya      bullet-list › item › paragraph "a" + bullet-list(*) of two empty items
  the port  bullet-list › item › paragraph "a\n* \n*"
            — an empty list item may not interrupt a paragraph at all

"- [ ] \ntext\n===\n"
  muya      task-list › task-list-item › setext-heading "text"
  the port  task-list › task-list-item › paragraph ""  +  setext-heading "text"
            — the same rule for a setext underline, which the S4 fold does not
              reach because `---` and `===` break `marked`'s loop differently
```

**Not registered**, on rule 3: no input in `cargo xtask diff`'s 22 or
`cargo xtask blocks`' 1344 reaches any of the three, so an entry would report
stale and fail the build. Held instead by **nine `PENDING` entries** — all of
`listSerialization`'s residue — by
`block::tests::the_three_shapes_the_task_marker_fix_does_not_reach`, and by
this section.

~~**Owed to S6**, and the reason is the shape of the fix rather than the size of
it: reproducing `marked` here means re-lexing an item's content rather than
patching a sibling list, which is a change to how *every* list item's children
are built. S6 is the stage that rebuilds a region's subtree and diffs it, so it
is the stage that will have the machinery.~~ — **paid at S6, and the machinery
was not the region reparse's.** The de-indented-copy shape it needed is
`footnote_block`'s, from **S5**; what S6 added is the rule, not the machine. The
prediction was right that S6 would do it and wrong about why, which is the
distinction §6 asks for when it says to run rather than read.

### Owed forward — a serializer differential, and the register cannot fire without one

S4 has an opinion about markdown and `spec/divergences.json` still has no
`layer: "block"` entry, for a reason that is structural rather than incidental:
**rule 3 makes an entry stale unless a differential case disagrees, and no
harness in this repository compares serialized markdown.** `cargo xtask diff`
and `cargo xtask blocks` compare the state tree, which is `parse`'s output;
`cargo xtask divergences` compares token streams. A deliberate serializer
divergence would have nowhere to be registered.

S4 took the measurement a runner would have taken — **1346 of 1346 inputs
serialize byte-identically to `ExportMarkdown.generate`** — by hand, once, and
recorded it in §6. Building the permanent runner is owed to whichever stage
first wants to *disagree* with `ExportMarkdown`, on the argument S1 gave for
`parse_blocks`: the stage that needs a harness is the stage that should build
it. `tools/diff/dump-ts-state.mjs` would need a sibling that runs
`ExportMarkdown` over the same `--inputs` file, which is the whole of the work.

### Owed by S5 — an HTML differential, and the second half of the register's blind spot

S4 found that a **serializer** divergence cannot satisfy register rule 3,
because no harness compares serialized markdown. S5 found the same thing about
the **renderer**, in a different place, which is what makes it a property of
the harness set rather than an incident: `cargo xtask conformance` compares
against the **spec's** expected HTML, not against muya's output. So a
deliberate disagreement with muya's HTML has no differential case to point at,
reports `Stale`, and fails the build.

Two were found and both are held by tests and by this section instead:

1. **`mt_md::sanitize` drops `style="…"` and `<svg>`** where
   `EXPORT_DOMPURIFY_CONFIG` keeps them. Measured against jsdom + DOMPurify.
   Both are the conservative direction — a sanitizer may only ever differ by
   admitting *less* — and `sanitize::tests::style_and_svg_are_dropped_where_dompurify_keeps_them`
   makes the claim checkable. **Owed to `mt-export` at M6**, which is the
   milestone that exports styled documents and rendered diagrams.
2. **The rendered `<pre class="front-matter">` loses the body's trailing
   newline**, because `Block::Frontmatter`'s text is trimmed
   (`block::front_matter_text`) and muya's `frontMatterRender` uses the
   *token's* text. It is §4 C3's cost visible one byte at a time, and it is
   **not** reconstructed: the front-matter regex's closing delimiter is not
   anchored to a line start, so a body does not always end in a newline and
   appending one unconditionally would be wrong in exactly the shape §4 C1's
   front-matter mechanism exists to reproduce.

**The runner is owed forward rather than built here**, on the argument S1 gave
for `parse_blocks` and S4 repeated for `ExportMarkdown`: the stage that needs a
harness is the stage that should build it, and the stage that needs this one is
whichever first wants to *disagree* with `renderToStaticHTML` deliberately.
`tools/diff/` would need a script that runs it over the same `--inputs` file,
which is the whole of the work. `cargo xtask normalize` is not that runner — it
compares two normalisers, not two engines — but it is the shape one would take.

### Owed by S5 — two things `mt-inline` is measured on now and was not before

`spec/expected-failures.json` is, from S5, **a to-do list for the inline
layer**: §6's "S5's re-baseline" assigns all 231 additions to a mechanism and
every one of them is `mt_inline`'s or §4 C2's. Two are worth naming here
because they are the largest and because nothing else in the repository would
surface them:

1. **Emphasis is 82 of 132** (CommonMark §6.2). `mt_inline` matches emphasis
   with a fixed ordered array of regexes and CommonMark specifies a
   delimiter-run algorithm; `_foo _bar_ baz_`, `_(_foo_)_` and `**foo*` are
   three shapes it cannot express. **This is a statement about what the editor
   renders**, not only about the exporter, because both go through the same
   tokenizer — which is the whole reason S5 chose it.
2. **The emoji table is not transcribed.** `emojiExtension` is unconditional in
   `getHighlightHtml` and resolves an alias against `config/emojis.ts`'s 1,812
   entries. Measured over both fixture files, **zero** examples reach a valid
   alias, and an unresolved alias renders as its own source in both engines —
   which is what makes the omission invisible to the gate and worth writing
   down. Owed to whichever milestone first renders emoji on screen.

### Owed by S6 — three things the generator found and one it made cheaper

The first is not this port's at all, and it is the one worth acting on.

1. **`pulldown-cmark` 0.13.4 panics on `"> - [a]: /x\n\t"`.**
   `Option::unwrap()` on `None` inside `OffsetIter::next` (`parse.rs:2199`),
   reached before any of this crate's code runs — the probe drives the parser
   directly. ~~Four ingredients: a block quote, a list item, a link reference
   definition, and a following line that is whitespace-only and ends in a tab.
   The three neighbours (`> [a]: /x\n\t`, `- [a]: /x\n\t`, `> - x\n\t`) all parse
   normally, so it is those four together.~~

   **Corrected at S7 — the shape as stated is too narrow, in two directions at
   once.** The block quote is **not** required, and the line need not end in a
   tab: any whitespace character CommonMark does not count as blank will do.
   `"- [a]:x\n\u{b}"` panics — a list item, a reference definition, and one
   vertical tab. **Every row of the table above and the `#[should_panic]` test
   stay true**; this widens the class rather than contradicting it, which is the
   distinction that matters for the three options below, because a pre-scan
   written against the four-ingredient reading would have missed most of the
   inputs that reach it. `is_the_known_upstream_panic` in
   `crates/mt-md/tests/round_trip_properties.rs` is the widened predicate, it
   skips **89 of 1,600** generated documents, and
   `the_upstream_panic_guard_covers_the_recorded_shape_and_the_wider_class` pins
   both readings so that neither can be quietly narrowed again.
   `docs/upstream-issues.md` carries the same correction.

   **`mt_md::parse`'s doc comment says it is total and that is now false**, for
   this one input class. 0.13.4 is the latest published version, so there is no
   upgrade to take, and catching it is not open either: the release profile is
   `panic = "abort"`. What is owed is the decision — file it upstream, pin a
   patched fork, or pre-scan for the shape — and it is owed to whichever stage
   first has a user who can type it, which is M4. `docs/upstream-issues.md`
   records it; `block::tests::pulldown_cmark_panics_on_a_definition_in_a_quoted_list_item_before_a_tab_line`
   is `#[should_panic]`, so the day the upstream fix lands the test fails and
   says so.

2. **A child's range can escape its parent's**, on `-\t- [a]: /x[xter\n`. A tab
   inside a list item's marker padding is the one case where a stripped line's
   text is not a slice of the source (`StrippedLine::exact`), so the scanner's
   range is computed in stripped space and lands one byte to the left of its
   item. `tests/source_ranges.rs` asserts nesting **over the corpus**, which
   contains no such document; the invariant is therefore about the corpus rather
   than about every string, and
   `block::tests::the_ranges_are_not_injective_and_do_not_always_nest` is the
   input that says so. Not repaired here because `strip_lines`' clip is 30 of
   S2's 47 first-run disagreements and no fixture asks for it to move.

3. **`seen_labels` forces a whole-document region whenever a definition-shaped
   line appears at or before the region's end, in *either* source.** The precise
   version is per-block label contributions carried beside the tree, so that a
   region can be told which labels were live at its start; it is owed to whichever
   stage first profiles a keystroke in a document full of link definitions.
   `mt_md::reparse`'s module docs carry the argument for the conservative form.

**And the one it made cheaper:** §10's per-kind reverse offset map, below, is
still owed — but S6 found it needs **none** of it. The leaf-text fast path is
restricted to `Paragraph`, which is the one leaf kind whose text *is* the
container-stripped lines, and every other kind takes the region path instead.
`block::paragraph_text` is that half, `block::prefix_chain` is the container half
S2 argued was free and S3 pinned with a test, and S6 is their first caller
outside a test. So "S6 will have it" — written twice and never checked — is
checked: it did not need it, and M4 still does.

### Owed by S7 — ~~three serializer findings~~ ~~five~~ **six, plus five unproven slice sites**, and the runner is now owed by four stages

**Extended at round 2, and again at round 3.** Findings 1–3 are round 1's,
findings 4 and 5 came out of the first soak, **finding 6 came out of the second**,
and the five slice sites at the end are the audit's. Finding 6 is the one that
breaks the family's shape: 1–5 converge and 6 does not. The five earlier findings
came out of generated or fuzzed markdown, all five are pinned by a named test
with a reproducer, and all five are deferred for the **same** reason: confirming
a repair means comparing against `ExportMarkdown`, and no harness in this
repository does. That runner has now been declined four times on one rule — *the
stage that needs a harness is the stage that should build it* — S1 for
`parse_blocks`, S4 for `ExportMarkdown`, S5 for `renderToStaticHTML`, and S7
here. Two sections above ("Owed forward — a serializer differential" and "Owed by
S5 — an HTML differential") say what it would take:
`tools/diff/dump-ts-state.mjs` needs a sibling that runs `ExportMarkdown` over
the same `--inputs` file, which is the whole of the work.

1. **The round trip oscillates with period 2, and muya does not.**
   `"> - [a]: /x\n>   * "` alternates between the loose rendering
   (`"> - [a]: /x\n>\n>   *\n"`) and the tight one (`"> - [a]: /x\n>   * \n"`)
   forever; asked directly, muya produces the tight one on its **first** pass and
   stays there. So the port reaches muya's fixed point on every even pass and
   leaves it again on every odd one. **This is a port defect and the only one of
   the three that is**, and it is the sharpest form of the harm §11.3's row
   exists to catch: a user saving that document twice gets two different files,
   and a third time gets the first back. Not repaired here because looseness is
   decided by `_insertLineBreak` and by what `loose` is read back from — the
   mechanism the nine `listSerialization` cases turned on — and it is settled in
   this repository by `cargo xtask blocks --require-ts` over 1344 inputs against
   the running engine, which is the harness a property-test stage does not run.
   Changing looseness on a guess is how 1344/1344 stops being 1344/1344. Pinned
   by `the_round_trip_oscillates_between_a_tight_and_a_loose_rendering`. **Owed
   to whichever stage next touches the serializer's looseness**, which is the
   same stage §10 has owed the runner to since S4.

2. **A fence with a backtick in its info string has no constant settling time.**
   n fences of `` ~~~a` `` followed by `` ~~~` ~ `` settle after exactly **n + 2**
   applications, so no `SETTLES_BY` can cover the family and excluding it by name
   is the only true statement about it. Driven against the reference engine, muya
   is **byte-identical for the first three passes** and then **keeps growing
   where the port stops**. That last clause is a serializer divergence, it is
   `FIXED_POINT_EXCEPTIONS`' mechanism 2 seen from the other side, and S7 reports
   it rather than deciding it. Pinned by
   `the_settling_time_grows_with_the_number_of_backtick_info_strings`.

3. **Serialized output diverges from muya on two generated documents** —
   `"1. a\n--\n0\n\t- a"` and `">a\n \t- a\nd"`. Small, and the reason to write
   them down is not their size: **§8's row *"a serializer divergence is chosen
   deliberately and cannot be registered"* has had no evidence since S4**, when
   the hand-taken measurement came back 1346 of 1346 byte-identical. These two
   are the failing differential case register rule 3 requires and has nowhere to
   fire. They are owed to the runner, and the runner is owed to the stage that
   first wants to disagree with `ExportMarkdown` on purpose — which, with two
   documents where it already does by accident, is closer than it was.

4. **Content indented past a list item's column sheds 4 columns per round trip,
   and no constant bound exists.** Added at round 2, from soak shard 1.
   ````format!("- a\n{}```o\n", " ".repeat(4 * n))```` settles after exactly `n`
   applications, 6 of 6 measured; the tab spelling gives the identical table, so
   the quantity is **indentation in columns** and not the whitespace character.
   The parser consumes a continuation indent that the serializer re-emits four
   columns short, so each pass peels one level: a document indented 400 columns
   needs 100 applications. **This is data loss a user watches happen** — an
   over-indented fence inside a list moves on every one of the next *n* saves —
   and it is §11.3's *"Data safety"* row rather than a convergence rate. Pinned
   by `the_settling_time_is_known_to_grow_with_a_list_items_stray_indent` (the
   exclusion) and `the_settling_time_grows_with_a_list_items_indentation` (the
   law, at both sides of the threshold).
5. **A backslash run in a fence info string halves on every round trip,
   ⌊log₂n⌋+1.** Added at round 2, from soak shard 2 — which contains **0
   backticks of 163 bytes** and is therefore not finding 2's family in a second
   spelling. `format!("~~~ a{}\nx\n", "\\".repeat(n))` settles after ⌊log₂n⌋+1
   applications, 8 of 8 measured; shard 2 carries 37 backslashes and
   ⌊log₂ 37⌋ + 1 = 6, the measured value. The parser unescapes `\\` → `\` in the
   info string and the serializer emits it without re-escaping, **so a user who
   saves a fence whose info string contains backslashes loses half of them each
   time.** The repair — make `serialize ∘ parse` the identity on an info string —
   is a serializer change, which is why it is here and not in the commit.

6. **A fourth family that does not settle at all — it grows by one line per
   application, forever.** Added at round 3, from the second soak's `proptest
   soak` on **linux and macos**, at `round_trip_properties.rs:2311`. Each
   application of `serialize ∘ parse` **appends one more copy of a line**:
   `m1` holds 2 copies and `m9` holds 10. The trigger is a **lone `\r` inside a
   fenced code block's body**.

   **This one is different in kind from 1–5 and the difference is the point.**
   Findings 2, 4 and 5 are slow — `n + 2`, linear, ⌊log₂ n⌋ + 1 — and every one
   of them reaches a fixed point. This one **diverges**. No `SETTLES_BY` covers
   it at any value, and it is not a convergence rate at all: it is §11.3's
   *"Data safety"* row in its most direct form, a document that gets one line
   longer every time it is saved.

   **What must be established first, because it decides the fix.** Whether the
   behaviour is pre-existing or was introduced by round 2's line-grid change is
   **unestablished**, and round 1's soak cannot answer it — it died at 424 s on
   the char-boundary panic, so its silence about this family is a fact about the
   run's length rather than about the engine. Bisecting the two commits over the
   reproducing input is a bounded piece of work and it is the first thing to do:
   a pre-existing defect is the serializer's fence path, and a new one is the
   terminator asymmetry, and those are different repairs in different files.

**And the five slice sites, added at round 2 and owed as *unproven* rather than
as broken.** The crate-wide audit produced 17 rows; **ten** are fixed with tests
and **two** are covered by argument rather than by type. The five left are
`block.rs` **2359** (`trim_trailing_blanks`),
**2277** (`task_checkbox`), **2486** (`marker_char`), **2938** (`raw_info`) and
**988** (`Builder::walk`'s region). Each slices a range **and uses the text**, so
the boundary snap that fixed the other ten would not preserve an answer here —
it would return a *different* one, quietly, which is the failure `block.rs:3361`
demonstrated one line from a crash. None fired in the 3,000,000-run sweep, and
"did not fire" is the whole of what is known about them. What is owed is the
type-level fix the triage's own verdict points at — a range whose provenance
carries its boundary guarantee, so that `Built::range` cannot be handed to a
slice without one — and it is owed to whichever stage first wants to stop
answering this question site by site.

**And a number that is owed *down*, not forward.** `SETTLES_BY = 8` is a **bound
on a defect**, not a property of a correct serializer: findings 4 and 5 are why
it is not 4, and 4 was itself the observed maximum of a generator that reached
neither family. When they are repaired, the constant should come down, its
derived threshold `4 * (SETTLES_BY - 2)` with it, and
`the_settling_time_is_known_to_grow_with_a_list_items_stray_indent` should be
**deleted rather than widened**. If it does not come down,
the residue is a third family and not a number to raise — which is exactly what
round 2 found when it raised it from 4.

**And an assertion that is owed *back*, which is round 3's whole content.**
Round 2 raised the constant to 8 and the very next soak falsified that too,
from a **305-byte** input — ` ```o ` and 300 backslashes, needing
⌊log₂ 300⌋ + 1 = 9. The property test had *predicted this in its own margin*
(*"a fence info string with 256 backslashes is where 8 stops being true — that
is a **finding, not a guard**"*) one round before the fuzzer produced it.

Raising it a third time was refused, and the reason is structural. Three
families' settling times grow with the size of the input — `n + 2` in the fence
count, linear in a list item's stray indentation, ⌊log₂ n⌋ + 1 in a backslash
run — so against a coverage-guided fuzzer over `-max_len=65536` **any constant
is a statement about `max_len` rather than about the engine**, and the
exclusion list would grow every run into an enumeration of engine defects the
property had been made blind to. `fuzz/fuzz_targets/round_trip.rs` therefore
**stopped asserting settling entirely** and now asserts totality under
repetition — §11.3's actual clause — while the settling claim lives in the
property test alone, where the generators emit shapes this repository
enumerates.

The asymmetry is deliberate and inverts this document's usual rule that two
harnesses should carry one claim: **a claim that is true of a generator and
false of a fuzzer is two claims**, and writing it once in each place is the
hole rather than the cover. What is owed is the day it goes back: when findings
4 and 5 are repaired the bound becomes real and small, and the byte fuzzer
should assert it again. That day is the same day the constant comes down, and
the two are one item rather than two.

**And one thing S7 does *not* owe forward, recorded because the omission would
read as an oversight.** `MAX_NESTING_DEPTH = 128` is not provisional and is not
awaiting a muya number to replace it: muya's own depth is V8's `--stack-size` in
a straight line (938 at 500, 9550 at 4000), so there is nothing to converge on.
What a later stage may want is a *different* number — a limit is a product
decision the moment a user can hit it — and the argument for changing it lives at
`Builder::would_exceed_depth` beside the two alternatives it was chosen against,
rather than here.

### Owed by S7 — the reparse divergences are a **class**, and M3/M4 must not build on `mt_md::reparse` without re-establishing it

**This is the most important item in §10, and it is the reason M2 closed on a red
soak rather than grinding another round.** Written as a class rather than as a
list of incidents, because that is what the evidence supports — and written as a
**judgement**, because five incidents is not a proof of one.

**The incidents, in the order they were found.** Every one is `Incremental`
answering something a full parse of its own source does not:

| # | Mechanism | Found by | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `leaf_text_edit` lacked the `defines_a_label` guard `span_for` already had — a mid-line edit dissolving a two-line reference definition splits a paragraph the full parse keeps whole | first soak, `fuzz reparse` | **fixed at round 2** |
| 2 | `preceded_by_blank_line` answering a question about a line that does not exist, so a hard boundary is declared where the full parse merges two paragraphs | first soak, `fuzz reparse` | **fixed at round 2**, by the line-grid asymmetry |
| 3 | A one-space insert **between** a `\r` and its `\n` splits one line ending into two, so the grid moves under the whole document — the `\r` whose meaning changes is *beside* the edit, not in it | the repaired generator, round 2 | **fixed at round 2** |
| 4 | A region containing a lone `\r` is not reasonable in isolation: `parse("    indented\n    code\n\n\r#")` drops the trailing heading, the same region alone keeps it | the repaired generator, round 2 | **fixed at round 2** |
| 5 | **Finding 7** — `SPEC: ""` with edit `[4] 11..37 → ""`, `fuzz/fuzz_targets/reparse.rs:194`, *"the trees disagree"* | **second soak**, `fuzz reparse`, **both shards** | **open, uncharacterised** |

Five distinct mechanisms — three from two soak dispatches, two from the generator
repair between them — four repaired and the fifth found by the same target on its
next run. **A list would be closed by fixing its last
member; a class is not, and nothing here says which of the two this is** — which
is precisely why the answer is a campaign rather than a sixth repair.

**What would establish its extent, concretely.** A dedicated reparse fuzzing
campaign, and **the target already exists**:
[`fuzz/fuzz_targets/reparse.rs`](../fuzz/fuzz_targets/reparse.rs), seeded at
**712** document-shaped inputs, sharded 2 × 4 h in `soak.yml`. What does not
exist is the method around it:

1. **Delta-debug every disagreement to a minimal edit session before repairing
   any of them.** Round 2 repaired the first two as they were found, which is
   why nobody can say today whether mechanisms 1–4 are four members of one class
   or four unrelated bugs.
2. **Attribute each one to a `ReparsePath`** — `LeafText` or `Region` — and to
   the guard that should have declined it. Four of the five are a *guard* that
   was absent or that answered a question about the wrong string; if the fifth
   is too, the class has a shape and the shape is the fix.
3. **Run it until it stops finding new mechanisms**, and record the length at
   which it stopped. That number is the evidence this milestone does not have:
   every dispatch so far has found something within hours, so no run has yet
   produced a *bound* rather than a defect.

**And the consequence for the next two milestones, stated plainly. M3/M4 must
not build on `mt_md::reparse` without re-establishing it first.** M4 is the
editor, and the incremental path is the one an editor uses on every keystroke —
`Incremental::document()` *is* what the UI renders and what `serialize` writes to
disk. A disagreement there is not transient bookkeeping that a later parse
repairs: the wrong tree persists until some later edit happens to trigger a
whole-document region, every intervening edit is computed against it, and saving
in that state **writes the wrong file**. The leaf source ranges disagree too, so
caret mapping is wrong for every byte after the split.

Two ways to satisfy this, and either is acceptable: run the campaign above and
close the class, or have M3/M4 drive full parses until it is closed and treat
`mt_md::reparse` as unproven optimisation. What is **not** acceptable is reading
S6's *"3.2 million generated edit sequences"* as the guarantee it is not — that
denominator had a hole cut in it (§8), and the byte fuzzer has disagreed with a
full parse on every dispatch since.

One mitigating fact, stated precisely rather than as reassurance: `normalize_source`
— `mt-cli`'s `read_markdown`, and where `mt-fs` will live — folds a lone `\r`
away, so **a document opened through the file layer cannot contain one**.
Mechanisms 2, 3 and 4 need one. That limits their exposure to callers who hand
`Incremental` a raw string: a fuzz target, and **a clipboard paste** — which M4
has.

### Owed by S7 — a stack overflow behind the clamp, reproducible but leaving no artifact

**Finding 8, and the honest form of it is short.** A stack overflow was observed
in a 420-second local `round_trip` run under ASan on Windows, and **reproduced in
a second run of the same length**. No crashing input exists in `fuzz/artifacts/`
after either.

**The first explanation was wrong.** The first run's stdout had been piped
through `head`, and the missing artifact was attributed to that pipeline
SIGPIPE'ing cargo-fuzz. The second run used no pipe and still wrote nothing. The
actual reason is how the crash ends — `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` (`0xc0000005`)
with ASan reporting `<empty stack>`, so **libFuzzer dies before it writes the
unit** and cannot symbolise the frame either. It is recorded as **reproducible
but uncharacterised**, which is a different and better state than the one this
section first claimed.

**It is not the unbounded recursion round 1 fixed.** That one is clamped at
`MAX_NESTING_DEPTH = 128`, all six of the crate's recursive walks were audited
rather than the one that crashed, and `"> " × 2000` — the 4,001-byte file that
overflowed a plain `--release` build — parses in 50 ms today. Anything behind the
clamp is a different mechanism.

**It may be an environment artifact rather than a product defect**, and the two
measurements that say so are worth carrying because they also say how to check:
ASan inflates stack frames substantially, and `html.rs`'s walk is the crate's
**deepest-framed** — **995 levels on a 1 MiB thread in release** and **369 in
`dev`**, against `parse`'s 1,229 — which is why it is the walk that fixes the
limit. A `dev`-plus-sanitizer build spends that headroom fastest, and 369 is the
number to measure against rather than 128.

**What would re-find it**, in the order that costs least:

1. **Re-run the same target under ASan with stdout redirected to a file rather
   than piped**, never through a truncating filter, with `fuzz/artifacts/`
   preserved and uploaded. That is the whole of what was missing.
2. **Ladder the depth under ASan without any fuzzer** — `"> " × n` for n up to
   the clamp, one process per depth, exactly as the muya measurement was taken —
   and record the frame budget ASan actually leaves. If 128 clamped levels of
   the deepest walk exceed the fuzz build's thread stack, the answer is an
   environment fact, the clamp is not implicated, and this item closes as
   *not a defect* rather than staying open on one line of terminal output.
3. Only then, if both come back clean, is *"observed once, unreproduced"* the
   final state — and it should be written down as that rather than dropped.

### Owed to M5 — HTML → Markdown on paste

§4's fourth row (`html5ever` + a port of turndown's rules; `htmd` evaluated
first) is out of M2's scope (§1) and belongs with the clipboard. Nothing in
`spec/` covers it and nothing in M2's gate touches it. `htmlToMarkdown.ts` is 88
lines and its evaluation question — `htmd` or a hand port — is a real dependency
decision that should be taken with the paste path in front of it, not now.

### Owed to M4 — the editor halves of two transcribed spec files

Added at S0, in M1's `autoLinkEncoding` shape: the case is transcribed, the
half that M2 can express is asserted, and the other half is recorded here
rather than left in a comment nobody reads.

1. **`listMarkerOption.spec.ts` (6 cases).** The transcribed half is that
   `serialize` emits `meta.marker` / `meta.delimiter` verbatim. The half owed
   is that `muya.updateParagraph('ul-bullet')` creates a list whose `meta`
   carries the boot option — `replaceBlockByLabel`, and the rAF state flush.
2. **`gitlabMathToggle.spec.ts` (4 cases).** The transcribed half is that the
   same source under the two option values yields two different block kinds, in
   both directions — which is *why* the toggle must re-parse. The half owed is
   that `editor.setOptions({ … }, true)` re-parses rather than re-rendering.

### Owed to M6 — two KaTeX cases, added at S5

`renderToStaticHTML.spec.ts`'s `honours_the_math_option` and
`honours_gitlab_compatibility_for_math_fences` assert that the output contains
`katex` or `<math`. `getHighlightHtml` builds its math extension with
`useKatexRender: true`; there is no TeX renderer in this workspace, and §9
schedules `mt-math` at **M6** (its crate doc says so, and §13 R3 says its
*evaluation* was due at M1).

The port emits muya's **other** math renderer — the one `lexBlock` uses,
`<pre class="multiple-math" data-math-style="…">` — which is a real muya shape
and not the one these two cases assert. `Options::SPEC` has `math: false`, so
they cost the conformance number nothing.

They belong beside the thirteen below: same table, different reason. §7's stage
column marks their file S5 and only a run says when a case passes, which is the
fourth time that lesson has had to be written down and the first time it has
crossed two milestones.

### Owed to M6 — thirteen `MarkdownToHtml` cases

`parityExportHtml` (6), `exportHtmlDir` (3), `footnoteExportHtml` (2) and
`mermaidExportResilience` (2) test the **standalone-document** export: inlined
stylesheets, `<html lang dir>`, heading slug ids, the TOC stylesheet, and
per-diagram error containment in the async mermaid pass. §2's port table does
not list `markdownToHtml.ts` and §4 C3 is explicit that `renderToStaticHTML` is
what M2 ports — `renderToStaticHTML.spec.ts` itself asserts the export
behaviours are *absent* from that function. They belong with `mt-export` at M6,
and they are owed rather than dropped. Three more (`blockSerialization`'s
live-`CodeBlock` cases) are M4's and one (`sequenceTheme`) is M3's; the full
table is in §6's S0 correction.

### Owed to M3/M4 — carried from M1 §10, unchanged

1. `autoLinkEncoding.spec.ts`'s renderer half (marktext #3548). The tokenizer
   half passes and `PENDING` is empty, which is precisely the state in which an
   owed check becomes a forgotten one.
2. The two renderer behaviours above D8's predicate: `header.ts`'s synthetic
   marker range and `htmlTag.ts`'s childless-tag override.

### Discharged in M2 — `normalizeHtml` **— done at S5**

M0 decision 12 and M1 §10 both owed this to M2, and it was **S5's**, not a
standing item: when `render_to_static_html` returns `Ok`, feed the same HTML
through `xtask/src/html.rs`'s `normalize_html` and through `runner.ts`'s, and
assert agreement over every rendered fixture. Note what it is *not*: the two
inherited limitations (an attribute value containing `>` breaks tag scanning;
the attribute pass does not lower-case tag names) are deliberate, because
fixing them would make the two runners disagree. The check is that the Rust one
reproduces the TypeScript one, bugs included — not that either is right.

**`cargo xtask normalize --require-ts`, 2,648 of 2,648 on the first run.** The
denominator is twice the fixture count on purpose: the port's 1,324 rendered
fixtures **and** the 1,324 expected ones, because the conformance comparison
normalises both sides and cmark's HTML is the half that contains constructs the
port never emits. Neither limitation was touched.

It is a separate `xtask` step rather than part of `conformance`, because
`conformance` needs neither Node nor the marktext clone and that is
load-bearing — it is the one harness that runs on a bare CI runner with the
committed fixtures and nothing else. This one needs Node and `tsx`, though
notably **not** the clone: `spec/runner.ts` lives in this repository, which
makes it the first `xtask` runner whose TypeScript half is ours.
